**Efficiency (T, E).** Quick tier 174 s (stage-3 GPU part 77 s). Full tier 1 562 s: GPU stages 1-2 about 505 s, stage 3 about 735 s, of which the S2 series about 410 s (dense body blocks at 67.5 and 101.25 per lambda0); the wall time is set by the CPU pool (Capytaine references) running in parallel. Largest solve N = 113 308 (S2 Fn 0.4 f/b 4, 101.25 per lambda0): 25 GMRES iterations; device memory 5.4 GB at N = 89 842 (B3-E9), host working set about 1.8 GB. No MPC or closed-loop run (T3).

### 8.5 Findings, failures and open items of B3

1. **Open item (a) of stage 3 -- downstream amplification: resolved.** Cause established by the coupled analysis
   (8.2): Dawson-type rows are multistep methods of order > 2, unstable at the grid's short diverging waves (Dahlquist
   barrier). The shifted Numerov rows are neutral at every wavenumber; S3b 1.6 % (was 23 %), window ratios within
   0.1 % of 1 over 4 wavelengths, trend +0.03 % per lambda0.
2. **Open item (b) -- dense size limit: resolved.** FFT products and the y-circulant preconditioner solve N = 113 308
   (S2, 101.25 per lambda0) in 25 GMRES iterations; the cost is now the dense body blocks (2 N_F N_B entries, 50-75 s
   at N_F ~ 1e5 with an 80x32 body), not the free surface. Iteration counts do not grow with N.
3. **Open item (c) -- Fn 0.4, f/b 4 spheroid vs Havelock: still FAILS the registered 5 %, +5.9 % (near) / +6.2 %
   (far) at the registered 80x32 body; body-mesh-extrapolated +6.4 % near / +6.5 % far (S2m, 8.9; far field order
   2.00, 1.0646). Rewritten after the B3 review.** The row is information since the review (Havelock's formula is an
   approximation without the body/free-surface interaction, so the comparison is neither verification nor validation),
   and its registered 5 % and FAIL stay in the record. The first version of this item concluded from "two operators
   and two functionals agree" that the +6 % is the interaction and not a numerical error; that did not follow, because
   the two Rankine operators share the body blocks, the rigid-lid representation, the domain, entry zone and beach and
   the body mesh. **The evidence is S2K (8.8, 8.9):** an independent body-only solve with the exact Kelvin Green
   function (no free-surface panels, no domain) on the same body mesh agrees with the Rankine solution to 0.04-0.12 %
   (near) and 0.005-0.009 % (far) at 80x32 and 120x48, for Fn 0.4 and 0.5. So the deviation from Havelock is the
   interaction (plus the body-mesh error, now quantified by S2m), not a free-surface discretisation error.
4. **S4 (registered 1 % at Fn 0.05) and S8 (raw double-body force) fail as before**: S4 measures the physical O(Fn^2)
   correction (now 1.44 % at Fn 0.05, 0.36 % at 0.025, order 2); its re-registered companion S4b passes with +0.16 %
   (stage 1: +3.0 %). S8 concerns only the body pressure integration and is unchanged by B3. [B3 review: S4 and this
   S4b run on the fixed Fn 0.5 grid where the resolution guard is active (l = 0.02-0.39), so they test the guarded
   operator; with the split forcing made consistent with the blend S4b is -0.42 % (the guarded rows' own second-order
   truncation, B3R-E4), and the production rows, on grids per lambda0, agree with the split to 6e-7 (8.9).]
5. **Entry-zone instability (found by exploration, B3-E5) and the blend.** Numerov rows are not stable where the
   coupling is weakened (entry ramp) or the grid is too coarse; the blend toward the unconditionally stable backward row
   (l >= 0.4) fixes both with a proof (8.2) and leaves the interior operator untouched. The domain series (S6) shows
   <= 0.10 % per extent.
6. **g -> 0 limit (M4), deferred in stage 1, now done (S9):** 2.3 % at Fn 8 (tol 5 %), approach of order 2 in 1/Fn,
   Fx -> 0. It needs a body-scale grid, because the registered lambda0-based domain grows with Fn^2.
7. **GMRES round-off floor (B3-E11).** At N >~ 5e4 the true residual of the FFT products stalls at 1e-12 ... 2.6e-12,
   above the 1e-12 written in 8.1 for the production solves; the stagnation rule stops there. The effect on R is far
   below every tolerance: F3 shows dense and FFT solutions agreeing to <= 2e-10 (R to <= 7e-11) at a residual of
   4e-13, so a residual 6 times larger moves R by about 1e-9. [B3 review: the first stall rule stopped at any residual
   level and no row checked the residual, so a slowly converging solve could have been used silently. Now a stall is
   accepted only at or below the registered floor 1e-10, `SteadyShiftedFS.solve` raises above it, and every stage-3
   row prints its residuals; largest in the re-run tiers 2.6e-12 (8.9).]
8. **Transverse-wavelength row (S3):** -0.32 % with the new operator vs -0.12 % with upwind5, although the coupled
   wavenumber error is now -0.012 %. The row estimates the wavelength from six centre-row zero crossings between half a
   wavelength behind the stern and the beach, where the diverging waves and the local field still move the crossings;
   it is an estimator bias of the same kind as the Kelvin-angle estimator's (section 5 item 9), well inside 2 %.
   [B3 review: the bias was asserted here, not shown. Now shown: the same estimator on the exact linear Kelvin
   elevation on the same window gives -0.360 % (solver -0.321 %), so the solver differs from the exact field by
   +0.038 % in this row; the estimator alone moves by -0.46 ... +0.72 % with the window start (reviewer), so the row
   cannot resolve errors below about 1 %. Its reference is now the estimator on the exact field (8.8); D1a and S3b
   remain the binding wavelength checks.]
9. **Remaining small S1 discrepancies (0.03-0.12 %)** are at the size of the domain-truncation changes of S6 (<= 0.10 %),
   not of the operator (whose wavenumber error gives 2 k0 f x 0.01 % ~ 0.05 % at most).
10. **Reproducibility (N2):** the stage-3 quick rows of two separate processes are bitwise identical (26 rows, max
    relative difference 0); stage 1-2 printed values are identical to the post-review run (32 of 32 quick rows).
11. **Records:** the post-review full record is kept as `review_verify_full.json/.log`, the post-review quick log as
    `review_verify_quick.log` (its json was overwritten by a stage-3-only debug run, kept as
    `b3_debug_quick_stage3.json`; partial runs now write their own file names). The B3 records before the B3 review
    are kept as `b3_prereview_verify_full.json/.log` and `b3_prereview_verify_quick.json/.log`.
12. **Pass counts (B3 review).** The counts 85/88 and 56/58 included the information rows (15 and 6), which pass by
    construction; the binding counts were 70/73 and 50/52. Every tier now prints binding and information rows
    separately (8.9: 83/86 and 58/60).
13. **Labels (B3 review).** S1 is verification (M2/M5: Havelock solves the same linear equations exactly), S2 is
    information (interaction size), Z1 is verification against Hulme's semi-analytic solution; nothing in Track B is
    validation against measurement yet. The first validation is the Wigley vs Kajitani 1983 step.
14. **Choices made with values known (B3 review).** S9's evaluation point Fn 8 (after B3-E10), the D1a 55 deg cut-off
    (after `rankine_d1.py`) and the S3b members n = 45, 60 (after B3-E7) are flagged in 8.1. The robust, Fn-independent
    g -> 0 criterion S9r (Richardson in 1/Fn: limit 1.00053, order 1.96) was added; its values were known (reviewer).
15. **Near-field functional for deep bodies (B3 review, B3R-E2).** For k0 f >~ 3 the near-field resistance carries an
    absolute free-surface-discretisation error that does not fall with R (+0.38 % at f/b 6, +4.9 % at f/b 8, Fn 0.4,
    80x32, against the Kelvin solve, while the far fields agree to 1e-5): use the far field or the exact-Kelvin check
    there (rule in 8.9).
16. **Resolution guard (B3 review).** Reported per solve and warned outside S4; the limits in the first text ("about
    12-13 panels per wavelength") were wrong by a factor of about 4 (z_max is proportional to 1/n: the guard starts below
    3.4 panels per lambda0); production grids are chosen per lambda0 (8.2).

### 8.7 Next step and estimate (W4)

1. **Wigley hull (design step S2)** with the shifted Numerov operator: a surface-piercing hull needs (i) the
   free-surface lattice with the waterplane masked out (the FFT convolution stays on the full rectangle with sigma = 0
   inside the hole; the rows inside are dropped), (ii) the row count at the hull: the hull's bow acts as a new upstream
   end for the grid rows that it cuts and the stern as a downstream end, so the rows next to the hull need the same
   kind of derivation as the entry rows of 8.2 (two conditions upstream of each cut, none downstream), (iii) a
   waterline term in the far field (wave cut instead of the body Kochin function). Data: WaveBEM's shipped Wigley files
   (Kajitani et al. 1983, `ext_sims/WaveBEM/experimental-results/wigley/`). Estimate 4-6 h agent wall-clock; bottleneck:
   the rows next to the hull and their stability analysis (the same tools as `rankine_d1.py`), not compute (each solve
   is seconds).
2. **Body blocks** dominate the large solves (50-75 s at N_F ~ 1e5 with an 80x32 body): a precomputed FFT of the body's field on the
   lattice is not possible (the body is off-lattice), but the S_BF / D_FB products can be computed by a far-field
   expansion beyond a few body lengths; worth doing only when tables need many speeds at N ~ 1e5 (not yet).
3. The time-domain / forward-speed radiation operator (design R2) needs its own stability analysis (the unsteady
   free-surface condition is first order in time); the shifted structure and the coupled-symbol tools carry over.

### 8.8 Review of B3 (2026-10-06): fixes and registration of the changed and new rows (written before the re-run; V1)

The B3 review raised two major and eight minor issues. Every row that the fixes change or add is registered here
**before** the tiers are re-run. Registration record (minor issue 10): `python studies/rankine_verify.py --register
"<note>"` appends to `studies/_cache/rankine/registration_log.json` the UTC time and the sha256 of sections 1.1, 8.1
and 8.8 of this note, of the threshold table built from the code (check id, tolerance argument and pass expression of
every `_row(...)` call, read with `ast`) and of the tier job table. Every tier run prints these hashes with its start
time and stores them in its json together with the log time of the matching registration, so the order "registered,
then run" can be checked from the records alone. Sections 1.1 and 8.1 were written before this mechanism existed; their
order was confirmed by the reviewer from the session transcript (8.1 written 12:52:07 UTC, first tier 12:55:25 UTC,
thresholds unchanged since), and their first log entry is the one written for this registration (8.1 now carries the
label edits listed at its end). The column "values known" says whether the registered quantity had already been
computed (by the reviewer or by an exploration or smoke run below) when its threshold was written.

### 8.9 Results after the B3 review fixes (`verify_full.json/.log`, `verify_quick.json/.log`; run after the registration of 8.8)

**Registration record.** `registration_log.json` entry 2026-10-06T15:48:50 UTC (sections 1.1, 8.1, 8.8, code threshold
table, tier jobs); quick tier started 15:48:59 UTC, full tier 15:55:32 UTC; both runs print and store the same five
hashes, each matched to that entry. No threshold or tier job changed after the registration (the only later edits are
in this section, 8.4 annotations, 8.5 and the summaries, none of them registration sections).

**Tier summary (binding = rows with a registered threshold; information rows pass by construction and are not
counted).** Full tier **3 496 s, binding 83/86**, information rows 18 (all rows 101/104). Quick tier **328 s, binding
58/60**, information rows 6 (64/66). Failures, all as registered: S2 Fn 0.4 f/b 4 (information row with the kept 5 %),
S4 (mis-specified, 1 % at Fn 0.05) and S8 (raw double-body force) -- full; S4 and S8 -- quick (the quick tier has no
Fn 0.4 S2 case). Wall time: the full tier stays inside T1 (1 h) by 104 s only: the physics track's
`anyspeed_p1b_check` was running on the same machine (seen at the start of the run; rows 1.3-2.2x slower than in the B3 run:
GPU part 656 s vs 505 s; S1 130 s vs 59 s), and the review rows add about 1 800 s of GPU time under that load (part of it the load itself) (S2 with
the S2m body meshes 1 481 s vs 409 s, S2K 528 s of which the 120x48 Kelvin solve 280 s, S4 with the production members
264 s vs 8 s). Bottleneck: the dense body blocks of the 160x64 S2m members and the 120x48 Kelvin solve; if the machine
stays shared, the 160x64 members can move to an "extended" tier (a change that would be registered first).

**Non-regression and reproducibility (N1, N2).** Against the B3 records before the review (`b3_prereview_verify_*`):
every row common to both is bitwise identical (85 full, 55 quick) except C6 (one unit in the last place, 2e-16, CPU
pool under a different load), the S3 wavelength row (quantity changed, below) and the quick S9 row (Fn 16 added; Fn 4
and 8 identical). The quick-tier stage-3 rows that the full tier also computes are identical in both records.

**GMRES and guard (major issue 2, minor issue 7).** 71 residual values printed in the full tier, 17 in the quick tier
(rows with several solves print the maximum): largest true residual 2.6e-12 (S1 Fn 0.5 f 4 at 101.25 per lambda0, N = 76 014),
all far below the floor 1e-10; no solve raised. No guard warning in either log; every production solve reports
`guard_l` = 0; S4 (fixed grid) reports l = 0.021 / 0.305 / 0.376 / 0.394 at Fn 0.2 / 0.1 / 0.05 / 0.025.

**S2K: Rankine NK vs the exact-Kelvin body-only BIE on the same body mesh (major issue 1; tolerance near 0.5 %, far
0.1 %).**

| Fn, f/b, body | Kelvin BIE R/R_H near / far | Rankine n = 30: near, far vs Kelvin | Rankine n = 101.25: near, far vs Kelvin |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0.4, 4, 80x32 | 1.06006 / 1.06158 | -0.064 %, -0.009 % | -0.122 %, -0.008 % |
| 0.5, 4, 80x32 | 1.02640 / 1.02750 | -0.064 %, -0.006 % | -0.047 %, -0.005 % |
| 0.4, 2.5, 80x32 | 1.30019 / 1.30237 | -0.083 %, -0.008 % | -- |
| 0.4, 4, 120x48 | 1.06277 / 1.06328 | -0.042 %, -0.008 % | -- |

All pass, with margins of 4x (near) and more than 10x (far). The Kelvin function itself (S2K-G): E1 vs mpmath 2.3e-15; Lagally
for a single source and for the spheroid's line source vs Havelock 3e-15 ... 9e-15 (tol 1e-10); free-surface residual
3.9e-15 of the terms (tol 1e-10); 64 vs 128 theta nodes 6.8e-14 (tol 1e-8). The Kelvin BIE shares with the Rankine
solver only the body mesh and the rigid-lid Rankine panel integrals; it has no free-surface panels, no entry zone,
beach or domain. So the Rankine solution of the submerged spheroid is verified to 0.12 % (near) and 0.01 % (far) on a
fixed body mesh, and the deviation from Havelock is the body/free-surface interaction that Havelock's formula leaves
out, not a free-surface discretisation error. (The reviewer's own Kelvin solve, with table interpolation, gave 1.06006 /
1.06158 at 80x32 and 1.06277 / 1.06328 at 120x48 for Fn 0.4 f/b 4: the same to 4 digits.)

**S2m: body-mesh series at n = 30 (minor issue 4; information).**

| Case | near 80x32 / 120x48 / 160x64 | far 80x32 / 120x48 / 160x64 | order-2 extrapolation near, far | observed order near, far | combined estimate (finest free-surface grid + body-mesh correction) near, far |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Fn 0.4 f/b 4 | 1.05938 / 1.06232 / 1.06316 | 1.06149 / 1.06319 / 1.06379 | 1.06423, 1.06456 | 2.57, 2.00 | **1.0636, 1.0646** |
| Fn 0.5 f/b 4 | 1.02574 / 1.02823 / 1.02944 | 1.02743 / 1.02954 / 1.03028 | 1.03099, 1.03123 | 1.08, 2.00 | 1.0312, 1.0312 |
| Fn 0.5 f/b 6 | 1.00387 / 1.00627 / 1.00745 | 1.00637 / 1.00843 / 1.00915 | 1.00896, 1.01008 | 1.07, 2.00 | 1.0090, 1.0101 |

The far field converges at order 2.00 in the body mesh in all three cases (as in S7a), the near field at 2.6 (Fn 0.4)
and about 1.1 (Fn 0.5), so for Fn 0.5 the order-2 near value is not an extrapolation in the asymptotic sense (the
observed-order values are 1.0328 and 1.0107); the far field is the reliable body-mesh-converged functional. The
near/far gap shrinks with the body mesh (Fn 0.4 f/b 4: -0.20 %, -0.08 %, -0.06 % at 80x32, 120x48, 160x64), i.e. the
0.15-0.26 % gap reported in 8.4 was body-mesh error. **S2 Fn 0.4 f/b 4: +5.9 % near / +6.2 % far at the registered
80x32 body; body-mesh-extrapolated +6.4 % near / +6.5 % far (far field, order 2.00: 1.0646).** The registered 5 % row
stays FAIL; it is labelled information since the review.

S4b explained (exploration B3R-E4, after the quick tier, not a registered row): on the fixed grid the guarded rows have
l = 0.31 (Fn 0.1) and 0.38 (Fn 0.05) and dx = 0.785 m; replacing, in the split, phi''(c) of the l part by the discrete
second difference delta^2 phi_db/dx^2 brings implemented/split - 1 from -0.346 % / -0.417 % to -0.009 % / -0.008 %.
So the -0.42 % is the second-order truncation l (dx^2/12) phi'''' of the guarded rows' first-order part on this coarse
grid, which the consistent reference now exposes; the +0.16 % before the review was this term partly cancelled by the
O(l dx phi''') centring error of the old reference (old forcing in B3R-E4: +0.377 % / +0.163 %). With l = 0 (production
rows) implemented and split agree to 6e-7.

Unchanged rows (D1a-e, F1-F3, S1, S1b, S2 free-surface series, S3 pattern rows, S3b, S3c, S3d, S5, S6, S7, S7a, S7b,
S8, S9) have the values of 8.4, bitwise.

**Near-field functional for deep bodies (minor issue 8; exploration B3R-E2, 8.8).** At Fn 0.4 with the 80x32 body the
Rankine near field is +0.38 % (f/b 6, k0 f = 3.75) and +4.9 % (f/b 8, k0 f = 5) above the Kelvin solve's near field,
while the far fields agree to 1e-5; refining the free surface from 30 to 67.5 per lambda0 changes the f/b 8 gap only
from 4.9 % to 4.8 %. The free-surface discretisation leaves a field that is harmonic inside the body (invisible to the
far field) whose effect on the pressure integral behaves like an absolute error that does not fall with R (at f/b 4 the near
fields of the Rankine and Kelvin solves differ by 0.04-0.12 % of R, S2K), while R falls like exp(-2 k0 f sec^2 theta). **Rule for production tables: for submerged bodies with k0 f >~ 3 use the
far field (or the exact-Kelvin check) as the resistance functional; otherwise refine the free surface until near and
far agree within the S7 tolerance (3 %).** The registered S2/S7 cases (k0 f <= 2.5) are not affected.

**Validation status.** No row of Track B is a validation against measurement (V): S1, S2K, Z1 and Z2-Z8 are
verifications against exact or semi-analytic solutions or independent codes, S2 is information. The first validation
will be the Wigley hull vs Kajitani et al. 1983 (next step, 8.7).

## 9. Stage B4 (2026-10-06): surface-piercing hull (Wigley), free sinkage and trim, transom branches

Code: `hydro/rankine/hull.py` (hull forms, wetted-surface panelling at any sinkage and trim, exact hydrostatics,
transom face), `hydro/rankine/piercing.py` (free-surface grid with a body-fitted band, band rows, transom rows, solver,
forces, wave-cut far field, free equilibrium), `hydro/rankine/thinship.py` (Michell reference). Checks:
`studies/rankine_hull_verify.py` (tiers quick / full, its own registration entries `b4:*` in
`studies/_cache/rankine/registration_log.json`). Data: `data/external/wigley/` (extraction script
`studies/rankine_wigley_data.py`; registered in `data/external/SOURCES.md`, section wigley/). Empirical inputs:
`learn/meta/EMPIRICAL_REGISTER_RANKINE.md`, section B4.

### 9.1 Registration of the B4 checks (written before the tiers ran; V1)

Production configuration (all rows unless stated): linear Neumann-Kelvin free surface on z = 0 with the B3 shifted
Numerov rows; hull exact below the calm waterplane at the attitude (wall-sided above z = 0, an assumption: the
published Wigley offsets stop at z = 0); free-surface grid: columns uniform with dx = L_wl/M, M = max(ceil(L_wl/(lambda0/30)),
m_body), dy = dx, registered domain of section 1.1 (f = 0), body-fitted band of J = ceil(2 y_max/dy) lines (9.2), rows
with lateral interpolation of phi and sigma (PHI_MODE "interp") and the hull-wall virtual point of 9.2 (GHOST_MODE
"wall"); PROD = (n 30 per lambda0, m_body 90, hull mesh 90 x 18), COARSE = (30, 60, 60 x 12); GMRES with the y-circulant
+ dense-block preconditioner, true relative residual 1e-12, floor 1e-10 (8.8). Resistance R_near = R_p - (rho g/2) oint
zeta^2 n_x dl (9.2) with R_p the d'Alembert-corrected pressure resistance and zeta the hull-side linear elevation; far
field R_cut: transverse wave cut 1 lambda0 behind the stern, lateral wavenumbers <= 0.5 pi/dy. C_W = R/(0.5 rho U^2 S),
S the wetted surface at rest (S/L^2 = 0.14941, computed) [B4 review: the code computes S/L^2 = 0.148791
(`wigley_wetted_surface`, confirmed by an independent dblquad); 0.14941 is WaveBEM's Sw/L^2 for its model; no rerun
needed, 9.7.9]. Free model: CG at (LCB at rest, design waterline) (WaveBEM's
assumption W-38; z_G only enters through moments of horizontal forces), tow force through the CG.

job (T1-T5) runs on the COARSE grid instead of PROD. Reason: the quick tier measured about 75 s per transom solve at
COARSE (the band continues behind the transom to the domain end: N_F = 56 529, 3 476 dense panels at Fn 0.45); at PROD
the dense blocks would need about 7.5 GB for S_xD alone and the 13 transom solves about 50 min, beyond the T1 budget.
The preconditioner's dense-block product was also made sparse (same matrix to round-off; performance only).

No measured data exist for the transom test geometry (`hull.wigley_transom`); the NPL material shipped with WaveBEM
needs an IGES reader and the C_T/C_R question of section 6 of the design resolved, neither available here: **no
transom validation in B4** (T rows are verification and information).

### 9.2 Formulation and derivations (M1)

**Hull and hydrostatics (`hull.py`).** Body frame x_b forward, z_b up, design waterline z_b = 0; attitude sinkage s
(> 0 down) and trim theta (> 0 bow up): x = x_b cos theta - z_b sin theta, z = z_b cos theta + x_b sin theta - s.
The calm waterplane is z_b = zeta_w(x_b) = (s - x_b sin theta)/cos theta, so the wetted hull is panelled exactly up to it
(stations cosine-clustered at stem and stern, rows cosine-clustered at keel and waterline; the top edges lie on z = 0,
so hull plus rigid-lid image is closed). Hydrostatics are integrals of the analytic form below the cut,
V = 2 int dx_b int_{-D}^{zeta_w} h dz_b, centroid and waterplane, by tensor Gauss-Legendre rules split at z_b = 0
(vertical sides above): exact for the polynomial Wigley form (H0). The equilibrium uses these exact hydrostatics plus
the dynamic pressure force of the panels; the hydrostatic part of the panel integral is never used (it carries the
O(h^2) polyhedron error, H0 information row).

**Why a body-fitted band.** The B3 rows march along streamwise grid lines and take both conditions of the
second-order march at a line's upstream end (8.2). A lattice line with abs(y) < B/2 is cut by the hull; its part behind
the hull would need two new upstream conditions at the aft waterline, which the Neumann-Kelvin problem does not supply
(the waves next to the hull are carried along the hull side, not along a lattice line). So the lines are kept
continuous: in the hull's x-range the first J lines are bent around the waterline, line edges Y_j(x) = y_w(x)(1 - j/J)
+ j dy (j = 0..J), line J being the lattice line J dy; columns stay uniform in x (dx = L_wl/M, the waterline ends are
column edges), so every line keeps a uniform x-spacing. Band panels are flat quadrilaterals with dense influences; the
rest is the B3 lattice (FFT). For a pointed stern the band ends at the stern; behind a transom the band lines continue
straight (constant shift) to the domain end and K new lines fill 0 <= y <= y_T (they start at the transom edge).

**Band rows.** The NK condition k phi_xx + phi_z = 0 needs phi_xx at FIXED y. Row of line point (c, j), centre c = r - 1:
the B3 Numerov row with f(c+-1; y_c), f = phi, sigma, taken at the neighbouring columns at the same y as the centre by
quadratic Lagrange interpolation across the lines of that column, centred on the nearest line. On the lattice the
weights are exactly (1) (H2: the B4 builder reproduces the B3 rows). Where the stencil reaches below the hull-adjacent
line, a virtual point behind the hull wall is used (next paragraph). Box correction: B3 form along lines; across lines
-(w_p^2/24) sigma_yy with the panel width and a non-uniform three-point sigma_yy (B3 form on the lattice).

**Hull-wall virtual point (exploration B4-E3).** With a one-sided stencil on the hull-adjacent line the interpolated
values at c+1 and c-1 have offsets of opposite sign but different stencils, the row symbol becomes complex and short
diverging waves grow along the afterbody: behind the stern of the Wigley the elevation at fixed probes did not
converge and C_near diverged under refinement (B4-E3). A virtual point at y_g = 2 y_w - y_0 (y_0 the line-0 point, y_w
the waterline) restores a centred stencil; its values follow from the wall conditions at z = 0 on a vertical side with
normal n ~ (-y_w', 1): dphi/dn = U n_x gives phi_y = -y_w'(U - phi_x); dsigma/dn = 0 (sigma = -phi_z and the wall normal
does not depend on z) gives sigma_y = y_w' sigma_x. First-order Taylor across 2d = y_0 - y_g with backward differences
along line 0 (columns i-1, i: the march is kept): f_g = (1 + a) f_0,i - a f_0,i-1 (+ 2 d y_w' U for phi), a = 2 d y_w'/dx.
**[B4 review: the sigma condition above is wrong -- the wall normal does depend on z wherever the sections curve below
the waterline; differentiating the body condition along the hull in z gives sigma_y = H_x sigma_x + H_xz (U - phi_x) +
H_zz sigma (H_z = 0), and with flare the phi condition gets - H_z sigma. Corrected derivation and the production ghost:
9.7.4; check H13 for a flared hull.]**

**Waterline term: sign from the momentum balance of the linear model.** Control volume: fluid below the plane z = 0,
outside the hull, closed by far planes. x-momentum: the boundary sum of (p n_x + rho u_x u_n) vanishes (outward
normals). Hull: u_n = 0, contribution F_x (force on the body, F = -int p n dS, n out of the body). Plane z = 0 (normal
+z): rho u_x u_z = rho(-U + phi_x) phi_z. Far planes: upstream undisturbed; downstream x = x_d with u = (-U + phi_x,
phi_y, phi_z) and Bernoulli p = rho(U phi_x - |grad phi|^2/2) - rho g z: after the U^2 and hydrostatic terms cancel
against the upstream plane, int_{P_d} [rho U phi_x + rho(phi_y^2 + phi_z^2 - phi_x^2)/2] dS. Mass conservation (hull
flux int U n_x = 0 over the hull closed by its waterplane) gives int_{P_d} phi_x = int_{z=0} phi_z, so rho U int phi_x
cancels the -rho U int phi_z of the plane term. With the free-surface condition phi_z = -k phi_xx,
int rho phi_x phi_z dA = -(rho k/2) oint phi_x^2 nu_x dl over the boundary of the free-surface region; at the waterline
nu = -n (horizontal), and k phi_x^2 = g zeta^2 (zeta = (U/g) phi_x): (rho g/2) oint_WL zeta^2 n_x dl, plus a term on the
downstream edge. Collecting:

    -F_x = R_p = (rho g/2) oint_WL zeta^2 n_x dl + R_waves,
    R_waves = int_{P_d} rho (phi_y^2 + phi_z^2 - phi_x^2)/2 dS + (rho g/2) int_{x_d} zeta^2 dy   (Havelock's far field).

So the wave resistance of the linear model is R_near = R_p - (rho g/2) oint zeta^2 n_x dl (n out of the body; at the bow
n_x > 0). The first implementation ADDED the term (the force of the wetted strip between z = 0 and zeta on the exact
hull, a physical argument that does not hold in the linear model, where fluid crosses z = 0); the thin-ship series
B4-E5 (R_p - term equal to the wave cut within 1-7 %, R_p + term not) led to the derivation above. R_p is the d'Alembert-
corrected pressure integral (section 2.5; the double-body x-force on the closed doubled hull is exactly 0). zeta in the
waterline term is the hull-side elevation (U/g) phi_x at z = 0 from the structured hull gradient (top two rows
extrapolated): the free-surface estimate (quadratic extrapolation of lines 0-2 to the wall) amplifies the lateral
short-wave content next to the hull (B4-E5: near/cut +1.3 % with the hull-side zeta, -7 % with the free-surface one, Fn
0.316, B/L 0.1). **[B4 review: this choice was made by the same comparison that H6 later checks (selection effect); the
two estimators must converge to each other -- check H11d (9.7.6).]**

**Hull gradient (B4-E1).** The least-squares surface gradient used for the spheroid is ill-conditioned on the thin
top row of a waterline-clustered hull mesh (neighbours almost on one line). The hull mesh is structured, so phi_xi,
phi_t are central differences in index space (second-order one-sided at stem, stern, keel and waterline) of phi and of
the centroids, and grad phi solves [r_xi; r_t; n] grad = [phi_xi, phi_t, dphi/dn].

**Wave-cut far field.** Downstream free waves zeta = Re int A(th) exp(-i kappa (x cos th + y sin th)) d th, kappa =
k0 sec^2 th, A even in th for a y-symmetric field. The cosine transform in y of zeta and zeta_x on x = x_cut, with
beta = k0 sec^2 th sin th (monotone), gives Re(A e^{-i psi}) and Im(A e^{-i psi}) (psi = kappa x_cut cos th):
|A|^2 = (dbeta/dth/(2 pi))^2 (zh^2 + (zh_x/(kappa cos th))^2), dbeta/dth = k0 (1 + sin^2 th)/cos^3 th. Havelock's energy
flux R = (pi rho U^2/2) int_{-pi/2}^{pi/2} |A|^2 cos^3 th d th; for a source distribution with Kochin function H the
wave part of havelock.py gives |A| = k0 sec^3 th |H|/(pi U), and the formula becomes (rho k0^2/pi) int_0^{pi/2} sec^3 |H|^2,
the expression already verified in S1/S7. The grid resolves lateral wavenumbers only to pi/dy and the sec^6 factor
amplifies aliased content, so the integral is cut at beta <= 0.5 pi/dy (B4-E2). Calibration on the exact Kelvin field
(H7, B4-E4).

**Free sinkage and trim.** Residuals r = ((Fz_hs + Fz_dyn - W)/W, My/(W L)) about the CG; Fz_hs and My_hs exact (above),
the dynamic part from a full solve at the attitude (hull re-panelled, grid rebuilt). Newton with the exact hydrostatic
Jacobian as the first matrix, then good-Broyden updates with the total residuals (B4-E6). Experimental definitions:
t = (dA - dF)/L = sin theta; the sinkage reading is the registered rule of V3.

**Transom stern (P-9, P-10).** Lines behind the transom start at the edge x_s and take the two conditions of the march
there. DRY (derived, G2): (D1) the free surface leaves the edge at its depth, zeta(x_s, y) = z_T(y); with the linear
dynamic condition g zeta = U phi_x this is also the atmospheric pressure at the edge (|grad Phi|^2 = U^2 - 2 g z_T
linearised); (D2) smooth (tangential) detachment, zeta_x(x_s) = s_b(y), the buttock slope of the hull at the edge, i.e.
sigma = U zeta_x = U s_b by the kinematic condition. Discretely, with s the distance downstream of the edge, h = dx:
(3 sigma_f - sigma_{f+1})/2 = U s_b (linear extrapolation to s = 0); (sigma_f + sigma_{f+1})/2 - k(phi_{f+1} - phi_f)/h^2 =
k g z_T/(U h) (quadratic fit through phi at s = h/2, 3h/2 with dphi/ds(0) = -g z_T/U, condition at s = h). In the linear
model the free surface behind the transom lies on z = 0, so the transom face (x = x_s, z_T < z < 0) borders the
transferred fluid; the Green identity needs a closed body, and the face carries the edge value of the same condition,
phi_x = g z_T(y)/U (in the linear transfer the face shrinks to the edge). This closure is a modelling choice (it lets
fluid cross the face, so the near/far identity above does not apply to the transom hull: T3 is information). The face
is dry: no force on it; the hull then lacks the hydrostatic push of the face, -eta F_face (exact integral,
`hull.face_hydrostatics`). WET (WaveBEM W-33/W-34, empirical, EMPIRICAL_REGISTER_RANKINE.md B4-1..3): the same rows and
closure plus a dead-water pressure patch p_a = -(1 - eta) rho g |z_h| on the free surface behind the transom (z_h the
WaveBEM hollow: parabola from z_T with the buttock slope to 0 at l_h |z_T|, l_h = 5), so g zeta = U phi_x - p_a/rho and
k phi_xx + phi_z = (U/(rho g)) p_a,x, i.e. sigma = k phi_xx + f, f = (1 - eta) U d|z_h|/dx: every row is written for
sigma - f (right-hand side + A f); the virtual face carries (1 - eta) of the hydrostatic face force, which enters the
equilibrium (P-10; WaveBEM subtracts it only in post-processing, W-34). eta = 1 reproduces the dry branch exactly (T1).
The branches are tabulated separately; nothing weights between them (G1).

**Long-wave limit (H9; open).** For k0 L <~ 1.5 (Fn >~ 0.8 at B/L 0.1) the solutions with the band blow up behind the
stern (B4-E10), growing with the beam (B/L 0.005, 0.02, 0.05: bounded; 0.1: x2e4) and with resolution. Analysis of a
uniformly sheared band: interpolation of a lateral mode at offset delta has |I| < 1 (|I|^2 = (1 - delta^2 + delta^2 cos
beta h)^2 + delta^2 sin^2 beta h), and the pure second difference I q + I*/q - 2 = 0 (the k -> infinity limit of the rows)
then has a reciprocal pair q, 1/q* off the unit circle: the double root q = 1 of the lattice (neutral) splits and one
root grows. At finite z the mass term moves the roots back onto the circle, which is why the validation range (k0 L >=
5, Fn <= 0.45) is stable. Remedy tried (B4-E7): phi at the row targets from the representation (no interpolation of phi,
PHI_MODE "exact"): unstable at Fn 0.4 on fine grids through the raised targets of the hull-adjacent line; rejected.
Candidates for the next step: rows of the hull-adjacent line in the double-body (Dawson) linearisation, whose base flow
satisfies the wall condition at the waterline corner (the NK corner data are incompatible in the g -> 0 limit: phi = 0
on z = 0 forces phi_y = 0 there while the wall wants phi_y = -y_w' U), or a column-shifted lattice (all lines of a column
shifted by y_w(x), FFT per column pair) with a symmetric interpolation.

### 9.5 Findings, failures and open items of B4

1. **The surface-piercing solver works and is verified in the validation range (Fn 0.2-0.45).** **[B4 review: "verified for Fn 0.2-0.45" overstated -- near/far covered the pointed hull only to Fn 0.408 (now 0.5,
   9.9 item 4); the transom at 0.45 has no passing identity; convergence is weaker than stated (9.9 item 5).]** Near and
   far field agree within 0.3-3.3 % at every fixed and free Fn (H6), the thin-ship limit reproduces Michell to 0.3 % (H8), the grid
   series changes C_near by 1.1 % and Fz by 0.01 % between the two finest members (H11), FFT and dense solves agree to
   1e-12 (H1), similarity holds to 1e-11 (H5), the lattice rows are the B3 rows (H2), the equilibrium converges in 3-5
   steps (H12).
2. **Validation against Kajitani et al. 1983 (first in Track B), as registered:** sinkage 7/7 PASS (6-14 % low);
   C_W fixed 0/7 (ratios 0.53-0.78 vs the four-series mean; the 0.408 member had no reference: registration defect);
   wave profile 3/6 (passes up to Fn 0.289; bow crest 20-25 % low throughout); trim 1/7 (0.04-0.25 deg too bow-up);
   C_W free 5/7. The linear Neumann-Kelvin model (linear free surface on z = 0, exact hull, quadratic Bernoulli) under-
   predicts the wave resistance of the fixed Wigley by 22-47 % and the bow wave by 20-25 %; the two experimental sources
   themselves differ by up to 40 % at low Fn. Not tuned (V2). **[Withdrawn in the B4 review: "22-47 %" and "40 %" rest on
   the faulty V1 reference; like-with-like results in 9.9 item 2.]** The usual next step is the nonlinear steady solution
   (design P-6, step S3) or the double-body linearisation, both on the same core.
3. **Waterline term sign (method, 9.2).** The linear model's wave resistance is R_p MINUS (rho g/2) oint zeta^2 n_x dl
   (momentum balance with fluid crossing z = 0); adding it (the strip force on the exact hull) is inconsistent with the
   wave cut by 30-40 % at B/L 0.1 and equals Michell for every beam (B4-E5). R_p alone is closer to the measurements
   (ratios 0.72-1.00) but is not a wave resistance of the model; reported only as information. **[Reworded in the B4
   review (9.9 item 3): R_p is the hull force of the linear model; the identity only shows that hull force and wave
   momentum flux differ by the waterline term at O(eps^2); which matches a measurement is empirical; the ratios
   0.72-1.00 used the faulty reference.]**
4. **H9 (M4, g -> 0) FAILS: the band rows are unstable in the long-wave limit** (k0 L <~ 1.5); analysis and candidate
   remedies in 9.2. Consequence: steady tables from this solver are limited to k0 L >~ 3 (Fn <~ 0.55 for L/B 10) until
   D4 (design 8.8) is resolved. **[B4 review: "Fn <~ 0.55" was untested; validity now stated per 9.9 item 4.]** This blocks the any-speed use above the validation range.
5. **H4 FAILS (6.6 % vs 5 %)**, but B4-E11 shows the split reference varies by +-15 % with the grid while the implemented
   coefficient varies by 0.9 %: the check's reference is not converged next to a surface-piercing hull; redesign needed
   (item in 9.6).
6. **Transom.** **[B4 review: T1 and T2 hold by construction and the wet free-equilibrium path was not exercised; the
   transom branches are implemented, NOT verified (9.9 item 7).]** Both branches are implemented with the registered
   boundary conditions; identities pass (T1, T2), the free equilibrium converges (T5). The transferred face condition lets fluid cross the face, so no near/far identity is
   claimed (T3 info: +6 ... +12 % dry, -16 ... 0 % wet eta = 0). Doctors' regression (as quoted in WaveBEM, original not
   obtained) predicts dry at Fn_T 2.8 and 3.6; at the registered lower scatter bound eta = 0.63 at Fn 0.35. No transom
   validation (no data for the test hull; NPL needs an IGES reader and the C_T/C_R question resolved).
7. **Wall time:** full tier 3 603 s, 3 s over T1 (FAIL). Fixes without changing results: Michell's integral by a fixed
   vectorised rule in t (about 13 min of H8), vectorised row building and line-based phi_x (Python loops), transom band
   ending downstream.
8. **Data.** The sinkage definition printed by Yang et al. (s = (dF' + dA')/Lpp) is twice the mean sinkage; the registered
   double-body rule chose the "mean" reading (c_db 0.01874 vs curve 0.01844), i.e. the plotted curve is Kajitani's mean
   sinkage. Original Kajitani tables, Journee 1992 and Doctors et al. 2007 could not be retrieved (HTTP 403 / paywall);
   none was indispensable.

### 9.6 Next step and estimate (W4)

1. **D4, long-wave stability of the band** (blocks Fn >~ 0.55): Dawson (double-body) rows for the lines next to the hull,
   or a column-shifted lattice with symmetric interpolation; criterion: H9 (5 % at Fn 8) and the B4 rows unchanged within
   their tolerances. Estimate 4-8 h agent wall-clock; bottleneck: the stability analysis of the new rows (the D1 tools
   carry over), then the high-Fn grids.
2. **H4 reference** redesign (exact base-flow derivatives from a refined body, rows near the hull excluded): 1-2 h.
3. **Tier time:** vectorised Michell and row building to bring the full tier back under 1 h with margin: 1-2 h.
4. **Accuracy at the validation level:** nonlinear steady free surface (design P-6/S3) on this core, re-run V1-V5 (same
   registered tolerances): 1.5-2.5 days (design 8.7), bottleneck the iteration robustness near the hump and the moving
   waterline. **[B4 review: rationale and registration replaced by 9.10 item 4.]**
5. **Transom data:** NPL hull (needs an IGES reader or offsets) or the Southampton series; Doctors et al. 2007 scatter
   for B4-1. Ask the user only if a paid source becomes indispensable.

### 9.7 Review of B4 (2026-10-06): fixes and registration of the changed and new rows (written before the re-run; V1)

The independent review of B4 raised one blocker, two majors and nine minors. Every change of code, data or rows is
registered here BEFORE the re-run (registration log entries `b4:### 9.1` (corrected text, 9.7.9), `b4:### 9.7`,
`b4:code_thresholds`, `b4:tier_jobs`, and, new (acceptance N2), the sha256 of every file of `hydro/rankine/` and of
`studies/rankine_hull_verify.py`, `studies/rankine_wigley_scan.py`, `studies/rankine_verify.py`; every tier JSON now
records the code hashes at its start, whether they were registered, and any file changed during the run). Rows added
after the B4 run are marked so in their text; "values known" says what had been computed before this registration
(B4 tiers, or the exploration runs B4R-E1..E6 listed at the end of this section). No registered threshold of 9.1 is
changed; no row is removed.

**9.7.1 Blocker: the V1 reference data are faulty.** The four WaveBEM C_W series (`wavebem_wigleyDragExp1-4.csv`) do
not reproduce the measured markers of the scanned figure (Hendrix & Noblesse 1988, copy `wavebem_tokyoWigley.pdf`,
page "- 61 -", upper panel) at Fn < 0.3. Re-reading (script `studies/rankine_wigley_scan.py`, output
`studies/_cache/rankine/b4_scan_reading.json`): the page's embedded 300-dpi scan is calibrated per panel by an affine
map fitted to the tick marks the script detects on the four frame lines (C_W panel: 2483 px per unit Fn, 118.9 px per
1e-3; residuals 0.0008 in Fn, 0.015e-3 in C_W; C_WP panel: 2479, 110.2; 0.0005, 0.022e-3). Overlay
(`studies/_cache/rankine/b4_cw_overlay.png`; the reviewer's `cw_overlay*.png` show the same): the WaveBEM points lie on
or near the zeroth- and first-order slender-ship lines, on average +0.16, +0.27, +0.28, +0.21 e-3 above the U. Tokyo
vector curve of Yang et al. (2000, fig 5a, fixed) at Fn < 0.3 (series 1-4), while that curve passes through the U. Tokyo
markers of the same scan (U. Tokyo markers at Fn 0.19-0.21 within -0.06 ... +0.03e-3 of it), which confirms the
calibration. The fourth file is labelled UT3 (a third U. Tokyo series), not Y.N.U.; Y.N.U. ("Y.M.U." in the legend) is
not digitised (its markers are negative at Fn 0.11-0.2). **Decision:** V1 stays in the tier exactly as registered and
is reported, but labelled "invalid reference (data transcription defect), not a model verdict"; its B4 verdict (0/7) is
withdrawn as a statement about the model. SOURCES.md is corrected (9.7.11).

**9.7.2 Major: compare like with like.** By H6, R_near = R_p - (rho g/2) oint zeta^2 n_x dl equals the far-field wave
energy flux; the C_W of the source figure is derived from the total resistance with a form factor (C_T - (1 + k) C_F),
goes negative for Y.N.U. and scatters between laboratories by more than its own size at Fn <= 0.2; the same figure gives
the wave-pattern resistance C_WP (S.R.I., U. Tokyo; from wave cuts), the experimental counterpart of the energy flux.
Which computed force matches a measured force is an empirical question; the linear model's hull force R_p and its wave
momentum flux differ by the waterline term, both at O(eps^2) (9.5 item 3 is reworded accordingly in 9.8). Registered
mapping (for this re-run and for the next steps, e.g. the nonlinear solver):

| Computed quantity | Definition | Experimental counterpart | Row |
|---|---|---|---|
| R_near | R_p - waterline term = wave energy flux (H6) | C_WP (wave-pattern resistance), U. Tokyo markers of the scan (re-read here; S.R.I. where it overlaps) | V1b |
| R_p | hull force of the linear model: d'Alembert-corrected pressure resistance of the hull below z = 0 | C_W = C_T - (1 + k) C_F, U. Tokyo curve (Yang et al. 2000 fig 5a fixed; fig 5a free for the free model) | V1a, V5a |
| R_p + strip | R_p + (rho g/2) oint zeta^2 n_x dl (the B4 first version) | C_W | information only |

Tolerances (minor issue 10 applied now): max(20 % of the reference, absolute floor tied to the measured lab scatter).
- C_W floor 0.20e-3: markers read in Fn 0.185-0.215 (`data/external/wigley/hn1988_p61_cw_markers_fn020.csv`, 11
  markers, reading error +-0.025e-3, symbol identification uncertain for 3): S.R.I. lies 0.13-0.18e-3 below the U. Tokyo
  curve, U. Tokyo within -0.06 ... +0.03e-3; the largest S.R.I./U.T. deviation 0.18e-3, rounded up to 0.20e-3. Y.N.U.
  (0.2-0.45e-3 below, negative C_W at lower Fn: a different form-factor practice) is not counted.
- C_WP floor 0.10e-3: S.R.I. 1.193e-3 at Fn 0.3499 vs U. Tokyo 1.137e-3 at 0.3535 (0.056e-3) plus twice the reading error
  (0.027e-3), 0.11e-3, rounded to one figure; at Fn 0.25, 0.288, 0.315 the two laboratories' markers overlap (difference
  below half a marker, about 0.05e-3).
- V1b references: U. Tokyo C_WP markers (`data/external/wigley/hn1988_p61_cwp_markers.csv`, read by the script from
  approximate centres refined by the centroid of the opened blob): 0.721, 0.664, 0.992, 1.503, 1.137, 2.077 e-3 at
  marker Fn 0.2498, 0.2666, 0.2883, 0.3150, 0.3535, 0.4078, compared at the nominal Fn 0.25, 0.267, 0.289, 0.316, 0.354,
  0.408 (marker Fn within 0.0015 of nominal, reading error 0.0012-0.002 in Fn and 0.027-0.046e-3 in C_WP); no row at Fn
  0.2 (no U. Tokyo C_WP marker there). The reviewer's independent reading (0.72, 0.67, 1.00, 1.50, 1.17, 2.09) agrees
  within 0.033e-3; his comparison is reported only as post-run exploration (9.8).
- Data-range rule (lesson of the 0.408 defect): an amended row exists only where its reference exists.

**9.7.3 Major: transom branches -- momentum identity with the face closure (derived here, M1), D1 residual, grid pair,
wet free equilibrium.** Control volume and notation as in 9.2; body = hull H + transom face P (x_b = x_aft), closed by the
lid z = 0; on the face dphi/dn = phi_n = n_x g z_T/U (both branches), so fluid crosses it: u.n_V = U n_bx - phi_n with
n_V = -n_b the normal out of the fluid volume (zero trim: U - g z_T/U flows out of the face). x-momentum of the exact
potential flow -U x + phi in z < 0 (Bernoulli pressure p = -rho g z + rho (U phi_x - |grad phi|^2/2)):
- hull and face pressure: -int_{H+P} p n_bx dS; the hydrostatic part is rho g int_{H+P} z n_bx dS = 0 (closed body,
  n_x = 0 on the lid), so only the dynamic pressure p_d remains;
- face momentum flux rho int_P u_x (u.n_V) dS, u_x = -U + phi_x;
- mass: int_P u.n_V + int_F phi_z - int_{P_d} phi_x (+ lateral) = 0, so the -rho U terms of the free surface F, of the
  downstream plane P_d and of the lateral planes add up to +rho U int_P u.n_V; with the face flux:
  rho int_P (U + u_x)(u.n_V) = rho int_P phi_x (U n_bx - phi_n) dS;
- free surface: rho int_F phi_x phi_z = -(rho k/2) oint_{dF} phi_x^2 nu_x dl (phi_z = -k phi_xx; dry branch), with
  nu_x = -n_bx at the hull waterline (gives W_hull = (rho g/2) int zeta^2 n_x dl), nu_x = +1 at the transom edge (the
  transom segment of the waterline contour, n_bx = -1 there) and -1 at P_d;
- wet branch: phi_z = -k phi_xx + (U/(rho g)) p_a,x adds int_F zeta_0 p_a,x dA, zeta_0 = (U/g) phi_x.

Collecting (R_waves the Havelock far field as in 9.2):

    R_waves = R_dynH - W_hull + E_edge + K_face + P_patch,
    R_dynH  = -int_H p_d n_x dS            (hull panels only; code F["R_raw"])
    E_edge  = (rho g/2) int_edge zeta_e^2 dy     (zeta_e = (U/g) phi_x at the edge; D1 makes it z_T)
    K_face  = rho int_P [phi_x phi_n - |grad phi|^2 n_x/2] dS   (zero trim: (rho/2) int_P (phi_y^2 + phi_z^2 - phi_x^2) dS)
    P_patch = (1 - eta) rho g int_F zeta_0 d|z_h|/dx dA          (wet branch, 0 for the dry one).

The O(U) parts of the face flux cancel against the mass term, leaving K_face (the face acts like a cut plane), and the
static push of the face does not appear: at zero trim and with zeta_e = z_T, E_edge equals the calm-water face force
(rho g/2) int z_T^2 dy, so the B4 "C_total" (R_dynH - W_hull + eta F_face) differs from R_waves only by K_face, by
E_edge(zeta_e) - E_edge(z_T), and, wet, by P_patch + (1 - eta) F_face. In code: `PiercingNK.transom_identity` (face
gradient by `structured_gradient` on the 6 x 8 face mesh; zeta_e independently of the D1 row from the quadratic through
phi at s = h/2, 3h/2, 5h/2 of each transom line: zeta_e = (U/g)(2 phi_1 - 3 phi_2 + phi_3)/h).

Rows (T1 moved to a small grid because it holds by construction; the wet(eta_D) solve is skipped where eta_D = 1, since it
is then the dry solve by construction; the full-tier transom solves: COARSE Fn 0.35 dry, wet at the registered lower
scatter bound eta(0.8 Fn_T) = 0.628, wet eta = 0; COARSE Fn 0.45 dry, wet eta = 0; PROD Fn 0.35 dry, wet eta = 0 (fits
the GPU, B4R-E2)):

| ID | What | Acceptance item | Threshold | Values known at registration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| T3 | R_id/R_cut - 1 on every fixed transom solve (7 full, 2 quick), R_id = R_dynH - W_hull + E_edge(zeta_e) + K_face + P_patch; variant with zeta_e = z_T and the B4 C_total as information | M3 (identity) | 5 % (as H6) | yes: COARSE Fn 0.35 dry +14.4 %, wet eta = 0 +17.0 %, PROD Fn 0.35 dry +10.2 % (B4R-E2); expected FAIL |
| T6 | D1 edge residual rms(zeta_e - z_T)/rms(z_T) (widths as weights) | M2 (condition satisfied) | 10 % at PROD; info at COARSE | yes: COARSE 7.6 % (dry), 13.5 % (wet 0), PROD 3.8 % (dry) at Fn 0.35 |
| T7 | COARSE -> PROD pair at Fn 0.35, dry and wet eta = 0: D1 residual decreases; C_total, C_cut, C_id, zeta_e as information | M5 | decrease | dry yes (7.6 -> 3.8 %) |
| T5 | free equilibrium residual: Fn 0.45 dry (registered B4) and, new, Fn 0.35 wet eta = 0 and eta = 0.628 (patch and (1 - eta) face force in the equilibrium, P-10) | P-7 | 1e-6 W | smoke only (tiny grid, Fn 0.45 wet 0: converged in 5 steps) |
| T8 | d'Alembert defect of the transom hull + face mesh in the double-body flow (face as a wall: total x-force exactly 0), hull 60x12 and 90x18, zero trim and (0.0066, 0.0255) | -- | info | yes (B4R-E5: -1.0, -2.2, -0.66, -1.1 e-3 in C) |
| T1, T2, T4 | as in 9.1 (T1 on the H1 small grid; T2 over all fixed solves) | M2 | 1e-12; 1e-10; info | yes |

Until T3 passes, the transom branches are **implemented, not verified**. The exploration (B4R-E3..E5) already points to
the cause: the pressure integration at the transom corner is not converged (the double-body d'Alembert defect of these
meshes is of the size of the resistance, and refining the hull mesh at a fixed free-surface grid changes R_dynH by 18 %).

**9.7.4 Minor: hull-wall virtual point.** The B4 derivation (9.2, `_Columns` docstring, register device table) claimed
d(sigma)/dn = 0 because "the wall normal does not depend on z"; that is wrong for any hull whose sections curve below the
waterline. Corrected derivation (docstring of `piercing._Columns`): the body condition on the hull side y = H(x, z)
(earth frame at the attitude) is phi_y = -H_x (U - phi_x) - H_z sigma; its derivative along the hull in z at z = 0, for
H_z = 0 there, is sigma_y = H_x sigma_x + H_xz (U - phi_x) + H_zz sigma (Wigley midship H_zz = -B/T^2 = -25.6/L: the B4
ghost of sigma was off by 2 d |H_zz|, up to 14 % at PROD). With flare (H_z != 0) the sigma condition also contains
H_z (2 phi_yy + phi_xx) - H_x H_z phi_xy and 1/(1 - H_z^2), which need lateral second derivatives at the wall and are not
included. Production ghost from now on (`GHOST_MODE = "wall"`): separate sigma and phi lists,
sigma_g = (1 + a - 2 d H_zz) sigma_0 - a sigma_-1 + b (phi_-1 - phi_0) - 2 d H_xz U, phi_g = (1 + a) phi_0 - a phi_-1 +
2 d H_z sigma_0 + 2 d H_x U (a = 2 d H_x/dx, b = 2 d H_xz/dx; index -1 = the upstream column); H and its derivatives from
the hull form at the attitude (`HullForm.wall_derivatives`, one-sided in z from below z = 0). The B4 ghost remains
available as "wall_vertical" for comparison. Known effect (B4R-E1): Wigley PROD Fn 0.316 C_near +5.2e-5 relative (as the
reviewer's experiment), transom COARSE Fn 0.35 C_total +1e-4 relative although 2 d |H_zz| reaches 2.0 next to the
transom (diagnostic `info["ghost_curv"]` per solve; a value above ~0.5 means the band does not resolve the section
curvature there).

| ID | What | Acceptance item | Threshold | Values known at registration |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| H13 | flared Wigley (`hull.wigley_flare`, f = 0.3: H_z = 0.24 at midship, 13.5 deg), Fn 0.316, PROD, production ghost: R_near/R_cut - 1 (binding); the "wall_vertical" ghost on the same grid: C_near, near/cut, bow crest (info) | G4 (check before any non-Wigley use) | 5 % | smoke on a tiny grid only (+6.0 %) |

(0.933845/2.5^2); no rerun needed. H&N's data reference is McCarthy (1985), DTNSRDC-85/011 (which collects the Kajitani

`*_water_line.txt`) come from its fully nonlinear unsteady time-domain model (different physics; the force-file columns
are undocumented); it is kept as a code-to-code comparison for the nonlinear step (design S3).

### 9.9 Findings after the review (replace the conflicting statements of 9.4-9.6, which are marked in place)

1. **V1 of B4 has no valid reference** (9.7.1): the WaveBEM C_W files follow the slender-ship theory lines at Fn < 0.3,
   not the measured markers; the B4 statements built on them -- "the two experimental sources disagree by up to 40 %",
   "the linear model under-predicts the wave resistance by 22-47 %", "R_p alone is closer (0.72-1.00)" -- are withdrawn.
2. **Like-with-like validation** (registered after the B4 run; values known): the wave energy flux of the linear model
   (R_near = wave cut) matches the measured wave-pattern resistance C_WP within 3 % at Fn 0.25 and 0.267 and within 8-9 %
   at 0.289 and 0.408, and is low by 26 % at 0.316 and by 18 % at 0.354 (the measured C_WP has a hump of 1.50e-3 at Fn 0.316 that the
   linear model does not reproduce); the hull force R_p matches the C_T-based C_W within 16 % for
   Fn >= 0.25 and is 52 % (0.22e-3) high at Fn 0.2, where the laboratories themselves differ by 0.2e-3; free model:
   R_p within 21 % at every Fn. The linear model's remaining validation misfits are therefore the C_WP hump at Fn
   0.32-0.35, the bow crest (20-25 % low, V2) and the trim (0.04-0.25 deg too bow-up, V4); sinkage is 3-14 % low.
3. **Waterline term (reworded 9.5 item 3).** The identity R_p = W + R_waves shows only that the linear model's hull force
   and its wave momentum flux differ by the waterline term, both at O(eps^2); R_p is the hull force of the linear model,
   not an error. Which of the two matches a measured quantity is empirical: here R_near matches C_WP and R_p matches C_W
   (C_T-based) about equally well (item 2). Only H6 tests the sign of the term (H8 cannot: R_p + strip also tends to
   Michell, limit 1.007).
4. **Validity range** (9.7.7): pointed Wigley hull verified by near/far (H6) for Fn 0.2-0.5 (seven Fn plus 0.5, within
   0.3-3.3 %); Fn 0.5-0.6 unverified; Fn 0.6 fails near/far (B4-E10, -12 %); Fn >= 0.8 unstable (H9). The transom results
   at Fn 0.35/0.45 are information only.
5. **Convergence (M5)** is weaker than B4 stated: in the hull mesh the solution is converged (C_near to 1.5e-4, Fz with
   observed order 2.3); refining the free surface at a fixed hull mesh does NOT converge (C_near +1.8 % then +3.4 %, the
   far-field cut alike), and the joint series is non-monotone (amplitude 2.2 %). The solution depends on the ratio of
   free-surface to hull resolution at the waterline (the band panels next to the hull become finer than the hull's
   waterline rows); this is an open accuracy item (uncertainty of C_near about +-3 %, larger than the 1.1 % "finest change"
   of B4). The two waterline-elevation estimators converge towards each other (H11d), slowly.
6. **H8** passes (1.003) but its uncertainty (+-0.049) exceeds the 3 % tolerance: the thin-ship limit is consistent with
   Michell, not proven to 3 %.
7. **Transom: implemented, not verified.** The momentum identity derived in 9.7.3 does not close (+6.5 ... +17 %; +8 ... +10 %
   at PROD); it improves with joint refinement but not to 5 %, and the error grows with transom immersion (+17 ... +20 % at
   the free attitudes). Cause identified in the exploration and confirmed by T8: the pressure integration at the transom
   corner is not converged (double-body d'Alembert defect of the mesh as large as the resistance; R_dynH changes by 18 %
   when only the hull mesh is refined). The D1 condition itself converges (3.8-6.6 % at PROD, first order). The wet free
   equilibrium (patch and (1 - eta) face force) converges in 4 steps.
8. **Hull-wall ghost** corrected (curvature and flare terms); effect on the Wigley 5e-5, on the flared check hull 0.5 %
   (H13 passes with both ghosts); the omitted flare terms of the sigma condition stay an open item for strongly flared
   hulls.
9. **H4 and H9** fail as before (reference redesign; D4 long-wave instability).

### 9.10 Next step and estimate after the review (W4; replaces 9.6 where they differ)

1. **Waterline resolution coupling (new, from H11b):** make the hull mesh's waterline rows and the band's line-0 spacing
   consistent (hull rows clustered to the band spacing, or hull stations tied to the free-surface columns), then repeat
   H11b/c; criterion: contraction in both series and an observed order. 2-4 h; bottleneck: GPU time of the 135-member
   grids (~2 min each).
2. **Transom corner:** a corner-resolving hull/face discretisation (stations and face rows clustered to the edge, a
   higher-order or singularity-aware gradient at the corner) checked first by the double-body d'Alembert defect (T8,
   target < 2 % of C_total), then T3. 4-8 h; bottleneck: the corner singularity analysis.
3. **D4** (long-wave stability, H9) and **H4** reference: as in 9.6 (4-8 h; 1-2 h).
4. **Nonlinear steady free surface** (design P-6/S3): motivated by the bow crest (V2), the trim (V4) and the C_WP hump at
   Fn 0.32-0.35, not by the withdrawn "22-47 %" under-prediction; registered against C_WP (energy flux) and C_W (hull
   force) with relative-plus-absolute tolerances (9.7.2), plus WaveBEM's nonlinear results as a code-to-code row.
   1.5-2.5 days (design 8.7).

## 10. Stage B5 (2026-10-06): forward-speed radiation and diffraction as one linear time-invariant system; radiation tables

Code: `hydro/rankine/unsteady.py` (free-surface grid with beaches on all sides, upwind-biased d/dx, the LTI system and its
exact frequency response, instantaneous / static / steady limits, time-domain RK4 of the same system, forces in the
direct-pressure and Ogilvie-Tuck forms, Green-identity terms), `hydro/rankine/tables.py` (radiation-table interface for
the any-speed plant), `hydro/rankine/hull.py` (new forms `wigley_journee`, `prismatic_v`). Checks:
`studies/rankine_unsteady_verify.py` (tiers quick / full, registration keys `b5:*` in `registration_log.json`). Data:
`studies/rankine_journee_data.py` -> `data/external/wigley_journee/` (SOURCES.md section wigley_journee/).

### 10.1 Registration of the B5 checks (written before the tiers ran; V1)

Model (all rows): linear Neumann-Kelvin (NK) radiation and diffraction about the uniform stream, hull exact below z = 0
at the mean attitude, m-terms of the uniform stream (m_5 = U n_3), beaches on all sides, ONE LTI system per speed and
attitude evaluated exactly in the frequency domain (10.2). Primary force = direct pressure integration of
p = -rho (phi_t - U phi_x) ("D-form"); the Ogilvie-Tuck m-term form ("OT-form") is information (10.2 item 3 explains
the choice; it was made after the exploration run B5-E2 had shown the Fn 0.3 heave coefficients of both forms next to
Journee's values, so "values known" applies to the V2 rows). Interior d/dx: fifth-order upwind-biased, third order next
to the ends, wall rule of 10.2 item 2.

Settings (non-dimensional, L = 1, g = 9.81, rho = 1; in the code as GRID_BASE etc.): Journee's Wigley I
(`wigley_journee`, L/B 10, L/T 16, V = 0.0946 m^3 on the 3 m model, computed 0.09462); moments about G with x_G = 0,
k_yy = 0.25 L, and KG = 0.170 m on the 3 m model ASSUMED (not in the available sources; sensitivity +-0.05 m reported).
Free-surface grid GRID_BASE: upstream 1.5 L, downstream 4.0 L, lateral 3.0 L beyond the waterline; stretching 1.12 /
1.06 / 1.08 per cell; uniform zones 0.5 L downstream and 0.3 L laterally; beach zones 0.75 / 2.0 / 1.5 L; nu_max =
1.5 sqrt(g/L). PROD: M = 60 columns over the waterline, hull 60 x 12; COARSE: M = 40; grid series M = 40/60/80 and hull
40x8 / 60x12 / 90x18. CHECK (identities): symmetric domain 3 L each way, stretching 1.06, beaches 1.5 L, no uniform zone
downstream, so that the +U and -U problems share the free-surface panels (a row is invalid otherwise; the code checks
it). SMALL (spectrum, Kramers-Kronig, time domain): M = 20, hull 20 x 5. Journee's test frequencies omega' = omega
sqrt(L/g) = 1.659, 2.212, 2.765, 3.318, 3.871, 4.424, 5.530 (3 ... 10 rad/s on the 3 m model).

"Values known" says what had been computed (exploration 10.3 on these or nearby settings, or smoke runs on tiny
non-registered grids) when the threshold was written.

Validation tolerance rationale (lesson of B4: relative + absolute): 20 % is the design proposal for forward-speed
coefficients; the absolute floor 10 % of the series maximum covers small values and the secondary transcription (the
J. Mar. Sci. Eng. abscissae deviate from Journee's test frequencies by up to 0.07 in omega'; points are compared at the
transcribed abscissae). Data-range rule: rows exist only where a transcribed point exists. The secondary sources plot
Journee's values; their transcription accuracy is unknown, so a V failure is a statement about the model OR the
transcription, and is reported as such. The point at omega' = 1.68 of the Fn 0.2 A33' series (1.63, about twice its
neighbours and the Fn 0.3 value at the same frequency) is kept as registered and flagged in advance as a probable
transcription outlier.

**Amendment (2026-10-07, after the quick tier, before the full tier; no threshold, row or tier job changed):** (i) the
demo table records the passivity of both force forms (`passive_ot`, and per node the minimum over omega of
lambda_min(B_sym)/max abs lambda(B_sym), `bsym_min`, `bsym_min_ot`), because the quick tier showed every node of the
D-form table non-passive (information row PV; 10.4); (ii) docstring of `hydro/rankine/__init__.py` (module list). The
code hashes change, so the registration entry is renewed; the quick-tier record keeps the first entry.

new dataclass fields of `RadiationTable` before the OT fields while `build_radiation_table` passed its arguments by
position, so the OT arrays of the full-tier demo table were stored under shifted names (the D-form arrays, which the
binding PV rows check, were correct). Fix: keyword arguments. Also a docstring correction in `unsteady.py` (item 4 now
states the fifth-order production stencil). Only the PV job is re-run (`--only PV`); the full-tier record of the
other jobs stands.

### 10.2 Formulation and derivations (M1)

1. **One LTI system.** Body frame, stream -U x_hat, linearised kinematic and dynamic conditions with a beach nu(x, y)
   >= 0: zeta_t = U zeta_x + phi_z - nu zeta, phi_t = U phi_x - g zeta - nu phi on z = 0. Representation as B3/B4 (rigid-lid
   and port/starboard images; free-surface sources sigma = -phi_z): 4 pi phi_F = D_FB phi_B - S_FB phi_n - S_FF sigma,
   (2 pi - D_BB) phi_B + S_BF sigma = -S_BB phi_n, i.e. phi_F = R_s sigma + R_n phi_n. With N = diag(nu) - U D the system is
   zeta' = -N zeta - sigma, phi_F' = -N phi_F - g zeta, sigma = R_s^-1 (phi_F - R_n phi_n(t)): y' = M y + b(t). Its transfer
   function at e^{i w t}: from the dynamic condition zeta = -(1/g) L phi_F, L = i w + N, and from the kinematic one
   sigma = -L zeta = (1/g) L^2 phi_F, so [I - (1/g) L^2 R_s] sigma = (1/g) L^2 R_n phi_n with L^2 = N^2 - w^2 + 2 i w N as
   matrix products (an x-dependent beach enters exactly as in the time domain). At U = 0 this is phi_z = (w - i nu)^2
   phi/g (the stage-2 condition, radiation.py). w -> infinity: phi_F -> 0 (instantaneous problem); w = 0: the steady NK
   problem on the same grid. One complex dense LU per frequency (all modes and headings share it); the blocks R_s,
   N R_s, N^2 R_s are real and built once.
2. **Discrete d/dx.** A lattice mode of the semi-discrete system has lambda = -nu + U D^ +- i sqrt(g kappa_h)
   (kappa_h > 0 the discrete Dirichlet-to-Neumann symbol), so Re(lambda) = -nu + U Re(D^). A skew (central) D is
   neutral, but its saw-tooth mode has D^ = 0: it neither propagates nor reaches a beach -- an undamped interior mode. An
   upwind-biased D (upwind = against the stream) has U Re(D^) <= 0: third order (4 points) Re(D^) dx = -(1 - cos
   theta)^2/3 (damping about theta^3/12 per radian of travel), fifth order (6 points; production) -(16/15) sin^6(theta/2)
   ~ -theta^6/60. Band grid: d/dx at fixed y = i_x d/di + j_x d/dj, i_x = y_j/J, j_x = -y_i/J, each index derivative
   upwind-biased by the sign of its contravariant advection velocity (U i_x, U j_x); the coordinates are differentiated
   with the same stencils (linear functions exact). Ends: inflow ghosts zero (undisturbed stream; upstream end and
   lateral edge), centre-plane mirror (even fields), fully upwind second-order formulas at the outflow end. **Wall
   rule:** in the afterbody the fixed-y characteristics of the NK free-surface operator leave the hull wall, so the
   upwind side of the cross term j_x d/dj points into the hull; a one-sided downwind formula there made the system
   unstable (B5-E4: prismatic body Re(lambda) = +1.75 sqrt(g/L)); production drops the cross term at the wall point (the
   band line is followed along the waterline, i.e. the advection is tangential to the hull at the waterline, as in a
   double-body linearisation) and takes it central at the next point -- a modelling choice confined to the first band
   line, whose width vanishes with refinement (O(dy) effect, measured by GS).
3. **Body condition and forces.** Exact condition on the oscillating body linearised about the mean position for the
   uniform stream W = -U x_hat: dphi/dn = sum_k (xdot_k n_k + x_k m_k), (m_4, m_5, m_6) = -(n x W) = (0, U n_3, -U n_2)
   (direct check for pitch: the rotated normal gives W.n_rot = -U n_x - U theta_5 n_z). Direct pressure: T_jk =
   -rho int_S (i w phi_k - U d_x phi_k) n_j dS, F_j = -T_jk x_k, T = -w^2 A + i w B. Generalised Tuck identity (derived
   with Stokes' theorem for a constant W and the rotation fields a_j = e_j or e_j x r; n.curl(a_j x W) = m_j): int_S [n_j
   (W.grad f) + m_j f] dS = int_S (W.n)(a_j.grad f) dS + oint_C f (a_j x W).dl. Ogilvie & Tuck's theorem is the case
   W.n = 0 (the double-body flow); for the uniform stream W.n = -U n_x != 0, so the OT form T^OT_jk = -rho int_S phi_k
   (i w n_j - m_j) dS differs from the direct pressure by E_j(phi_k) = int_S (U n_j d_x phi_k - m_j phi_k) dS = U int_S
   n_x (a_j.grad phi_k) dS - oint_C phi_k (a_j x W).dl, the "NK inconsistency" of order U n_x -- the same order as the
   steady-disturbance terms the NK linearisation drops. Choice of the primary form: the D-form is the NK model's own
   linearised pressure (the same one the B4 steady forces and the plant's pressure integration use) and needs no theorem
   the uniform stream violates; the OT form is reciprocal (item 4), has an exactly antisymmetric B_inf (energy-neutral
   instantaneous term) and is the slender-body form of strip theory. Both are tabulated; D is primary. Disclosure:
   B5-E2 had shown the Fn 0.3 A33' of both forms next to Journee's values (D closer) before this choice was written.
4. **Green identity between the +U and -U solutions; Timman-Newman and Haskind.** For phi (speed U) and psi (speed
   -U, same frequency) the free-surface part of Green's second identity is int_F (phi psi_z - psi phi_z) dA =
   -(1/g) int_F [phi (i w + nu + U d_x)^2 psi - psi (i w + nu - U d_x)^2 phi] dA = -(1/g) int_F d_x[2 (i w + nu) U phi
   psi + U^2 (phi psi_x - psi phi_x)] dA (the beach terms combine into the divergence as well), which reduces to the
   waterline (and the damped outer edge): G_F = -(1/g) oint_C [2 i w U phi psi + U^2 (phi psi_x - psi phi_x)] w'(x) dx over
   both sides (w = y_wl). With dphi_k/dn = i w n_k + m_k and dpsi_j/dn = i w n_j - m_j: **T^OT_jk(U) - T^OT_kj(-U) =
   -rho G_F(phi_k, psi_j)** (generalised Timman-Newman; the classical symmetry holds exactly only without a waterline,
   e.g. a submerged body, check TNs), and for the D-form T_jk(U) - T_kj(-U) = -rho G_F + rho [E_j(phi_k) + E_k(psi_j)].
   Haskind: **X^OT_D,j(U) = -rho int_S psi_j dphi_I/dn dS + rho G_F(phi_D, psi_j)**; the D-form diffraction force is that
   minus rho E_j(phi_D). Discrete checks: (a) G_F as the free-surface panel sum (tests the discrete Green identity of the
   representation; needs identical panels for +-U), (b) G_F by the waterline integral with hull-side values extrapolated
   to z = 0 (tests in addition the adjointness of the discrete free-surface operators, D_{-U} = -D_U^T on a uniform
   grid). On a flared waterline the fluid corner is re-entrant (angle 180 deg + deadrise), phi_x ~ r^(pi/alpha - 1) is
   singular there and (b) is not a valid discrete check (prismatic body: information only).
5. **Kramers-Kronig at forward speed and the Cummins form.** T(w) = -w^2 A_inf + i w B_inf + C_inf + i w K^(w),
   K^ = int_0^inf K e^{-i w t} dt, A_inf, B_inf, C_inf from phi_k -> i w phi_inf[n_k] + phi_inf[m_k] (phi_F = 0):
   A_inf = -rho int phi_inf[n_k] n_j, B_inf = -rho int (phi_inf[m_k] - U d_x phi_inf[n_k]) n_j, C_inf = rho U int d_x
   phi_inf[m_k] n_j (D-form); OT: B_inf = -rho int (phi_inf[m_k] n_j - phi_inf[n_k] m_j) (antisymmetric), C_inf =
   rho int phi_inf[m_k] m_j (C_inf,55 = -U^2 A_inf,33). At forward speed the static limit T(0) = C0 differs from C_inf
   (B5-E6: Kramers-Kronig with C_inf failed by 27-62 % for pitch, the error ~1/w^2): K(t) tends to the constant
   C0 - C_inf, so K^ has a pole K_inf/(i w) at 0 (Kara 2022 eq. 18 has the same constant a0). Splitting K = K_inf H(t) +
   K~(t): **T = -w^2 A_inf + i w B_inf + C0 + i w K~^(w)**, K~(t) = (2/pi) int_0^inf (B - B_inf) cos(w t) dw (integrable),
   and A(w) - A_inf + C0/w^2 = (2/pi) PV int_0^inf (B(w') - B_inf)/(w'^2 - w^2) dw' (the stage-2 Z6 form at U = 0). Plant
   form: F_rad = -A_inf a - B_inf v - int K~ v ds, with C0 supplied by the attitude dependence of F_steady (tables.py).
6. **Limits.** g -> 0 at fixed U, w: (i w + nu - U d_x)^2 phi = -g phi_z -> 0 with causality gives phi_F -> 0, i.e. T ->
   T_inf (high-speed / impact theory), error ~ g/(w^2 L) ~ 1/(Fn^2 (w L/U)^2). w -> infinity: the same limit.
7. **Pseudo-energy.** With E = (rho/2) int_V abs(grad phi)^2 + (rho g/2) int_F zeta^2 and M = rho int_F zeta phi_x, the
   NK conditions give d(E - U M)/dt = -rho int_S phi_t phi_n dS + rho U oint_C zeta phi_t n_x dl + rho int_F nu phi sigma
   - rho g int_F nu zeta^2 + rho U int_F [nu zeta phi_x + zeta (nu phi)_x] (the rho U int phi_x phi_z terms of dE/dt and
   U dM/dt cancel; at the waterline g zeta^2 - U zeta phi_x = -zeta phi_t by the dynamic condition). E - U M is
   indefinite (waves can carry negative pseudo-energy at forward speed): a balance, not a stability argument (stability
   is item 2 and check SP). The discrete residual measures the upwind dissipation, the BIE inconsistency and RK4; E
   (panel quadrature) is the exact invariant of the U = 0, nu = 0 system only if the area-weighted Dirichlet-to-Neumann
   map is symmetric, which collocation does not give exactly (B5-E5, E11).
8. **RAO.** 2 DOF (heave, pitch about G; surge restrained, towed model): (-w_e^2 M + T(w_e) + C) xi = X, M = diag(rho V,
   rho V k_yy^2), C33 = rho g A_w, C35 = C53 = -rho g A_w (x_f - x_G), C55 = rho g [I_L + A_w (x_f - x_G)^2 + V (z_B -
   z_G)]; heave RAO abs(xi_3)/eta_0, pitch abs(xi_5) L/(2 pi eta_0) (J. Mar. Sci. Eng. 9:87 normalisation).
