### 10.5 Findings, failures and open items of B5

1. **One LTI system, verified.** The forward-speed radiation/diffraction problem is one linear time-invariant system per
   speed and attitude whose exact frequency response, time-domain integration (TD 0.67 %) and Cummins reconstruction
   through the table pipeline (CU 0.35 %) agree; it is causal and stable at every forward speed tested (SP: every mode
   decays, Wigley Fn 0.2-1.0, prismatic Fn 0.4), Kramers-Kronig holds within 0.5 % at Fn 0.2 and 1.5-2.1 % at Fn 0.3 once the
   static stiffness C0 replaces C_inf (derived: at forward speed the memory function tends to a constant) [R: these KK
   magnitudes were an evaluation bug; corrected 0.03-0.06 %, 10.8.3], and it reduces
   exactly to the stage-2 zero-speed results (U0 <= 0.5 % on the diagonal, hemisphere vs Hulme 0.49 %), to the
   high-frequency / zero-gravity limit (INF, G0 0.27 % at Fn 8 with the predicted Fn^-2 approach -- the long-wave failure of
   the B4 steady band rows, H9, does not occur in this non-marching operator) and is Froude-similar to 3e-7.
2. **Haskind and Timman-Newman at forward speed.** For the NK model both hold only with a free-surface waterline term,
   derived here; with that term evaluated as the discrete free-surface sum both identities close to 0.2-0.6 % (Wigley,
   prismatic body) [R: that form is only the discrete Green identity of the BIE representation and does not test the
   free-surface operator; M3 Timman-Newman at forward speed for surface-piercing hulls had NO passing check (TN (b) 9.9 %,
   TNp (b) 312 %), 10.8.4]; with the waterline reduction Haskind closes to 1.3 % but Timman-Newman to 9.9 % (FAIL vs 3 %; 6.1 % at
   M = 60; the wall rule and the first-order waterline extrapolation, post-run (2)). The classical symmetry A35(U) =
   A53(-U) is a theorem only without a waterline: it holds to 0.43 % for a submerged spheroid, and fails for the
   surface-piercing hulls by the size of the waterline term (OT form 66 %, D-form 86 % on the Wigley, 117 % on the flared
   prismatic body). The classical Haskind relation misses 19 % of the excitation. **These are properties of the NK
   linearisation, not solver errors;** the double-body linearisation (design R3) is where the classical relations can be
   expected to hold better.
3. **Force definition matters at forward speed.** The direct pressure (D) and the Ogilvie-Tuck m-term form (OT) differ by
   the NK inconsistency E_j (derived generalised Tuck identity): A53 at Fn 0.3 is +0.017 (D) vs +0.086 (OT) vs +0.107 (strip
   theory). Validation does not single out a form: D fits A33' better at low frequency (Fn 0.3: 0.98 vs 1.20), OT fits
   B33' at Fn 0.2, B55' and the RAOs better (heave RAO 1.01 vs 1.21 at lambda/L 1.25). Passivity does: the D-form B_inf has
   an indefinite symmetric part (Wigley +-7.3e-4, prismatic body larger), i.e. the instantaneous force can feed energy into
   a combined heave-pitch motion at high frequency -- an artefact of the NK inconsistency that a time-domain plant must not
   integrate; the OT B_inf is exactly antisymmetric. Recommendation for the plant until the double-body basis exists: use
   the OT kernels (`form="OT"` in interp_slice) for time-domain simulation, keep the D-form for frequency-domain
   comparisons; both are in every table (10.6). [R: the basis was incomplete -- the OT B(omega) of the demo table is
   non-passive at omega' 13.9-40 (high frequency, outside the validity band), no coupled time-domain test existed, and the
   D-form was chosen as primary after seeing V2 data. The recommendation is now based on the free-decay test CD (10.8.6,
   10.9).]
4. **Validation (first forward-speed comparison of Track B; secondary transcriptions of Journee 1992):** 52/65 points
   within max(20 %, 10 % of the series maximum). Excitation 12/12 (heave and pitch within 0-5 % except one point at 23 %,
   inside the floor), RAOs 10/12, heave added mass 13/14 (the failing point is the pre-flagged outlier [R: withdrawn: the
   flag was set with the model value known and the point may be physical; V2 A33' was selection data, so the Fn 0.2 A33'
   result is 6/7, 10.8.7-8]), heave damping
   10/14 (low by 22-37 % at omega' 1.66-2.2 and 5.5 at Fn 0.3; Kara's transient-Green-function NK code shows the same low-
   frequency deficit in the same figure), pitch added mass 4/7 and pitch damping 3/6 (low by 15-31 %). No parameter was
   tuned (V2). The pattern (heave good, pitch coefficients low, low-frequency heave damping low at Fn 0.3) is the known
   weakness of the uniform-stream linearisation and points to the double-body basis (R3).
5. **Low-speed end (U0c FAIL).** The coefficients approach the U = 0 values as O(U^2), but at Fn 0.05 B55' is already 11 %
   off (5 % registered): on a fixed grid the slow-speed wave systems are unresolved. Tables must start at a speed where
   lambda0 = 2 pi U^2/g spans >= 15-20 cells, or use the U = 0 solution below it with the measured O(U^2) error (2.8 % at Fn
   0.025, 11 % at 0.05 on this grid). [R: the explanation is a hypothesis (no refinement was run) and the recommendation
   is withdrawn until the refinement rows U0cr (10.9) are evaluated.]
6. **Energy (SP U = 0 FAIL, expected).** Without any beach at U = 0 the semi-discrete system is not exactly energy-neutral:
   the collocation Dirichlet-to-Neumann map is not symmetric, so half the eigenvalues grow at up to 3.3e-4 sqrt(g/L)
   (about 4 % per 100 s at the 5.4 m USV, acceptance 0.1 %); with forward speed or a beach every mode decays. The tables are
   computed in the frequency domain (unaffected); the remedy for a beachless zero-speed time-domain use is a symmetric
   (Galerkin) or symmetrised Dirichlet-to-Neumann map. At forward speed the energy residual (-1.2 % at the end of the
   forcing, -5.5 % over the record) is numerical absorption by the upwind d/dx of unresolved short waves, never creation.
7. **Kramers-Kronig, D-form at Fn 0.3 (FAIL by 0.06 %):** the largest error sits at omega' = 1, next to the critical
   frequency tau = 1/4 (omega' 0.83) where B varies fastest; OT passes (1.49 %), Fn 0.2 passes (0.46 %). A denser frequency
   band around tau = 1/4 is the obvious fix; reported as registered. [R: wrong diagnosis, withdrawn: the error was an
   evaluation bug of the [0, omega_0] quadrature term, (2/pi)(B(wt) - B_inf) w0/wt^2, which peaks at omega' = 1 because of
   its 1/wt^2 shape; corrected values 0.03-0.06 %, 10.8.3.]
8. **Steady limit vs B4 (information).** The omega = 0 limit of this system gives Fz and My within 0.7 % and 1-8 % of the B4
   solver but C_near 13-15 % higher; its own near/far gap shrinks under refinement (+7.1 % -> +4.9 %) and B4's free-surface
   series was itself not converged (H11b). Two discretisations of the same steady NK problem do not yet agree in
   resistance; this matters for the steady tables (B4), not for the radiation coefficients (GS converged to 1.5 %) [R:
   with the B5 wall rule the coefficients converged at first order (true order about 1.1, Richardson error at M 60 about
   5 %), 10.8.2].
9. **C0 vs the steady derivative (information).** The radiation problem's static stiffness has no heave term (uniform-
   stream m_3 = 0) while the steady force of the same NK model changes with sinkage by -0.021 (rho = 1, L = 1; 3 % of
   the hydrostatic C33 = rho g A_w = 0.68): in the plant's form this term comes from F_steady(U, q), which is the reason the plant must not add
   C0 (10.6). [R: withdrawn: C0 is the static stiffness of the system whose kernels the plant integrates, and it differs
   from the steady-force slope; the B5 law mixed two linear models. The plant law now adds C0 about the slice attitude and
   evaluates F_steady only there; the difference is a binding consistency row (C0B), 10.8.5.]
10. **Transcription caveats.** The J. Mar. Sci. Eng. markers sit up to 0.07 off Journee's test frequencies, the Fn 0.2
   A33'(1.68) point is about twice its neighbours and the pitch RAO point at lambda/L 1.75 breaks its neighbours' trend;
   the original report would settle these (not retrievable; not indispensable).

### 10.6 Radiation-table interface for the any-speed plant (`hydro/rankine/tables.py`)

**Force law consumed by the plant (B5 review, 10.8.5; replaces the B5 law)** (per speed U and slice attitude qbar =
(sinkage, trim); symmetric modes surge, heave, pitch; x forward, z up, pitch right-handed about +y = bow DOWN positive;
moments about the table's reference point, body frame, carried with the attitude):

    F(t) = -A_inf(U, qbar) a(t) - B_inf(U, qbar) v(t) - int_0^t K(t - s; U, qbar) v(s) ds - C0(U, qbar) (q(t) - qbar)
           + F_steady(U, qbar)
           (+ hydrostatics and Froude-Krylov integrated exactly on the instantaneous wetted surface, plant side)

This is the radiation solver's LTI system about the slice attitude exactly as verified (CU through this law, both
forms), plus the steady force at the slice attitude only. K is the integrable kernel K~ of 10.2 item 5 (windowed above the
zero-speed 4-cell frequency, 10.8.6); A_inf, B_inf, K and C0 must be taken from ONE force form (`interp_slice(...,
form=...)`). The plant chooses qbar (e.g. the free-equilibrium attitude of the current speed from the B4 tables) and
re-slices when U or qbar change (kernel frozen at the slice attitude -- the approximation that design step C measures).
[R: the B5 text said that C0 "must NOT be added" because F_steady evaluated at the instantaneous q supplies it; that is
withdrawn: in the uniform-stream linearisation C0 differs from the steady-force slope (check C0B), so the B5 law mixed two
linear models, and its stiffness was not the one the kernels were built with.] C_inf (reference only) is stored for both
forms.

**Functions** (all SI units; omega in rad/s, t in s):

| Function | Signature | Purpose |
|---|---|---|
| build | `build_radiation_table(hull, U_list, sink_list, trim_list, omega, t, grid_kw, hull_mesh, rho=1025, g=9.81, ref=(0, 0), device=None) -> RadiationTable` | `node_slice` at every (U, sink, trim): hull panelled at the attitude, free-surface grid `build_grid(hull, sink, trim, sign(U), **grid_kw)` (U = 0: symmetric extents; U < 0: astern, mirrored grid), wall-trace closure, radiation at every omega, A_inf/B_inf/C_inf (limit of the discrete system), C0 (omega = 0), windowed kernels of both forms, validity band and kernel window per node, passivity flags, KK distortion of the window |
| node | `node_slice(hull, U, sink, trim, omega, t, grid_kw, hull_mesh, ...) -> dict` | all table quantities of one node (also used by the direct mid-box check PVm) |
| solver | `build_solver(hull, U, sink, trim, grid_kw, hull_mesh, ...) -> (solver, Coefficients, grid)` | one LTI system with the production closure |
| store | `save_table(tab, path) -> sha256` | npz + JSON sidecar (metadata, sha256); refuses to overwrite (N3) |
| load | `load_table(path) -> RadiationTable` | verifies the sha256 |
| path | `table_path(root, hull_name, physics_hash, label)` | `studies/_cache/rankine/<hull>@<physics hash>/<label>.npz` (design 8.5) |
| slice | `interp_slice(tab, U, sink, trim, form="D") -> dict(A_inf, B_inf, C0, C_inf, t, K, form)` | multilinear in (U, sink, trim) inside the box; ValueError outside (no extrapolation); one form ("D" or "OT"; OT refused for pre-review tables without C_inf_ot) |
| force | `RadiationForce(slice, dt, stiffness=True)(a, v, x) -> F_rad` | the force law above without F_steady: -A_inf a - B_inf v - int K v ds - C0 x, x = q - qbar (trapezoidal convolution on the plant's step) |
| decay | `free_decay(slice, mass, C_hs, x0, dt, t_end, modes=(1, 2)) -> dict(t, x, v, E)` | reference integration of the law with hydrostatics (check CD) |
| kernel | `memory_kernel(omega, B, B_inf, t, taper=None) -> (K, tail)` | cosine transform with PCHIP interpolation, optional cos^2 window |
| KK | `kk_added_mass(omega, B, B_inf, A_inf, C0, wq, taper=None)` | A(omega) of a (windowed) kernel by Kramers-Kronig (window distortion) |
| stiffness | `static_stiffness(co) -> (C0_D, C0_OT)` | omega = 0 solve with the displacement (m-term) body condition |
| band | `validity_band(G, U, g, L) -> (omega_lo, omega_hi)`, `kernel_window_start(G, U, g)`, `along_track_wavenumbers(omega, U, g)` | dispersion-aware validity band and kernel window (EMPIRICAL_REGISTER_RANKINE.md, B5 review) |

**Storage format** (`RadiationTable`, version `rankine-radiation-2`): `U (nU,)`, `sink (ns,)` [m, > 0 down], `trim (nt,)`
[rad, > 0 bow up, hull.py convention], `omega (nw,)`, `A, B (nU, ns, nt, nw, 3, 3)` (D-form), `A_inf, B_inf, C0, C_inf
(nU, ns, nt, 3, 3)`, `t (nk,)`, `K (nU, ns, nt, nk, 3, 3)` (windowed), OT arrays `A_ot, B_ot, B_inf_ot, C0_ot, C_inf_ot,
K_ot`, per node `omega_lo, omega_hi, omega_t, passive, passive_ot, bsym_min, bsym_min_ot, taper_dA, taper_dA_ot (nU, ns,
nt)`; JSON: hull name, L, rho, g, ref, modes, version, creation time, grid and hull-mesh settings, force law, forms,
kernel window, wall closure, conventions, kernel tail, npz sha256.

**Recommended grids for the plant hull** (to be produced in step R3 once the hull offsets are wired in; not run in B5):
U from -3 kn (astern, mirrored grid) through 0 to the upper end of the solver's verified range, about 20 speeds, denser
where the steady flow changes fastest (Fn_L 0.25-0.5) and dense enough that the mid-box kernel check PVm passes; sinkage
and trim 3 x 3 around the free-equilibrium attitude of each speed (B4 `free_equilibrium`), e.g. +-0.3 % L and +-1 deg;
40-60 frequencies log-spaced in omega' 0.05-40 plus 20 in a band +-30 % around the critical frequency tau = U w/g = 1/4;
kernel times to t' = 30-60; a free-surface grid fine enough that the validity band covers the encounter frequencies of
interest (the dispersion-aware omega_hi; on M = 30 it is empty at Fn 0.2). Cost on this machine at M = 40 (NF ~ 5k,
~1.5 s per frequency): ~1.5 min per (U, q) node, ~4 GPU-hours for 20 x 9 nodes (GPU-bound; dense LU). [R: no planing-type
or flared hull until a flared-waterline check converges, 10.8.1.]

**Which form the plant should integrate.** Decided by the free-decay check CD (10.8.6) and recorded in 10.10 with the
re-run results; the passivity flags `passive`, `passive_ot`, `bsym_min`, `bsym_min_ot` are information. [R: the B5
recommendation rested on "OT non-passive only mildly at low frequency", which is wrong (OT is non-passive at omega'
13.9-40 in the B5 demo table), and on no coupled time-domain test.]

**Demo table** (prismatic V body, check PV) -- **interface demo only, free-surface operator unverified for this
geometry** (10.8.1): rebuilt in the re-run with the review code (10.9; the B5 file
`studies/_cache/rankine/prismV_L1_b0.111111_beta20_D0.035@7f1e87f5d45e/full_20261006T220402.npz`, sha256
`7f194ab7...6d11`, stays for the record but is not a deliverable), (Fn 0.3, 0.5) x (sink 0, 0.004 L) x (trim -0.01, 0
rad), 40 frequencies omega' 0.05-40, kernels to t' = 30, non-dimensional (L = 1, rho = 1, g = 9.81). The prismatic body
has no data; [R: the B5 statement "its results are verified by the same identities (TNp, SP)" is withdrawn -- TNp (b)
fails and cannot converge at its flared oblique waterline (10.8.1), and the coefficients do not converge on refinement
(B5R-E5)]; planing speeds are outside the verified range of the NK model.

### 10.7 Next step and estimate (W4)

1. **Double-body basis (design R3, first part):** m-terms and free-surface conditions from the rigid-lid double-body flow
   (W.n = 0, so Tuck's theorem holds and the D and OT forms coincide up to waterline terms; expected to cure the D-form
   non-passivity and the NK inconsistency); same LTI machinery; re-run all B5 rows. 1-1.5 days; bottleneck: second
   derivatives of the double-body flow at the hull (the B4 H4 reference showed they are noisy next to the hull) and the
   waterline treatment.
2. **Resolution at low speed and high frequency:** FFT-accelerated free-surface products on a uniform core grid (B3
   machinery) to reach dx ~ lambda0/20 at Fn 0.1-0.2 and the high-frequency short waves; then a U0c re-check. 1 day;
   bottleneck: preconditioning of the complex system.
3. **Plant tables (R3, second part):** the scarab hull at the speeds and attitudes of 10.6 with the free-equilibrium
   attitudes from B4, OT kernels for time-domain use; about 4 GPU-hours at M = 40 (dense LU bound), plus the transom
   question (the plant hull has a transom: the B4 transom branches are not verified, so a radiation table for it is not
   verified either until that is resolved).
4. **Timman-Newman (b) and KK (D, Fn 0.3):** second-order waterline extrapolation and a denser band around tau = 1/4
   (1-2 h, no new physics).
[R: item 4 is withdrawn -- the KK failure was an evaluation bug and TN (b) is addressed by the wall closure and the
corner analysis (10.8); the next steps after the review are in 10.11.]

### 10.8 Review of B5 (2026-10-07): fixes and registration of the changed and new rows (written before the re-run; V1)

The review found one blocker, six major and six minor issues. This section records, per issue, what was verified,
what changed, the derivations, and registers every changed or new row (table 10.8.9) before the re-run of both tiers.
Section 10.1 stays as registered; where 10.8 differs, 10.8 governs. The B5 tier records are kept as
`b5_prereview_unsteady_verify_{quick,full,full_PV}.{json,log}`; the re-run writes `unsteady_verify_{quick,full}.*`.
Exploration runs of the review (scratch scripts, method choices, not verification rows) are B5R-E1 ... E10 (10.8.10);
"values known" in 10.8.9 refers to them and to smoke runs on tiny non-registered grids (all code paths).

#### 10.8.1 Timman-Newman on the prismatic body (blocker): corrected corner geometry, corner type, localisation

- **Geometry (reviewer correct, verified).** prismatic_v has h = (z + D)/tan(beta) below the chine and the chine is
  above the water, so the hull leaves the waterline inward and downward at beta = 20 deg below the horizontal: the hull
  wedge is 20 deg and the FLUID corner 180 - beta = 160 deg, convex (a wall-sided hull: 90 deg). The B5 reason for making
  TNp (b) information ("re-entrant, 180 + deadrise = 200 deg, phi_x ~ r^-0.1") is withdrawn; TNp (b) is restored as a
  binding row (3 %) and is reported against it.
- **Corner type depends on the free-surface condition (derivation).** At a waterline point let s run along the waterline,
  n be the horizontal normal into the free surface and gamma the angle of the waterline to the x axis (tan gamma = w');
  d/dx = cos(gamma) d/ds - sin(gamma) d/dn. For a local solution phi ~ r^lambda f(theta) in the (n, z) plane, g phi_z ~
  r^(lambda-1) while the uniform-stream term U^2 sin^2(gamma) phi_nn of (i w - U d_x)^2 phi ~ r^(lambda-2) dominates
  wherever U sin(gamma) != 0. The leading-order free-surface condition is then phi_nn = 0 on theta = 0, i.e. f(0) = 0 unless
  lambda is 0 or 1 (the constant and the uniform-flow particular solution): the free surface acts like a DIRICHLET boundary
  next to an oblique waterline. With the Neumann hull, lambda_n = (n + 1/2) pi/alpha; for alpha = 160 deg lambda_1 = 9/16,
  so grad phi ~ r^(-7/16) on the hull side (the singular part and all its derivatives along the free surface vanish on
  theta = 0, so the free-surface-side trace of phi_x stays finite). Where the waterline is parallel to the stream (gamma =
  0, the prismatic middle part) the free surface is Neumann-like at leading order and lambda = pi/alpha = 9/8 (bounded
  gradient; the reviewer's statement holds there). A wall-sided waterline (alpha = 90 deg) gives lambda = 1 or 2 in both
  cases: regular. The prismatic waterline is oblique over both tapers (w' up to 0.43 at the bow, 0.64 at the stern).
  In a double-body linearisation the advection is tangential to the hull and the corner stays Neumann-like.
- **Evidence (B5R-E6).** Hull refined in z at a fixed free-surface grid (CHECK, M = 40, Fn 0.4, heave, omega' 2.212):
  abs(phi_x) on the top hull row at x = -0.42 (stern taper) 13.7, 18.4, 21.4, 25.7 for top-row distances 1.7e-3,
  4.2e-4, 1.1e-4, 2.7e-5 L (still growing; the free surface is resolved only to 0.0125 L, so the continuum exponent is not
  reached); at x = +0.41 (bow taper) 5.8, 7.2, 8.1, 9.2; at x = -0.10 (parallel part) 0.566, 0.574, 0.576, 0.577
  (bounded); on the wall-sided variant prismatic_v(D0 = 0.06) bounded everywhere (x = -0.42: 3.71, 4.13, 4.11, 4.16).
- **Consequence for (b).** The waterline reduction uses hull-side traces of phi and phi_x; at an oblique flared waterline
  the phi_x trace grows with hull refinement, so (b) cannot converge for this geometry in the uniform-stream model -- the
  reviewer's 3.12 (M 40, hull 40x8), 3.50 (M 60), 4.09 (M 60, hull 60x12) are this growth. It is a property of the NK
  linearisation at flared oblique waterlines, not a defect of the identity, but it means the free-surface operator on the
  planing-type body has NO passing physics-bearing check (statement of 10.6 "verified by the same identities (TNp, SP)"
  withdrawn).
- **Localisation (B5R-E1; B5 wall rule, M 40, hull 40x8, omega' 2.212).** The discrete free-surface Green sum was split
  by rows of the defect operator Delta = W L_-^2 - (L_+^2)^T W (W the panel areas, L_+- = i w + nu -+ U D_+-; the
  per-row sums reproduce the free-surface sum exactly): 97 % of it lies in the hull columns and 93 % in band lines 0-2.
  Column by column the waterline integrand (hull traces) exceeds the discrete defect by factors 3-20 along both tapers,
  largest at the pointed-end stations (stern column x = -0.487, w' 0.61, pair (53): waterline 2.3e-2 vs defect 1.3e-3),
  and both vanish in the parallel middle (w' = 0). The residual sits at the pointed ends and in the large-w' band grid,
  and is carried by the hull-side extrapolation of phi_x (above).
- **Rows.** TNp (b) binding 3 %; TNp at M 60 with hull 40x8 and 60x12 (TNpr, TNpr2): information; TNw: the same
  identities on the wall-sided planing-type variant prismatic_v(D0 = 0.06) (waterline on the vertical side above the
  submerged chine, regular 90 deg corner, still fore-aft asymmetric): binding 3 % for (b) and for the Green identity.
- **Demo table.** "Interface demo only, free-surface operator unverified for this geometry" (PV row text, 10.6). No
  tables for planing-type or flared hulls are recommended until a check that converges on a flared oblique waterline
  exists; the prismatic coefficient series does not converge with either wall closure (B5R-E5: A33' 4.04/4.04/4.03 with
  the B5 rule vs 5.25/5.23/5.04 with the trace closure at M 30/40/60; B55' 0.41/0.44/0.48 vs 0.141/0.072/-0.004).

#### 10.8.2 Hull-wall closure of d/dx (major): consistent trace closure replaces the B5 wall rule

- **Verified.** The B5 rule (cross term dropped on the first band line, central on the second) computes the derivative
  ALONG the waterline there; the error w' f_y is O(1) on a line of width O(dy): manufactured field (B5R-E4, a = b = 2 pi/
  0.25 L) wall-line error 10.1/9.8/9.5 % of a (Wigley I, M 20/40/80) and 20-37 % (prismatic), not decreasing (reviewer:
  2.6/2.5/2.4 % with his wavenumbers). The global effect is first order. grid_series used log 1.5 for both steps of the
  M 40/60/80 series (true ratios 1.5 and 4/3): the reported "median order 1.8" corresponds to about 1.1 (reviewer
  correct; for p = 1 the formula gives log 2/log 1.5 = 1.71). Fixed: observed_order solves (h1^p - h2^p)/(h2^p - h3^p)
  = d1/d2 and Richardson errors of the PROD member are reported (rows GSo).
- **Closure (derivation).** In the afterbody (U > 0; forebody for U < 0) the fixed-y characteristics of the uniform
  stream LEAVE the hull, so the wall is an inflow boundary of d/dx and the inflow datum is the trace of the field on the
  wall. One wall node per such hull column at band index j = -1/2 (on the waterline); band lines 0 and 1 use Lagrange
  weights on {-1/2, 0, 1} (second order, upwind-biased) and {-1/2, 0, 1, 2}; D f = D0 f + C f_w (C: N x n_w); coordinates
  are differentiated with the same stencils and the exact wall-node coordinates (linear functions exact). Traces: phi_w =
  E phi_B (hull solution, linear extrapolation in z from the top two rows, linear interpolation in x); zeta_w from the
  dynamic condition at the contact line, zeta_w = -(1/g)(phi_t,w - U phi_x,w) (nu = 0 there), with phi_x,w from the
  trace and the body condition: on a wall-sided waterline the body condition gives phi_y = w' phi_x + Q phi_n (Q =
  abs((-H_x, 1, -H_z))) and d phi_w/dx = phi_x + w' phi_y, so phi_x = (d phi_w/dx - w' Q phi_n)/(1 + w'^2) exactly; at a
  flared waterline the term w' H_z phi_z/(1 + w'^2) is omitted -- it is the singular term of 10.8.1, and no consistent
  closure exists there in the uniform-stream model. Frequency domain (exact elimination, `ForwardSpeedLTI.set_operator`):
  sigma = (1/g)[-w^2 phi + 2 i w N phi + N0 N phi + U^2 C phi_x,w], N phi = N0 phi - U C E phi_B, phi_B = P sigma + Q
  phi_n (P = -Bi S_BF, Q = -Bi S_BB): the same matrix structure with P1 = N0 R_s - U C (E P), P2 = N0 P1 + U^2 C (Tw E P)
  (rank-n_w updates) and extra right-hand-side terms. Time domain: phi' = -N0 phi + U C phi_w - g zeta, zeta' = -N0 zeta +
  U C zeta_w - sigma, phi_t,w from sigma_t (two extra LU solves per stage); spectrum from the corresponding state matrix.
  High-frequency limit: the O(w^2) balance gives phi_F0 = 2 U C E phi_inf[n_k] (zero in the continuum, where the hull trace
  of the phi = 0 solution vanishes; a discretisation term otherwise), so B_inf of both forms is evaluated from the problem
  with that free-surface potential (`Coefficients.instantaneous`); without this, B(w) tends to a value 3 % of abs(B_inf)
  off the phi_F = 0 value and the Cummins check failed by 3 % (B5R-E10). C_inf (reference only) omits the next-order
  term. The OT B_inf is then antisymmetric only up to this discretisation term.
- **Evidence.** Algebra: frequency system vs state matrix and the forced solution vs the kinematic condition agree to
  1e-14. Manufactured wall-line error converges (B5R-E4): Wigley 9.6e-2, 1.24e-2, 3.4e-3 of a at M 20/40/80 (orders 2.95,
  1.86), prismatic orders 2.5-2.7 and 1.25-1.40; band and interior as before. Spectrum (SMALL): every mode decays --
  Wigley Fn 0.2/0.3/1.0 max Re(lambda) -8.3e-3/-1.33e-2/-0.354, prismatic Fn 0.3/0.4/0.5 -0.0186/-0.0348/-0.074,
  wall-sided variant Fn 0.4 -0.0349 sqrt(g/L). Wigley TN (b) on CHECK M 40/60: 6.8/4.1 % (B5 rule 9.9/6.1 %), Green
  identity 0.57/0.34 %. Wigley grid series (Fn 0.2, M 40/60/80, omega' 1.659, 2.765, 4.424; B5R-E7): Richardson error of
  the M = 60 member <= 0.73 % for the diagonal A', B' (B5 rule up to 2.2 %), observed orders mostly >= 2, both closures
  converge to the same limits within 0.3 %. A variant taking phi_x,w from the hull's structured gradient (B5R-E2) was
  equivalent on the Wigley but erratic on the flared body (T(U) - T(-U) changed by factors 2-4 between grids), hence the
  body-condition form. With the closure: KK 0.03-0.06 % (SMALL, both forms, Fn 0.2/0.3), CU 0.35 % (D) / 0.10 % (OT), TD
  0.66 % (B5R-E10).

#### 10.8.3 Kramers-Kronig evaluation bug (major)

Confirmed: `_kk_errors` wrote the [0, w0] contribution as (f[0] - ft) log abs((w0 - wt)/(w0 + wt))/(2 wt) while I1, I2
already cover f - ft and ft on [w0, w_end]; the extra -ft int_0^w0 dw/(w^2 - wt^2) is (2/pi)(B(wt) - B_inf) w0/wt^2 in A.
On an analytic causal kernel (B5R-E3) the code's error equals that term to 1e-8 relative; with I0 = f[0] log abs(...)/
(2 wt) the error is 2e-6 (160 points) / 1.7e-7 (400 points). With the fix (and the trace closure) the SMALL-grid Wigley
gives Fn 0.3: D 3.0e-4, OT 5.1e-4; Fn 0.2: D 2.9e-4, OT 6.1e-4 (reviewer: 0.038/0.042/0.030/0.060 % with the B5
operator). The B5 registered FAIL (D-form Fn 0.3, 2.06 %) stays in the record; the re-run row is marked "evaluation bug,
corrected after the run". The explanation of 10.5 item 7 (error next to tau = 1/4) and the remedy of 10.7 item 4 (denser
band) are withdrawn. New row KKu: the analytic-kernel test of `_kk_errors` in both tiers.

#### 10.8.4 Green-identity rows relabelled; independent forward-speed reference (major)

- **Verified.** Form (a) of HK, TN, TNp is Green's second identity for two solutions of the discrete BIE representation:
  it holds for any free-surface operator (reviewer's demonstration with D = 0: (a) unchanged, (b) 18 % -> 83 %). These
  rows are relabelled GI ("discrete Green identity of the BIE representation, stage-1 type") and are no longer counted as
  M3 Haskind or Timman-Newman passes. At registration M3 Timman-Newman at forward speed for surface-piercing hulls has
  no passing check (B5: TN (b) 9.9 %, TNp (b) 312 %); the forward-speed operator is exercised by TNs, HK (b), TN (b),
  TNp (b), TNw (b) and WE.
- **Reference data.** Wu & Eatock Taylor (1988, Proc. R. Soc. A 417:433) could not be retrieved (bot challenge; not
  bought); its tables are reproduced in the "Analytical results [27]" columns of Mei, Candries, Lataire & Zou (2020),
  J. Mar. Sci. Eng. 8:106 (CC BY 4.0), tables 3 (surge), 5 (heave), 6 (couplings) for a sphere of radius r at centre
  depth 2r, Fr = U/sqrt(g r) = 0.4, k_e r = 0.6-5.0 (k_e = omega_e^2/g, all above the critical 0.3906), and tables 1-2
  (exciting forces). Transcribed from the PDF text layer: `data/external/wu_eatock_taylor_sphere/`.
- **Reference model (identified from the tables, before any comparison at that model).** (i) A11 decays toward the
  phi = 0 limit (image estimate 0.667 (1 - 3/128) = 0.651 in units of pi rho r^3) as 0.35/(k_e r): A - A_inf ~
  1/omega^2, which in a causal system with zero static stiffness (translations) needs a C_inf/omega^2 term; (ii) B13 ->
  -0.0033 (constant) at high frequency: a nonzero instantaneous damping between translations. Both are impossible with
  uniform-stream m-terms (zero for translations) and follow from the m-terms of the steady flow past the sphere with the
  m-term force (Mei et al. state that their ref. [10], which reproduces [27] to four digits, evaluates the m-terms from
  the steady potential). Reference model: NK free-surface condition + m-terms of the steady (double-body) flow + m-term
  (Ogilvie-Tuck) force; the steady wave potential is negligible at depth 2r (k_s h = 12.5). Implementation for the check:
  `sphere_db_mterms` (Phi = -U x'(1 + r^3/(2 R^3)), W = grad Phi, m_j = -(n.grad) W_j, from the linearised exact body
  condition on the displaced sphere); Tuck's identity int n_j W.grad f = -int m_j f holds with these m-terms to 0.6-0.9 %
  on a 48 x 48 sphere mesh (harmonic f = xz, x^2 - z^2; the residual is the panel-centroid W.n). Disclosure: the
  uniform-stream model was run at the chosen grid during the grid choice (B5R-E9, A11 = 0.625-0.632 nearly constant over
  k_e r) with the reference values known; the reference-model variant was not run before registration.
- **Grid (B5R-E9, self-convergence only).** Sphere 24 x 12 panels (half), r = 0.1 (grid length unit 1), free surface
  M = 8 columns over the sphere (dx = r/4), up 10 r, down 60 r (uniform 15 r, beach 30 r), lateral 40 r (uniform 5 r,
  beach 20 r), stretching 1.08/1.05/1.06, nu_max 1.5: M 8 vs 12 identical to four digits, domain x1.5 and beach x2
  <= 0.5 %, sphere mesh 32 x 16 changes A11 by +0.9 %.
- **Rows.** WE (binding): reference model, 13 k_e r: A11, A33 within 3 % of the value; B11, B33 within max(5 % of the
  value, 2 % of the series maximum); A13, A31, B13, B31 within max(5 % of the value, 5 % of the series maximum of the
  absolute value) (design-type values, written before the run). Information: the uniform-stream variant (WEnk) and the
  exciting-force amplitudes of both variants against the reference and against Mei et al.'s own NK column (WEx).

#### 10.8.5 Plant force law (major)

- **Verified.** CU checked F = RadiationForce - C0 x (C0 added, D-form) while 10.6 told the plant not to add C0 and to
  take the stiffness from F_steady(U, q(t)) with OT kernels; the C0 information row shows C0 != -dF_steady/dq (B5: (33) 0
  vs -2.13e-2; (55) D -1.91e-3, OT -4.63e-3 vs -3.99e-3). The B5 law mixed two different linear models and is withdrawn.
- **Chosen law (consistent).** F = -A_inf a - B_inf v - int K~ v ds - C0 (q - qbar) + F_steady(U, qbar): the LTI system
  of the radiation solver about the slice attitude qbar exactly as verified, plus the steady force at qbar only (the
  plant chooses qbar, e.g. the free-equilibrium attitude of the current speed, and re-slices when qbar or U change).
  A_inf, B_inf, K~ and C0 of ONE form. `RadiationForce(slice, dt, stiffness=True)(a, v, x)` with x = q - qbar.
- **Rows.** CU through this law for the D-form and the OT form (each against its own time-domain force), 1 % rms
  (values known 0.35 %/0.10 %, B5R-E10). C0B (binding, both forms): abs(C0_jk - (-dF_steady/dq)_jk) <= 5 % of the
  hydrostatic stiffness about the same point (diagonal C_jj; cross sqrt(C33 C55)), Wigley I Fn 0.3 (values known from the
  code-path run with the trace closure: D 6.2 % (55), OT 3.2 % (33) -- the D-form row is expected to FAIL). The C0B rows
  quantify how far the uniform-stream model's static stiffness differs from its steady-force slope; the law above does
  not use that slope dynamically.

#### 10.8.6 Validity band, kernel window, coupled time-domain test (major + minor)

- **Text corrected.** In the B5 demo table the OT form is non-passive only at omega' 13.9-40 (outside any validity band)
  and the D-form at omega' >= 6.9-8.7 as well; the statement "OT mildly at low frequency" (10.4, 10.5 item 3, 10.6) is
  withdrawn.
- **Validity band (minor 8; registered in EMPIRICAL_REGISTER_RANKINE.md).** Per node: omega_hi = the largest omega at
  which the shortest along-track wave max(k0, k2, k4 [tau < 1/4]) spans 8 hull-region cells, k2 = (g + 2 U w + sqrt(g^2
  + 4 g U w))/(2 U^2), k4 = (g - 2 U w + sqrt(g^2 - 4 g U w))/(2 U^2), k0 = w^2/g (derivation: (w -+ U k)^2 = g k along the
  track; k2 > k4 > k3 >= k0 >= k1 for tau < 1/4); omega_lo = the smallest omega at which the longest along-track wave k1
  (k0 at U = 0) fits the lateral beach. omega_hi = 0 when even the steady Kelvin wave 2 pi U^2/g is shorter than 8 cells.
- **Kernel window (deviation from the reviewer's "taper above omega_hi", with evidence).** Plant kernels are formed from
  B_inf + W (B - B_inf), W = cos^2 from omega_t to 2 omega_t with omega_t where the ZERO-SPEED wavelength spans 4 cells
  (above it even the zero-speed waves are unrepresentable). B5R-E8 (Wigley I Fn 0.2/0.3, prismatic Fn 0.3/0.5 nodes,
  M = 30 grids): without a window and with this window the free decay of the plant law is stable at every node in both
  forms and the KK distortion of the window in omega' 0.3-6 is <= 1.4 %; windowing above the dispersion-aware omega_hi
  (empty band at Wigley Fn 0.2 on M = 30) or above 4 cells of the k2 wave removes resolved damping near the heave-pitch
  natural frequencies at low Fn, distorts A(omega) by 6-20 % through Kramers-Kronig and made the plant law unstable at the
  prismatic Fn 0.3 node (energy x1e27). The arrays A, B stay unmodified; the KK distortion of the window is stored per
  node (taper_dA).
- **Coupled time-domain row CD (binding).** Free heave-pitch decay of the documented law with hydrostatics
  (`tables.free_decay`, symplectic Euler, dt' 0.02, horizon t' 200 ~ 150 s at the 5.4 m plant hull, x0 = (0.005 L,
  0.02 rad)) at every node of the demo table and of a Wigley I table (Fn 0.2, 0.3; zero attitude; same frequencies;
  M = 30, hull 40 x 8; CG at (0, z_G) with the assumed KG of V4), both forms: max E(t) <= 1.2 E(0) and max E over the last
  quarter <= max(max E over the third quarter, 1e-8 E(0)). The D-vs-OT recommendation is based on CD (and the passivity
  information), not on validation data.

#### 10.8.7 Validation (major)

V2 A33' (Fn 0.3) was used to choose the primary force form (B5-E2): it is now "used for force-form selection, not
validation" (information, both forms) and leaves the validation count. Binding rows for the OT form (V1o-V4o: A33', B33',
A55', B55' at Fn 0.2, B33' at Fn 0.3, excitation, RAOs) are registered here with the same per-point rule max(20 %, 10 % of
the series maximum); the B5 information rows had shown the OT ratios (values known). Both forms' pass counts are reported
side by side. No choice in this review is made on validation data; the Fn 0.2 pitch series (A55', B55') has not been used
for any choice.

#### 10.8.8 Minor items

- U0c: the explanation (slow-speed waves unresolved; at Fn 0.05 the k2/k4 waves span 0.5-0.8 cells at M = 40) is a
  hypothesis; rows U0cr (M 60) and U0cr2 (M 80) re-run U0c on finer grids (information). The recommendation to use the
  U = 0 solution below a threshold speed is withdrawn until then.
- Data caveats: the flag on A33'(1.68) at Fn 0.2 (10.1, "flagged in advance as a probable transcription outlier") was set
  AFTER B5-E8 had computed the model value at omega' 1.659; the point lies at tau = 0.33, near the critical tau = 1/4
  (omega' 1.25), and J. Mar. Sci. Eng. 9:87 reports a resonance near omega' 1.40 with large deviations there, so the value
  may be physical. The comment on the pitch RAO at lambda/L 1.75 was added after the run. Results are no longer described
  as "13/14 with the failure explained in advance".
- Normalisations: INF per diagonal entry and HK (b) relative to abs(X_diffraction) are reported as information next to the
  binding rows; `waterline_fs_term` extrapolates the integrand to the two waterline ends (w' one-sided) instead of setting
  it to 0.
- Interface: C_inf_ot is stored and returned for form "OT" (`interp_slice` refuses an OT slice from a pre-review table);
  PVm: the mid-box slice (Fn 0.4, sink 0.002 L, trim -0.005 rad) computed directly vs interpolated, both forms: A_inf
  within 1 % of max abs(A_inf) and the kernels within 10 % rms of the direct kernel (binding).

#### 10.8.11 Amendment 1 (2026-10-07, after the quick tier, before the full tier; no threshold relaxed, no row or job changed)

(i) Validity band, omega_lo (information only): the rule registered in 10.8.6 compared the longest along-track wave k1
with the LATERAL beach, but the waves reaching the lateral beach are the transverse ones (k = k0 at 90 deg to the track);
k1 travels downstream and k3 upstream (tau < 1/4 only). Corrected: omega_lo is the smallest omega at which the lateral
beach >= 2 pi/k0, the downstream beach >= 2 pi/k1 and, for tau < 1/4, the upstream beach >= 2 pi/k3. (The quick tier
reported omega_lo' = 3.3 at Fn 0.3 on the M = 20 demo grid, an empty band produced by the inconsistent rule.)
(ii) CD criterion tightened: the symmetric part of C_hs + C0 must be positive definite (static stability). In the quick
tier one D-form node of the demo table (M = 20, Fn 0.5, sinkage 0.004 L, trim -0.01 rad; D-form C0_55 = -0.110 against
the hydrostatic 0.078) has min eig sym(C_hs + C0) < 0: the trajectory diverges and overflowed near the end of the
horizon while the energy measure -- not a norm with an indefinite stiffness -- stayed at E(0); the row failed through the
finiteness test, but a slower divergence could have passed the energy rules. The added condition can only add failures.
(iii) Performance only: `RadiationForce` keeps the velocity history in an array (the quick tier used 591 s of its 600 s;
the demo-table job 254 s, mostly the free-decay convolutions).
The quick-tier record (registration 04:35:22 UTC) stands as run; the full tier runs with this amendment.

**Timman-Newman (b) on the planing-type bodies: where the waterline integral comes from (B5R-E11, after the run).**
With the hull traces, 87 % of the waterline integral of TNw (pair 13, omega' 2.212) accumulates over the stern run x <
-0.34 L (w' 0.34-0.74; 44 % over x < -0.453 L alone) and its magnitude is 2.0 x that of the free-surface sum; on the Wigley (w' <=
0.24) the integral is spread along the hull and agrees with the free-surface sum within 7 %. The flared-corner analysis
(10.8.1) explains the growth of TNp (b) under hull refinement (494 -> 575 %), but TNw shows that the blunt pointed plan
form (waterline half-angles 26 and 36 deg at bow and stern; the Wigley 13.5 deg) alone already defeats the waterline
reduction at these grids -- consistent with B5R-E1, which put the largest residual at the pointed-end stations.

### 10.10 Findings after the review (replace the conflicting statements of 10.4-10.7, which are marked [R: ...] in place)

1. **Free-surface operator at forward speed: now consistent at the hull wall for wall-sided waterlines, and checked
   against an exact solution.** The trace closure is consistent (manufactured wall-line error second order on the
   Wigley, first-to-second order on the prismatic grid) and stable (every mode decays), and the Wigley coefficients now
   converge at about second order (median 1.8 with the actual ratios, Richardson error of PROD <= 0.93 % for the
   diagonal coefficients; B5: first order, about 5 %). The submerged sphere reproduces the exact forward-speed solution
   of Wu & Eatock Taylor for the added masses (0.6-1.9 %, including the strong rise near the critical frequency), the
   heave damping and the damping couplings, but the registered row FAILS: surge damping B11 is 7-9 % low over k_e r
   0.8-2.5 and the small couplings A13/A31 deviate by 0.002-0.003 near the critical frequency. The identification of
   the reference model (steady-flow m-terms) was necessary: with uniform-stream m-terms the sphere misses the reference
   by factors (A11 flat at 0.63 against 1.24-0.72).
2. **Timman-Newman at forward speed for surface-piercing hulls: still no passing check.** TN (b) on the Wigley 6.7 % at
   M 40 and 4.0 % at M 60 (registered 3 %; B5 9.9/6.1 %), converging; TNp (b) on the flared prismatic body 494-575 %,
   growing with hull refinement (oblique flared waterline: Dirichlet-like corner of the uniform-stream condition,
   10.8.1); TNw (b) on the wall-sided planing variant 122 %, i.e. the blunt pointed plan form alone defeats the waterline
   reduction (87 % of its integral comes from the stern run). Haskind (b) passes (1.6 %). The Green-identity rows (0.1-1 %)
   are not M3 checks.
3. **Planing-type and flared hulls are NOT verified.** The free-surface operator has no converging physics-bearing
   check on the planing-type plan form (TNp, TNw), and its coefficients do not converge on refinement for the flared body
   (B5R-E5). No planing or flared-hull tables are recommended; the demo table is an interface demo only. In the
   uniform-stream basis an oblique flared waterline has a singular corner, so the double-body basis (tangential
   advection, Neumann-like corner) is a prerequisite for planing hulls, not only an accuracy improvement.
4. **Kramers-Kronig holds to 0.03-0.06 %** for both forms at Fn 0.2 and 0.3 once the B5 evaluation bug is removed; the
   B5 tau = 1/4 diagnosis was wrong.
5. **Plant law.** The consistent law F = -A_inf a - B_inf v - int K v ds - C0 (q - qbar) + F_steady(U, qbar) reproduces
   the time-domain system through the table pipeline for both forms (CU 0.35 %/0.10 %). The radiation problem's static
   stiffness is not the steady-force slope of the same model: heave 3.2 % of the hydrostatic stiffness for both forms
   (C0_33 = 0 against -2.16e-2), pitch 6.2 % (D, FAIL) and 1.6 % (OT). The law therefore uses the steady force only at the
   slice attitude.
6. **Which form the plant should integrate: OT** (decided on the free-decay test, not on validation data). Both forms
   decay at every node of the Wigley table and of the M = 30 demo table; on the M = 20 quick-tier demo table the D-form is
   statically unstable at one planing-speed node (its static pitch stiffness -0.110 exceeds the hydrostatic 0.078) while
   OT decays everywhere; OT's C0 is also the one consistent with the steady-force slope (C0B). Its B(omega) is not
   passive at every frequency either (no node of the demo table is), so the recommendation is "OT kernels with the OT
   B_inf and C0, windowed above the zero-speed 4-cell frequency", not a passivity claim. Validation counts (information
   for this choice): D 45/58, OT 50/58.
7. **Kernel window and validity band.** Tapering above the dispersion-aware validity limit (the reviewer's proposal)
   would remove resolved damping, distort A(omega) by 6-20 % and destabilised the plant law at one node (B5R-E8); the
   kernels are windowed only above the zero-speed 4-cell frequency (KK distortion 0.6-2 %, one node 11.5 %). The
   dispersion-aware band is conservative: on the M = 30 tables it is empty at Fn 0.2 and 0.3, while the grid and domain
   series (GS, DOM) show converged coefficients there; finer table grids are needed for a resolution-verified band.
8. **Table interpolation in speed is too coarse at this spacing.** Between Fn 0.3 and 0.5 the linearly interpolated
   kernel misses the directly computed one by 61 % (D) / 35 % (OT) rms (A_inf 1.5 %, sinkage/trim only): plant tables need
   much denser speed nodes (or interpolation of B(omega) followed by a kernel transform) -- PVm is the check.
9. **Low-speed end (U0c FAIL).** The 11 % change of B55' at Fn 0.05 is grid-independent between M = 40 and 80 (11.06,
   11.12, 11.13 %) and O(U^2) (ratio 3.65). The hypothesis "slow-speed waves unresolved" is not confirmed: all three grids
   leave the k2/k4 waves at 0.5-1.7 cells, so they share the same unresolved regime; resolving them needs the FFT core
   grid. Until then no low-speed table limit is recommended.
10. **Steady limit vs B4 (information).** With the consistent wall closure the omega = 0 near-field resistance of the B5
   system dropped from 13-15 % to 7.6 % above B4's, lift and pitch moment agree within 0.7-1.4 %; part of the earlier
   discrepancy was the B5 wall rule.
11. **Data caveats (corrected):** the Fn 0.2 A33'(1.68) flag was set with the model value known and the point may be
   physical; the pitch RAO comment at lambda/L 1.75 was added after the run; V2 A33' is selection data.

### 10.11 Next step and estimate (W4; replaces 10.7)

1. **Double-body basis (design R3)** -- now also the prerequisite for planing and flared hulls (finding 3): m-terms and
   free-surface advection from the rigid-lid double-body flow; the trace closure carries over with the advection
   tangential at the wall; re-run all rows including WE (whose reference model already uses steady-flow m-terms),
   TNp and TNw. 1.5-2 days; bottleneck: second derivatives of the double-body flow at the hull and at the pointed ends.
2. **Pointed-end diagnosis for the waterline reduction** (TNw): per-station localisation with the trace-closure operator
   and a planing-type test body with rounded waterline ends; 0.5 day.
3. **WE residual** (B11 -8 %, A13/A31 near the critical frequency): sphere-mesh and free-surface refinement around the
   critical frequency, steady free-surface image in the m-terms; 0.5 day.
4. **FFT core grid** for low speed (U0c) and a resolution-verified validity band at Fn 0.2-0.3; 1 day; bottleneck:
   preconditioning of the complex system.
5. **Plant tables** only after 1-3, with speed nodes dense enough for PVm and OT kernels; about 4 GPU-hours at M = 40
   (dense LU) plus the unresolved transom question.

## 11. Stage B6 (2026-10-07): double-body (steady base flow) linearisation -- the prerequisite for planing hulls

Code: `hydro/rankine/doublebody.py` (new: base flow `DoubleBodyFlow`, surface calculus `surface_gradient2`,
`wgrad_operator_fv`, free-surface operators `fs_operators`, LTI system `DBForwardSpeedLTI`, coefficients
`DBCoefficients`, time domain `DBTimeDomain`, steady problem `steady_db`, `steady_grid`, `steady_cut`,
`db_free_equilibrium`, builder `build_db_solver`); `hydro/rankine/tables.py` (option `linearisation="DB"` in
`build_solver`, `node_slice`, `build_radiation_table`; `static_stiffness` dispatches to the double-body coefficients;
the NK path is unchanged). Checks: `studies/rankine_db_verify.py` (tiers quick / full, registration keys `b6:*` in
`registration_log.json`), `studies/rankine_db_kk.py` (Kramers-Kronig evaluation with a band-referenced entry skip).
Outputs: `studies/_cache/rankine/db_verify_{quick,full}.json/.log`. Nothing was installed; no git commit; WaveBEM was not
consulted for this step (it has no linear forward-speed solver).

### 11.1 Registration of the B6 checks (written before the tiers ran; V1)

Model of every row unless stated: the double-body linearisation of 11.2 -- base flow = rigid-lid double body at the mean
attitude, free-surface conditions linearised about it with the conservative kinematic operator K_U = Wa^-1 A_{-U}^T Wa
(11.2 item 3), Ogilvie-Tuck m-terms of the base flow as the area-weighted adjoint of the finite-volume W.grad operator
(11.2 item 6; Tuck's identity holds exactly, so the direct-pressure and m-term forces coincide: one force form), beaches on
all sides, the B5 grids and settings (GRID_BASE, PROD M 60 / hull 60 x 12, COARSE M 40, CHECK symmetric domain, SMALL;
stage note 10.1), the diffraction problem with the incident-wave/base-flow free-surface forcing (11.2 item 2). Thresholds
are the B5 / B4 values for the same quantities wherever a row has a B5 / B4 counterpart; nothing was relaxed. "Values
known" refers to the exploration runs B6-E1 ... E17 (11.3; scratch scripts, method choices) and to smoke runs of every job
on tiny non-registered grids.

### 11.2 Formulation and derivations (M1; written before the tiers ran)

Notation as 10.2: body frame, stream -U x_hat, fluid z < 0, n out of the body (into the fluid), e^{i w t}, beach nu(x, y)
>= 0, sigma = -phi_z the free-surface source strength of the representation (10.2 item 1). F denotes the free-surface
panels / the plane z = 0 outside the waterline C, nu_C the in-plane normal of C pointing out of F (into the hull).

1. **Base flow.** Phi = -U x + phi_db, the rigid-lid double-body potential: Laplace outside the hull and its mirror image
   in z = 0, dPhi/dn = 0 on the hull, Phi even in z. W = grad Phi. Properties used below: (a) W.n = 0 on S; (b) W_z = 0 on
   z = 0; (c) Phi_xz = Phi_yz = 0 on z = 0 (odd z-derivatives of an even function); (d) div_h W = Phi_xx + Phi_yy =
   -Phi_zz on z = 0; (e) **on C, W is tangent to C for every hull, flared or wall-sided**: the in-plane normal of C is the
   horizontal projection of the hull normal, n_h = n - n_z z_hat (up to normalisation; n_h is orthogonal to the tangent of
   C because both n and z_hat are), and W.n_h = W.n - W_z n_z = 0 by (a), (b). Property (e) is what removes every
   waterline term of B5. Computation: phi_db by the Green formulation of bie.py with the y and z images on the hull
   panels; W on the hull from the surface gradient (normal part zero by construction); W and Phi_zz at free-surface
   points from the representation by fourth-order differences (doublebody.DoubleBodyFlow).
2. **Linearised conditions.** Total potential Phi + phi, elevation eta, with phi, eta = O(eps) and W = O(1) (no smallness
   of phi_db assumed). Exact conditions on z = eta transferred to z = 0 with (b), (c) (so W.grad and |W|^2 have no
   z-derivative at z = 0, and Phi_z(eta) = Phi_zz eta):
       kinematic  eta_t + W.grad eta - Phi_zz eta = phi_z,   i.e.  eta_t + div_h(W eta) = phi_z     (by (d)),
       dynamic    phi_t + W.grad phi + g eta = (U^2 - |W|^2)/2,
   errors O(eps^2). Steady part (phi_s, eta_s): div_h(W eta_s) = phi_s,z, W.grad phi_s + g eta_s = f_0 := (U^2 -
   |W|^2)/2, combined
       g phi_s,z + div_h(W (W.grad phi_s)) = div_h(W f_0)                                                    (S-DB)
   (eta_s the FULL steady elevation; Dawson 1977 kept only derivatives along the double-body streamlines and dropped the
   stretching term Phi_zz eta; Raven 1996 showed that this is inconsistent at the same order; (S-DB) is the form that is
   reciprocal under U -> -U, item 4). Unsteady part (radiation, diffraction), interactions with the steady wave potential
   neglected (the double-body linearisation of Nakos & Sclavounos 1990 / Kring 1994), with the beach:
       zeta_t + div_h(W zeta) = phi_z - nu zeta + f_k,     phi_t + W.grad phi = -g zeta - nu phi + f_d,
   f = 0 for radiation; body condition dphi/dn = sum_k (xdot_k n_k + x_k m_k) with the Ogilvie-Tuck m-terms of W (item 6).
   Diffraction: the incident wave phi_I is the uniform-stream wave (it satisfies the conditions with W_0 = -U x_hat);
   requiring phi_I + phi_D to satisfy the double-body conditions gives the free-surface forcing f_d = -w.grad phi_I, f_k
   = -div_h(w zeta_I), w = W - W_0, zeta_I = -(1/g)(i w_e phi_I - U phi_I,x) the incident elevation, and dphi_D/dn =
   -dphi_I/dn on S. LTI form and its exact frequency response as 10.2 item 1 with N -> two operators, N_phi = diag(nu) +
   A (dynamic), N_zeta = diag(nu) + K (kinematic): [I - (1/g)(i w + N_zeta)(i w + N_phi) R_s] sigma = (1/g)(i w +
   N_zeta)(i w + N_phi) R_n phi_n + f_k - (1/g)(i w + N_zeta) f_d. The steady problem (S-DB) is the omega = 0 response of
   the SAME system with f_d = f_0, phi_n = 0 (design P-3).
3. **Discretisation of the advection.** A ~ W.grad (dynamic condition): on the band grid of B5, W.grad f = u^i d_i f +
   u^j d_j f with the contravariant components u^i = W.grad i, u^j = W.grad j; each index derivative fifth-order
   upwind-biased against its contravariant velocity (10.2 item 2), metric derivatives with the same stencils. K ~ div_h(W
   .) (kinematic condition): **K_U = Wa^-1 A_{-U}^T Wa** (Wa the panel areas, A_{-U} the advective operator of the
   reversed flow on the same grid). Consistency: writing the upwind-biased stencil as the sixth-order central difference
   plus its sixth difference, A_U = u^d D6_d - (1/60)|u^d| delta6_d (sum over d; D6 skew, delta6 symmetric and <= 0 in
   the interior), A_{-U}^T = D6_d u^d - (1/60) delta6_d |u^d|, so in the interior K_U zeta = J^-1 [D6_d(J u^d zeta) -
   (1/60) delta6_d(|u^d| J zeta)] with J = Wa: the conservative form of div_h(W zeta) in curvilinear coordinates plus an
   upwind dissipation of the right sign (the kinematic equation zeta_t = -K_U zeta + ... is dissipative); the commutator
   of delta6 with |u| J is of higher order. Discrete reciprocity: Wa K_U = A_{-U}^T Wa exactly (item 4). Variant "nat": K
   = A - diag(Phi_zz) (Phi_zz from the representation), consistent too but not adjoint by construction (consistency check
   of the pair). **Hull wall:** u^j vanishes on the wall (property (e): the wall is a band line j = 0 edge and W is
   tangent to it), so the wall is a characteristic boundary of both operators and needs no boundary data -- unlike the
   uniform stream of B5, for which the afterbody wall is an inflow boundary (10.8.2). The field is continued by even
   reflection (mirror ghosts); its O(1) error in d_j f multiplies u^j = O(j + 1/2) (index units), an O(dy) local error on
   the first lines. No hull traces are needed.
4. **Green identity between +U and -U: Timman-Newman.** phi (speed U, base flow W, Phi_zz) and psi (speed -U: base flow
   -W, -Phi_zz). With L = i w + nu, A f = W.grad f and its formal transpose A* f = -div_h(W f) (so W.grad - Phi_zz = -A*):
       g phi_z = -(L - A*)(L + A) phi,    g psi_z = -(L + A*)(L - A) psi,
   and with int_F f (A* h) = int_F (A f) h + boundary terms,
       int_F phi (L + A*)(L - A) psi = int_F ((L + A) phi)((L - A) psi) = int_F psi (L - A*)(L + A) phi,
   so G_F = int_F (phi psi_z - psi phi_z) dA reduces to boundary terms. Every boundary term of the integrations by parts
   carries W.nu_C (int_F f W.grad h = oint f h W.nu_C dl - int_F h div_h(W f)), which vanishes on C by property (e), and the
   outer edge lies in the beaches. Hence **G_F = 0 and T^OT_jk(U) = T^OT_kj(-U) exactly (classical Timman-Newman) for
   every surface-piercing hull, flared or not** (with item 7 also for the D-form); the B5 waterline term and the "(b)
   waterline reduction" are identically zero in this linearisation, and check (b) becomes the classical relation. The
   stretching term is essential: with (L + A)^2 in both conditions the products do not cancel when div_h W != 0.
   Discretely, with K_U = Wa^-1 A_{-U}^T Wa, sum_F a (phi psi_z - psi phi_z) = (1/g)[((L + A_U)phi)^T Wa (L + A_{-U})psi -
   ((L + A_{-U})psi)^T Wa (L + A_U)phi] = 0 identically (any grid, any frequency), so the discrete classical defect equals
   the discrete Green-identity defect of the BIE representation (GI, a stage-1 quantity; B5: 0.1-1 %). The pair with K =
   A - diag(Phi_zz) has the defect sum_F a zeta_psi^T (K_nat - K_cons) ... = O(consistency error) -> 0 with refinement.
5. **Haskind.** phi_D (+U, forcing f_d, f_k) and psi_j (-U radiation): from the dynamic condition (L + A) phi_D + g zeta_D =
   f_d and the kinematic one, g phi_D,z = (L - A*)(f_d - (L + A) phi_D) - g f_k; with item 4 and (L - A) psi_j = -g
   zeta_psi (dynamic condition at -U),
       G_F(phi_D, psi_j) = int_F (zeta_psi f_d + psi_j f_k) dA,
       **X^OT_D,j(U) = -rho int_S psi_j dphi_I/dn dS + rho int_F (zeta_psi_j f_d + psi_j f_k) dA**,
   using int_S (phi_D dpsi_j/dn - psi_j dphi_D/dn) dS = G_F (Green, fluid normal convention of 10.2 item 4) and dpsi_j/dn
   = i w n_j - m_j, dphi_D/dn = -dphi_I/dn. The extra term is an AREA integral over the region where w = W + U x_hat != 0,
   the incident wave's interaction with the base flow -- not a waterline term (it vanishes when the forcing is omitted,
   i.e. for the "simplified" diffraction problem, for which the classical Haskind relation holds). Discretely exact up to
   GI with K_U (same algebra as item 4).
6. **m-terms.** For translations (m_1, m_2, m_3) = -(n.grad) W = -H n (H = Hess Phi, symmetric). On S: the tangential
   gradient of W.n vanishes, so the tangential part of H n is -(W.grad) n; Laplace with Phi_n = 0 gives n.H.n = Phi_nn =
   -div_S W. Hence **m_trans = n div_S W + (W.grad) n** and, with m_rot = -(n.grad)(r x W) = r x m_trans - n x W, **m_k =
   div_S(W n_k) for every mode** (n_k the generalised normal). Check against the uniform stream: m_trans = 0, m_rot = (0, U
   n_3, -U n_2) (10.2 item 3). Singularities: at a sharp stem of waterline half-angle alpha the base flow is a wedge
   stagnation flow in the horizontal planes, Phi ~ r^lambda cos(lambda theta), lambda = pi/(pi - alpha), so W ~
   r^(lambda - 1) and m ~ r^(lambda - 2): Wigley I (alpha 13.5 deg) r^-0.92, prismatic bow (23 deg) r^-0.85 --
   integrable but so close to r^-1 that the integral of m over the first station converges like delta^(lambda - 1)
   (delta^0.08 for the Wigley): no practical panel mesh resolves it. At a submerged chine the base flow crosses a convex
   edge of fluid angle 250 deg (generic mode lambda = 180/250 = 0.72): W ~ r^-0.28 is singular and m ~ r^-1.28 is not
   integrable (the wall-sided planing variant has such a chine in its tapers; physically the flow separates there). At
   the keel of a y-symmetric hull the symmetric cross-flow mode is regular (Wigley lambda 1.5-1.9), and at a flared
   waterline the double body has a knife edge of interior angle 2 beta whose z-even cross-flow mode is regular (prismatic
   body, beta 20 deg: lambda = 180/160 = 1.125). **Discrete m-terms ("sbp", production):** m_k = -Wa^-1 G^T Wa n_k with G
   the discrete W.grad on the hull panels (the operator of the direct-pressure force). Then the discrete Tuck identity
   sum_p a_p (n_j G f + m_j f)_p = 0 holds exactly for every panel function f (in particular sum_p a_p m_p = 0 because G 1
   = 0), and in the continuum -G^T n_k corresponds to div_S(W n_k) = m_k. Consistency is in the weak sense: sum_p a_p m_p
   f_p = -sum_p a_p n_p (G f)_p converges with the order of G for smooth f, while the pointwise values carry grid-scale
   errors where the mesh spacing varies (the column sums of non-uniform three-point stencils are not zero); these enter
   the BIE through the smoothing single-layer operator. The pointwise formula ("pointwise", information) is second-order
   accurate where the base flow is smooth (sphere, analytic flow: 2.9 % -> 0.4 % from 24 x 12 to 64 x 32), but its
   integral misses the stem singularity (exploration B6-E2/E3).
7. **Forces.** Direct pressure p = -rho (phi_t + W.grad phi) (hydrostatics and the steady pressure at the displaced body,
   item 9, separate): T_jk = -rho int_S (i w phi_k + W.grad phi_k) n_j dS. Tuck's identity for a curl- and divergence-free
   W tangent to S (Stokes' theorem applied to f (W x a_j), a_j = e_j or e_j x r): int_S [n_j W.grad f + m_j f] dS = oint_C
   f (a_j x W).dl, and the line term vanishes because W is parallel to dl on C (property (e)): (e_j x W).dl = e_j.(W x dl)
   = 0, ((e_j x r) x W).dl = (e_j x r).(W x dl) = 0. Hence **T^D = T^OT in the continuum for every hull** (the B5 "NK
   inconsistency" E_j is absent), and with the sbp m-terms also discretely (to round-off).
8. **Pseudo-energy.** E = (rho/2) int_V |grad phi|^2 + (rho g/2) int_F zeta^2, M_W = rho int_F zeta (W.grad phi). From the
   conditions of item 2 (f = 0): dE/dt = -rho int_F (W.grad phi) phi_z + (rho g/2) int_F zeta^2 Phi_zz - rho int_S phi_t
   phi_n (the second term from -rho g int zeta div_h(W zeta) = -(rho g/2) oint zeta^2 W.nu_C + (rho g/2) int zeta^2
   Phi_zz with the line term zero) and dM_W/dt = rho int_F phi_z (W.grad phi) - (rho g/2) int_F zeta^2 Phi_zz (using int zeta W.grad(W.grad
   phi) = -int div_h(W zeta)(W.grad phi), no line term), so
       **d(E + M_W)/dt = -rho int_S phi_t phi_n + P_beach**,  P_beach = rho int nu phi sigma - rho g int nu zeta^2 - rho
       int [nu zeta (W.grad phi) + zeta W.grad(nu phi)],
   with NO waterline power (B5: rho U oint zeta phi_t n_x dl). For W = -U x_hat, M_W = -U M of B5. E + M_W is indefinite
   (a balance, not a stability argument). Discretely, with K_U as in item 3 and the upwind dissipation V = (A_U +
   A_{-U})/2 (positive semi-definite in the Wa inner product), the residual is -2 rho g zeta.V zeta - 2 rho zeta.V(A phi):
   the first part <= 0, the second of either sign but confined to the dissipation band (unresolved waves).
9. **Steady forces.** Bernoulli about the base flow on the mean hull: p = rho [f_0 - W.grad phi_s - |grad phi_s|^2/2] -
   rho g z. The base-flow part rho f_0 carries no x-force in the continuum (d'Alembert for the double body); its discrete
   x-force is reported and excluded: R_p = -int_S [p - rho f_0] n_x dS (linear part R_lin; with the quadratic term
   R_quad). Lift and pitch moment include rho f_0 (dominant at low Fn: the double-body sinkage, B4 9.1 rule) and the
   linear wave part. Far field: the transverse wave cut of B4 (cut_from_samples, calibrated on the exact Kelvin field,
   check H7) applied to eta_s at a column one wavelength behind the stern (W -> -U x_hat there). The B4 identity R_near =
   R_p - (rho g/2) oint zeta^2 n_x dl was derived for the uniform stream; its double-body counterpart (with eta_s and
   the base flow in the momentum flux through z = 0) is not derived here, so the hull force R_p is compared with the
   C_T-based C_W and the cut with the wave-pattern C_WP (the like-with-like pairs of B4 9.7.2); R_p - (rho g/2) oint
   zeta_wl^2 n_x dl is information. Static stiffness of the steady pressure at the displaced body (C_s = -d/dq int p_s n
   dS with the frozen base pressure p_s = rho f_0): part of F_steady(U, q); the radiation problem's C0 (omega = 0 with the
   m-term body condition) and C_s together are what the steady-force slope -dF_steady/dq is compared with (C0B,
   information).
   *Why not the shifted-Numerov marching operator of B3/B4 for (S-DB)* (task: "consistent with the existing stable
   shifted-Numerov operator"): the B3 rows march along grid lines and need phi_xx at fixed y with two conditions at each
   line's upstream end; (S-DB) advects along the curved base-flow streamlines with a cross-stream component W_y d_y and
   variable coefficients, which breaks the line-marching structure (B4 had to interpolate laterally even for the uniform
   stream, and its long-wave instability D4/H9 is open). The B5 operator used here is non-marching, every forward-speed
   mode decays (check SP), and its omega = 0 limit is the steady problem on the same grid (design P-3) -- so the steady
   and unsteady problems are one model (C0 and the steady slope are comparable). It reduces to the B5 uniform-stream
   operator far from the hull; it is not the shifted-Numerov operator, whose role as the steady NK reference (B4) is kept.
10. **Limits.** U -> 0: phi_db, W, m-terms, A and K are O(U), the system tends to the zero-speed system of stage 2, and
   by the exact classical Timman-Newman relation (item 4) every diagonal coefficient is even in U: the approach is O(U^2)
   (as observed for NK in B5, where it is not implied by a symmetry). omega -> infinity and g -> 0 at fixed U, omega:
   phi_F -> 0 (instantaneous problem), as 10.2 item 6. Fn -> 0 for the steady problem: f_0 = O(U^2) and the wave part
   vanishes; the forces tend to the rigid-lid double-body forces (acceptance M4, exact by construction).
11. **Corner of a flared waterline in the double-body model (leading order).** Near C, in the plane normal to C (r, theta;
   free surface theta = 0, hull at theta = -alpha_f, alpha_f = 180 deg - beta), the base flow's cross component on the
   free surface is W_n = c lambda r^(lambda - 1) with lambda = pi/(pi - beta) for the knife edge of the double body
   (interior angle 2 beta, z-even mode; prismatic body: 1.125), c proportional to the cross-flow strength (nonzero only where the
   sections change along the hull). For phi ~ r^mu f(theta) the advective part of the free-surface condition contributes
   d_n(W_n^2 phi_n) ~ c^2 lambda^2 mu (2 lambda + mu - 3) f(0) r^(mu + 2 lambda - 4), which dominates g phi_z ~ r^(mu - 1)
   because 2 lambda - 3 < 0. Leading order: f(0) = 0 or mu (2 lambda + mu - 3) = 0. The Dirichlet family f(0) = 0 (the
   NK family of 10.8.1) is NOT admissible here: with f(0) = 0 the advective terms vanish identically on theta = 0 at the
   next order as well, which leaves g f'(0) = 0, so f = 0. The admissible singular exponent is mu = 3 - 2 lambda (0.75
   for the prismatic body): grad phi ~ r^-0.25, integrable and milder than the NK r^-7/16, with a coefficient
   proportional to c. On a wall-sided waterline (the double body is smooth across z = 0) W_n = O(r), g phi_z dominates,
   the corner is Neumann-Neumann and regular. (Leading-order analysis only; the convergence rows of 11.1 are its test.)
