**First double-body planing table (interface).** `studies/_cache/rankine/prismV_L1_b0.111111_beta20_D0.035_DB@4da2c8916b9b/full_20261007T064122.npz`
(+ `.json`), sha256 `9025730aed2f67e789489c3a08df81370c5175ad01a392ee56fa0f1bee091b50`, 69 s for 4 nodes x 40
frequencies (M 30); save/load bit-exact, node interpolation exact. A_inf is the same at every speed (the instantaneous
problem does not depend on U), so its interpolation is exact; the kernels interpolated at the mid-nodes miss the direct
ones by **14.4 % (Fn 0.5), 11.0 % (0.7), 18.5 % (0.9) rms** (PVs FAIL, registered 10 %), and with half the spacing (nodes
0.4/0.5, slice 0.45) by **8.1 %** (PVd): the error falls only about linearly with the spacing (the features near tau = 1/4
move with U), so 10 % needs Delta Fn of about 0.1 near Fn 0.45 and 5 % about 0.05. Validity band omega' 3.0-4.6 (Fn
0.4), 3.7-4.9, 4.3-4.9, empty at Fn 1.0 on M 30. **CD FAIL: the plant law is statically unstable at Fn 0.6, 0.8 and 1.0** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: withdrawn as stated: the FAIL at Fn 0.6 came from the symmetric-part criterion (the non-symmetric C_hs + C0 has eigenvalues 0.736 and 0.0049, det > 0, and the free decay falls to 1e-37); the Fn 0.8 negative eigenvalue is grid-dependent (-0.038 / -0.027 / -0.014 at M 30/45/60, review); C_s was missing from the plant law; re-evaluated with C_s and grid series in 12.5]**
(min eig of sym(C_hs + C0) = -3.4e-3, -5.0e-2, -0.129; the trajectories diverge at Fn 0.8 and 1.0); Fn 0.4 decays. The
quick-tier table (M 20) is `quick_20261007T054719.npz` in the same folder; the folder `...@a17354db09b2` holds a smoke table
of the tiny non-registered grids (not a deliverable).

**Post-run notes and diagnosis (scratch scripts B6-E18 ... E21 after the full tier; not registered rows).** (1) DXD,
where the maxima sit (Wigley I): the interior maximum of K is at the band/lattice junction next to the bow (x 0.46-0.48,
y 0.11, where the band lines kink at the waterline end), the band and wall-line maxima of K at midship. (2) K applied to a
constant field (exact: div_h W = -Phi_zz) on the hull's middle half: error 0.34 / 0.98 / 5.97 on line 0 and 0.39 / 1.04 /
4.70 on line 1 at M 20 / 40 / 80 (hull 40 x 8), i.e. growing with refinement, while the far lattice is exact (1e-13). (3)
Cause, verified: the base velocity computed from the panel representation at the line-0 centroids has a wall-normal
component of 1.4-3.6 % of abs(W) (hull 40 x 8; 0.8-1.4 % for hull 90 x 18) that does not decrease with the distance to the
wall (0.013 -> 0.0033 L) -- a discretisation error of the hull's double-body solution, not of the continuum, which has W_n
= O(distance). The contravariant velocity through the first line therefore grows under free-surface refinement at a fixed
hull mesh (abs(u^j) 0.54 / 1.34 / 5.86 index units per unit time at M 20/40/80 with hull 40 x 8; 0.39 / 0.48 / 2.27 with
90 x 18) instead of staying O(1), the wall stops being characteristic for the discrete base flow, and the mirror closure
and its transpose produce spurious wall fluxes of order u^j. The advective operator suffers less (its wall-row error is u^j
times a derivative error); the conservative one, whose wall rows ARE fluxes, more. The global effect is visible but
bounded in the solutions: GS converges (finest change 0.64 %, order 1.57, below B5's 1.77), and the uniform-stream
diagnostic (K 1 growing like 1/dy on the first lines, as it must for a stream that crosses the curved wall) confirms the
mechanism. Remedy (next step): give the operators a base flow that is tangent at the wall by construction (remove the
wall-normal part of W on the first band lines using the hull's tangential W at the waterline, or derive u^j there from the
continuity equation) and align the hull rows with the band near the waterline (the B4 H11b finding that hull and
free-surface resolution must be matched at the waterline is the same issue). (4) The "R_p - waterline term" information
value of 11.2 item 9 was not computed by the tier. (5) Timing: the GSp job took 430 s (estimate 140 s): both planing
bodies at four grids.

### 11.5 Findings of B6

1. **Reciprocity is now an identity of the model and of its discretisation.** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: true -- and therefore these rows cannot test the discretisation: the identities hold for ANY operator A or G and ANY base flow (12.2 item 1); the TN / Haskind values below measure the panel representation's Green-identity error only, and their comparison with B5 ("6.7 % -> 1.3 %", "1.6 % -> 0.22 %") is not like-for-like]** Because the double-body base flow is
   tangent to the waterline for every hull (11.2 item 1e), the waterline terms of B5 vanish: classical Timman-Newman holds
   (11.2 item 4), the direct-pressure and m-term forces coincide (item 7), the pseudo-energy has no waterline power (item
   8), and Haskind holds with an area term of the incident-wave/base-flow interaction (item 5). With the conservative
   kinematic operator the discrete free-surface Green sum vanishes to round-off (FSR 6e-13), so the remaining
   Timman-Newman defect is the reciprocity error of the panel representation itself (equal to the GI defect of B5).
   Results: Wigley 1.34 % (M 60: 0.75 %; B5 check (b) 6.7 / 4.0 %), Haskind 0.22 % (B5 1.6 %), submerged spheroid 0.44 %;
   the interaction term carries 10-33 % of the diffraction force (the forcing changes abs(X) by up to 48 % at lambda/L 1).
2. **Planing-type bodies (the B5 blocker).** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: the comparisons with B5 in this item ("122 %", "494-575 %", "two orders of magnitude smaller") are not like-for-like (item 1); the GSp convergence of the flared body cannot detect the O(1/h) inconsistency of the conservative kinematic operator at the grid non-smoothness lines (12.2 item 3), and the coefficients change by up to 34 % (median 10 %) when K is replaced by the consistent natural operator (review probe; row KCN)]** Timman-Newman on the wall-sided planing variant 2.9 / 1.8 / 1.5 % under hull
   refinement (B5: 122 %), converging (orders 1.2, 0.4). On the flared prismatic body 5.2 / 6.1 / 6.6 % (joint M 60:
   6.3 %), registered binding row FAILS and the defect does not converge (B5: 494-575 % and growing) -- two orders of
   magnitude smaller, but not closed. Diagnosis: with the m-terms set to zero the identity closes to 0.33 %; the defect is
   the representation's reciprocity error for the near-singular m-terms in the waterline row of a flared hull (at the
   knife edge of the double body the base flow's cross component vanishes only like r^(1/8), so m ~ r^(-7/8)). The flared
   body's coefficients converge under joint refinement (GSp 1.45 %, A33' 5.93 -> 6.02 over M 30-80), which they did not in
   B5; the wall-sided variant's oscillate by +-5 % (its submerged chine makes the base flow singular, m non-integrable).
3. **Submerged sphere (WE).** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: "the B5 mesh passes" is pre-asymptotic (the 48 x 24 member fails); "our two models reproduce each" is overstated: the full double-body model agrees with Mei et al.'s column on A13 near the critical frequency, but B11 differs by 10 % and A31 by 6 %]** The B5 failure (B11 7-9 % low at the B5 mesh) was the mesh resolution of the m-term part: with
   the Tuck-exact m-terms the B5 mesh passes (0.88; B11(1.0) 0.0150 vs 0.0154); at 48 x 24 the row fails (1.27) on A13/A31 at
   the frequency next to the critical one (k_e r 0.6: 0.0286 vs 0.0269). Every m-term variant converges towards this limit
   (B6-E4), so it is a property of the reference model on our free-surface discretisation near the critical frequency, not
   of the m-terms. The full double-body model (WEdb) reproduces instead Mei et al.'s own steady-wave-basis column near the
   critical frequency (A13 -0.0201 vs -0.0200): near tau = 1/4 the two published linearisations differ by this amount, and
   our two models reproduce each.
4. **Steady Wigley.** Fixed: hull force within 22 % at all six Fn (5/6 pass; B4 6/6), wave cut 3/6 (B4 5/6): the
   double-body steady model raises the wave resistance at every Fn -- it reproduces the measured C_WP hump at Fn 0.316
   (1.05; B4 0.74) but over-predicts by 41-45 % at Fn 0.25-0.289 (B4 within 2-8 %); wave profile 5/6 (B4 3/6), bow crest
   9-15 % low (B4 20-25 %). Free: sinkage 3/3 (0.94-0.96), trim 2/3 with the error cut to a third of B4's (+0.039, +0.042,
   +0.064 deg vs +0.080, +0.116, +0.213), free hull force 3/3. Net: better trim, profile and C_WP hump, worse low-Fn wave
   resistance; no silent regression -- each is reported row by row against B4.
5. **Journee validation (no tuning).** 49/58 (B5 D 45/58, OT 50/58); the double-body basis removes the low-frequency
   heave-damping deficit at Fn 0.3 (0.95, 0.89 vs 0.73, 0.78) and the low-frequency pitch-damping deficit at Fn 0.2
   (0.95, 0.92 vs 0.78, 0.78); the remaining failures are the lowest-frequency Fn 0.2 points near tau = 1/4, the high-
   frequency ends (B33' Fn 0.3 0.78/0.61, A55', B55') and the pitch RAO point at lambda/L 1.75 (as before).
6. **The double-body base flow is a low-Froude base flow.** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: the instability statement of this item is withdrawn as stated: wrong criterion for a non-symmetric stiffness (symmetric-part eigenvalue), C_s missing, the Fn 0.8 sign grid-dependent, at Fn 1.0 C0_33 changes by 20-30 % with refinement and the validity band is empty; C0 does not scale like U^2 (only its rigid-lid part does); the plant law is non-passive at every node (bsym_min -0.67 to -0.95) and this was not discussed; the C0B agreement "within 3.2 % of the hydrostatic stiffness" hides a factor 2 in C0_33 (C_s missing). Re-evaluation: 12.5, 12.6]** On the planing table the plant law with the double-body C0 is
   statically unstable from Fn ~0.6 (CD FAIL; min eig of sym(C_hs + C0) -3.4e-3, -5.0e-2, -0.13 at Fn 0.6, 0.8, 1.0; the
   double-body static stiffness grows like U^2 with a destabilising sign, the Munk-type stiffness of the double body in an
   unbounded stream). The rigid lid is the Fn -> 0 limit of the steady free-surface condition; its Fn -> infinity limit is
   phi = 0 on z = 0 (the pressure-release image), so at planing speeds the base flow must be the steady free-surface
   solution (the same machinery with W from it). Below Fn ~0.5 (Wigley Fn 0.2-0.3, prismatic Fn 0.4) the plant law
   decays at every node and C0 agrees with the steady-force slope within 3.2 % of the hydrostatic stiffness (C0B; B5 D
   6.2 %).
7. **Table interpolation in speed.** A_inf does not depend on U; the kernels do, and the linear interpolation error falls
   only about linearly with the node spacing (14-18 % at Delta Fn 0.2, 8 % at 0.1 near Fn 0.45; PVs FAIL): about Delta Fn
   0.1 for 10 % and 0.05 for 5 % near Fn 0.4-0.5 (the features near tau = 1/4 move with U); interpolation in tau = U
   omega/g or of B(omega) before the kernel transform is the alternative.
8. **Low speed (U0c FAIL as expected).** The O(U^2) change at Fn 0.05 (B55' +14.7 %, ratio of changes 3.78) is not an NK
   artefact (NK 11.1 %): the double-body basis makes the evenness in U exact but does not change the size of the U^2
   coefficient, which stays several times the strip-theory estimate; still open (unresolved k4 waves on every affordable
   grid; FFT grid needed).
9. **Free-surface operator near the hull (DXD FAIL).** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: diagnosis incomplete: independent of the base flow, the conservative K is inconsistent at every grid non-smoothness line (band kinks at the waterline ends, band/lattice junction, stretching transitions): with an exact uniform stream K 1 grows like 1/h there (review finding B2; rows DXa, DXu); the advective A converges]** The advective operator converges in the band and the interior of the
   Wigley (orders 1.8, 2.3) but not on its first line, and the conservative operator not on the first lines; cause verified
   post-run (11.4 notes 1-3): the panel representation's base velocity has a spurious wall-normal component (1-4 % of
   abs(W)) at the first free-surface line, so the wall is not characteristic for the discrete base flow and the mirror
   closure produces wall fluxes that grow when the free surface is refined at a fixed hull mesh; plus the band kink at the
   bow. The solutions still converge (GS 0.64 %, order 1.57; DOM <= 1.2 %), but the first-line consistency must be
   repaired before the operator is called verified next to the hull.
10. **Zero-speed spectrum without a beach** **[R 2026-10-07 after review: the explanation below is wrong: the quick tier ran this row with symmetric extents too (SMALL M 16) and it FAILED (1.5e-4, 668 eigenvalues with Re > 0); at U = 0 the system is zeta' = -R_s^-1 phi, phi' = -g zeta, so a growing mode appears wherever the discrete Neumann-to-Dirichlet map R_s has a complex eigenvalue pair -- a property of the particular panel grid, not of the domain shape (12.4, row SPr)]** passes on the symmetric-extent grid used here (6e-15); B5's failure (3.3e-4)
   was on the stretched forward-speed domain -- the rows are not comparable, and the registered "expected FAIL" was written
   without noticing the different grid.

### 11.6 Next step and estimate (W4)

**[R 2026-10-07 after review: replaced by 12.8 where they differ; the plant recommendation below is replaced by 12.6 item 9]**

1. **Wall-consistent base flow on the free surface** (finding 9): remove the wall-normal part of W on the first band lines
   (tangent at the wall by construction, from the hull's tangential W at the waterline), match the hull rows to the band near
   the waterline, then re-run DXD, GS, TN, TNp. 0.5-1 day; bottleneck: keeping the exact reciprocity (K stays the adjoint of
   the corrected A) while restoring first-line consistency.
2. **Steady free-surface base flow for planing speeds** (finding 6): W, Phi_zz and the m-terms from the steady linear
   free-surface solution (then the nonlinear steady flow of design S3), with the waterline terms that reappear at the order
   of the steady wave slope derived first; target: a statically stable plant law at Fn 0.6-1.0 and the planing table rows.
   1.5-2 days; bottleneck: the waterline/contact-line terms of a base flow that is no longer tangent to the waterline, and
   the high-Fn steady solution itself (spray and transom are out of reach of the linear model).
3. **Flared waterline** (finding 2): singularity-adapted treatment of the waterline row (analytic r^(1/8) cross-flow mode in
   the m-terms, or graded hull rows matched to the band); 1 day, outcome uncertain.
4. **Chines**: a submerged chine makes the base flow singular (non-integrable m); a separation (Kutta-type) condition at the
   chine is a research item (design S4); the wall-sided planing tables are not recommended until then.
5. **Low speed and table density**: FFT core grid for U0c; speed nodes Delta Fn <= 0.05-0.1 near Fn 0.4-0.5 or interpolation
   in tau. 1 day.
Plant recommendation now: double-body tables (one force form) for Fn <= 0.4-0.5 on wall-sided or mildly flared hulls
without submerged chines; no planing-speed tables until item 2.

## 12. B6 review (2026-10-07): corrected record, operator consistency with exact base flows, static stiffness and stability, steady domain, attitude robustness

The B6 review (adversarial; probe scripts in the session scratchpad `rev_b6/`, ideas reused, nothing copied) raised five
major findings (B1-B5), one minor-major (B6) and two minor ones (B7, B8). This section registers the changed and new
rows (12.1, written and hashed BEFORE any of them ran), gives the derivations (12.2), the exploration runs (12.3), the
corrections of the record (12.4), and after the tiers the results (12.5), findings (12.6), the plan for the review's
step 2 (12.7) and the next step (12.8). Every correction made elsewhere in this note is marked
**[R 2026-10-07 after review: ...]** in place; the B6 records are kept as
`studies/_cache/rankine/b6_prereview_db_verify_{quick,full}.json/.log`. Not done here (task scope): the review's step 2
(an edge-flux A/K pair, plan in 12.7) and the steady free-surface base flow. Nothing was installed; no git commit.

### 12.1 Registration of the review rows (written before the re-run; V1)

Settings, grids and thresholds of every row not named here are those of 11.1. "Values known" = exploration runs B6R-E1
... E8 (12.3) and the review's probes; thresholds are earlier registered values wherever a counterpart exists.

### 12.1a Registration record

`registration_log.json` entry **2026-10-07T13:15:12+00:00** (written right after 12.1, before any tier of the review ran): `b6:### 11.1` 64a588759d33 (unchanged since the B6 registration), `b6:### 12.1` 7543f4f70eca, `b6:code_thresholds` 1ac966d7f8a7, `b6:tier_jobs` 633331c671a0; code sha256 of 22 files, among them `analytic.py` 0afabacb4d97, `hull.py` c7fb5a321a82, `doublebody.py` 3506b78ccc11, `tables.py` 34e42ff9e08f, `rankine_db_verify.py` 71a62c6de2e0, `rankine_db_kk.py` 4731fd3215d2. The tier JSONs record whether the code at their start equals this registered code and whether any file changed during the run.

### 12.2 Formulation and derivations of the review changes (M1; written before the tiers ran)

1. **Why the identity rows cannot detect a wrong discretisation (B1).** With K_U = Wa^-1 A_{-U}^T Wa the discrete
   free-surface Green sum between the +U and -U solutions vanishes for ANY matrix A (11.2 item 4 is an algebraic
   identity of the pair, not a property of A), and with m_k = -Wa^-1 G^T Wa n_k the discrete Tuck identity holds for ANY
   matrix G (11.2 item 6). Hence FSR, SBP and TK are exact by construction, and TN / TNp / TNw / TNs / HK reduce to the
   Green-identity defect of the panel representation (the B5 quantity GI): they test that the solver, the force
   evaluation and the adjoint pairing are coded consistently, not that A, K, G or the base flow approximate the
   continuum. The review demonstrated it (a random G: SBP 8e-17, TK 8e-16; a deliberately wrong base flow 1.5 W plus
   swirl: FSR 2.5e-13, TN 2.5 % while B55' changed by 45 %; Haskind with white-noise forcing 4-7 %). They are kept as
   implementation rows (`impl`); the physics of the double-body free-surface conditions is tested only by operator
   consistency (DXD, DXa, DXu) and by the solutions (GS, GSp, WE, SK, SF, Vd).
2. **Exact double-body flow for operator rows (DXa).** The half prolate spheroid (semi-axes a along x, b across and
   down, centre on z = 0, `hull.half_spheroid`) has as rigid-lid double body the full spheroid, whose flow in the stream
   -U x_hat is classical (Lamb 1932 art. 105; Newman 1977 sec. 4.13): with the focal half-distance k = sqrt(a^2 - b^2),
   the focal distances r1, r2, the spheroidal coordinates zeta = (r1 + r2)/(2k), mu = (r2 - r1)/(2k),
       Phi = -U x + B mu Q1(zeta),  Q1 = (zeta/2) ln((zeta + 1)/(zeta - 1)) - 1,  B = U k / Q1'(zeta0),  zeta0 = a/k,
   (dPhi/dzeta = 0 on the body), W = grad Phi from grad r_i = (x -+ k, y, z)/r_i, and on z = 0 (where d mu/dz = d
   zeta/dz = 0, d^2 r_i/dz^2 = 1/r_i) Phi_zz = B [Q1 (1/r2 - 1/r1) + mu Q1' (1/r1 + 1/r2)]/(2k). Checks (B6R-E1):
   W.n on the body 5e-6 (points 1e-7 outside), W against finite differences of Phi 1.5e-7, Phi_zz + div_h W 2e-5 of
   max abs(Phi_zz) 3.7 (finite-difference error). W is tangent to the waterline, so the rows test the operators where
   the production base flow is supposed to be -- without the representation's near-wall error (11.4 note 3). The
   elliptic waterline has a vertical tangent at x = +-a; the band rows use abs(x) <= 0.6 a.
3. **Mechanism of K's inconsistency (B2), and the uniform-stream rows (DXu).** K 1 = Wa^-1 sum_d D_d^T (Wa u^d) (the
   transposed index-derivative stencils acting on the contravariant flux). In the continuum J^-1 d_d (J u^d) = div_h W
   by the metric identity d_i(y_j) = d_j(y_i) (geometric conservation law, GCL). Discretely the metric terms are upwind-
   biased six-point derivatives of the centroid positions and the weight is the panel area, not that derivative's
   Jacobian; at a line where the grid spacing changes abruptly (band kink at a pointed waterline end, band/lattice
   junction, start of a stretching) Wa u^d changes by O(1) relative over one cell, its transposed difference is O(1) in
   index units and divided by Wa = O(h^2) gives O(1/h) -- the review's K 1 growing like 1/h, which no refinement
   removes and which a joint refinement (GSp) cannot reveal because the relative jumps are the same on every grid. The
   advective A never differentiates the metric products (A 1 = 0 exactly) and is consistent there. With the uniform
   stream W = (-1, 0) the exact values are A f = K f = -f_x and K 1 = 0, independent of any base-flow computation; the
   band lines 0-2 over the hull are excluded because that stream crosses the curved wall (the mirror closure assumes a
   tangent flow). Remedy: step 2 (12.7).
4. **Frozen base-pressure stiffness C_s (B3) and the plant law.** A static displacement q_k (xi_1 = e_x, xi_3 = e_z,
   xi_5 = e_y x (r - r_ref): pitch bow down) changes the double-body steady force F_j = -int_S(q) p n_j dS, p = rho f_0
   + linear wave pressure - rho g z, in three ways: (a) the hydrostatics (C_hs); (b) the base flow and the waves change
   at fixed points -- the linearised body condition dphi/dn = m_k q_k, i.e. the radiation problem's omega = 0 response
   C0 (its rigid-lid part C0_lid); (c) the hull surface moves through the FROZEN base-pressure field p = rho f_0:
       dF_j = -int_S (xi_k . grad p) n_j dS - int_S p dn_j dS + oint_C p n_j xi_z / abs(n_h) dl,
   with dn = e_y x n for pitch (n_5 about the carried reference is rotation-invariant), and the last term the wetted
   strip of width xi_z/abs(n_h) that leaves the water at the waterline C (abs(n_h) the horizontal part of the hull
   normal there). On S the base flow is tangent, so grad f_0 = -(W.grad)W = -(W.grad_S)W is a surface derivative (the
   discrete W.grad G of the base flow applied to W). C_s,jk = -dF_j/dq_k (`doublebody.frozen_pressure_stiffness`). The
   same formula with p = -rho g z returns C_hs (check: C33 = rho g A_w, because int_S n_z dS = -A_w), which fixes the
   strip sign. For the rigid-lid force alone, -dF_db/dq = C0_lid + C_s to first order (chain rule: Eulerian change plus
   motion through the frozen field): the CS row compares C_s from the formula with K_db - C0_lid from re-panelled
   central differences. In the uniform-stream (NK) linearisation f_0 = 0, so C_s = 0 and C0 was complete (B5); in the
   double-body linearisation it is not -- the B6 plant law omitted C_s (11.2 item 9 named it, the code never computed
   it). Plant law since the review: F = -A_inf a - B_inf v - int K v ds - (C0 + C_s)(q - qbar) + F_steady(U, qbar)
   (`tables.RadiationForce`, `free_decay`; C_s stored per node, zero for NK tables; TABLE_VERSION 3).
   **C0 + C_s is not the steady-force slope.** The slope K_full = -dF_steady/dq also contains the change of the steady
   WAVE problem with q: its free-surface forcing div_h(W f_0) and its operator change with the base flow, and the steady
   wave potential interacts with the displacement. The radiation problem neglects these interactions (the double-body
   linearisation of Nakos & Sclavounos / Kring), so C0 + C_s - K_full = (C0 - C0_lid) - (K_full - K_db) is the size of
   the neglected steady-wave interaction, a modelling difference, not a discretisation error. C0B (re-registered)
   measures it relative to the dynamic stiffness; the review's probe gave 9.4 % in heave (0.3 % of C_hs): expected
   FAIL, reported, and the plant uses C0 + C_s (the radiation problem's own consistent stiffness plus the frozen part),
   not the steady slope.
5. **Static stability of a non-symmetric stiffness (B4).** For M x'' + C x = 0 with M symmetric positive definite and C
   not symmetric, the motions are x = v e^(s t) with M^-1 C v = -s^2 v: a real negative eigenvalue of M^-1 C gives a
   real positive s (divergence; for 2 x 2 equivalent to det C < 0), a complex pair gives one root with Re s > 0
   (circulatory coupling, flutter without damping), real positive eigenvalues give a neutral undamped system. Positive
   definiteness of the symmetric part (the B6 criterion) is sufficient for the eigenvalues of C to have positive real
   parts but neither necessary nor sufficient for the absence of divergence of M x'' + C x = 0. Static criterion since
   the review: every eigenvalue of (M + A_inf)^-1 (C_hs + C0 + C_s) has a positive real part (no divergence); a complex
   pair (the heave and pitch frequencies of the planing body are nearly equal, so the non-symmetric coupling makes
   them complex: circulatory coupling of the UNDAMPED system) is reported and decided by the damped trajectory. The
   trajectory test of the damped plant law with memory remains the dynamic criterion, measured in a positive-definite norm (Ep with C_hs; the B6 E used the
   symmetric part of the total stiffness, indefinite at the planing nodes, which made "E max <= 1.2 E0" meaningless:
   E max/E0 -0.58 at Fn 0.8).
6. **Attitude robustness at the pointed ends (B5).** (a) Wetted range: `HullForm.mesh` placed the stations over [x_aft,
   x_fwd] at every attitude; at a pointed end whose keel rises to the design waterline (prismatic_v: D -> 0) the end
   stations are DRY for the smallest trim or rise of the right sign (zeta_w + D < 0), their z-range was inverted and the
   panels twisted (the review's abs(W) 7.3e4, F_z -150 instead of -0.0074 at trim -1e-3). `wet_range` finds the keel
   emergence point (bisection of g = zeta_w + D, a simple root since D' != 0 at the end) and the stations span the
   wetted range; both ends wet (every B4-B6 mesh) -> the identical stations. The waterline and its ends follow the same
   range. (b) Above the design waterline the prism kept its V up to the chine (`above="form"`); the vertical extension
   from z_b = 0 (a Wigley convention) made the waterline wall-sided with a knuckle for any sinkage. (c) A floor 1e-9 x
   the line's longest increment on the chord increments of the surface calculus (no effect on any registered mesh;
   coincident centroids of collapsed panels no longer divide by zero). Remaining geometry, not numerics: at a nonzero
   attitude the prism's chine, whose height above the waterline is p(x)(b0 tan(beta) - D0) -> 0 at the tips, is
   submerged near the deeper tip (fluid angle 250 deg: W ~ r^-0.28), and the stem segment meets the keel in a 3-D
   corner; the cosine-clustered tip panels sample these singular flows, so max abs(W) at centroids grows under
   refinement (exploration B6R-E2) while the integrated force stays continuous. AT3 bounds the area-weighted rms
   instead of the maximum, and AT4/AT5 measure continuity and smoothness through the linear prediction.
7. **Steady domain scaled with the steady wavelength (B6).** STEADY_GRID (upstream 1.0 L, beach 0.5 L) is shorter than
   one transverse wavelength lambda0 = 2 pi U^2/g above Fn 0.28 and put the Fn 0.408 cut inside the downstream beach.
   Rule (`steady_grid`, numerical device registered in EMPIRICAL_REGISTER_RANKINE.md): l = lambda0/L, b_up = max(0.5,
   l), b_dn = max(1.5, l), b_lat = max(0.75, l), up = max(1.0, 0.5 + b_up), down = max(2.5, 1.5 l + b_dn), lat = max(1.5,
   0.75 + b_lat): every beach at least one wavelength, an undamped upstream/lateral zone of at least 0.5 / 0.75 L, and
   room for the cut 1 lambda0 behind the stern plus half a wavelength. For l <= 0.49 (Fn <= 0.28) it returns STEADY_GRID.
   Reference: on a deep spheroid (disturbance of the base flow at z = 0 about 1 %, where the double-body and
   Neumann-Kelvin models coincide to that order) the exact Kelvin body solve (B3 S2K) is an exact solution of the
   linear problem: B6 domain +12.8 %, scaled +1.0 % (exploration B6R-E4). A caveat found on the way (B6R-E7): where
   the wave resistance is exponentially small (f/b 6, Fn 0.35, C_W 2.4e-7) the steady pipeline has a domain-dependent
   floor of order 1e-6 in C_W (16 x the exact value at the scaled domain, 3.5 x at twice that domain): irrelevant for
   surface-piercing hulls (C_W ~ 1e-3), a limit of the near-field resistance of deeply submerged bodies at low Fn.
8. **Finite-difference step of the base flow at free-surface points (B8).** field_velocity / field_phizz difference the
   representation with a fourth-order stencil of half-width 2 h; with h = 1e-3 L fixed, points closer than ~4 h to a
   hull panel put stencil points next to the panels (review: 56 % error at d = 0.00125 L). The step is now
   min(1e-3 L, d/4) with d the exact distance to the hull panels and their images (floored at 1e-6 L), computed only
   where a cheap lower bound (centroid distance minus circumradius) is below 4e-3 L: unchanged values wherever the first
   centroids are beyond 4e-3 L.
9. **Experiment 3' (high-Fn image).** The Fn -> infinity limit of the linear steady free-surface condition is phi = 0
   on z = 0, i.e. the z-odd (pressure-release) image instead of the rigid lid. For the hull without waves the total base
   flow stiffness is K = C0 + C_s = -dF_db/dq for either image. With the odd image the base flow crosses z = 0 at the
   waterline, so the edge-flux G (zero waterline flux), its adjoint m-terms and the frozen-field formula are not
   consistent there; the total K = -dF_db/dq is therefore taken by re-panelled central differences for both images
   (no m-terms needed), and the production split C0_lid + C_s is printed for the lid only (exploration B6R-E3: the
   split with the Lagrange operator and pointwise m-terms misses the stem singularity of the m-terms and is wrong on
   the prism by an order of magnitude). Question: does the sign of the heave or pitch
   stiffness flip relative to the rigid lid (as a suction toward a rigid wall turns into a repulsion from a
   pressure-release surface for a submerged body)?

### 12.4 Corrections of the record (written before the re-run; each marked in place)

1. **Identity rows (B1).** The B6 summaries counted SBP, TN, FSR, TK, HK, HK (U = 0), TNp, TNw (and TNs) as passed
   verification and presented "B5 6.7 % -> B6 1.3 %", "494 % -> 5 %", "1.6 % -> 0.22 %" as progress. With the adjoint
   constructions these rows hold for any operator and any base flow (12.2 item 1); the comparisons with B5's check (b)
   (which did test the free-surface treatment) are not like-for-like and are withdrawn as evidence. Recount of the B6
   tiers without them: quick 74/92, full 111/139 binding; implementation rows quick 7/8, full 8/9 (TNp fails). Marked
   in the Chinese summary of B6, in 11.4 and in 11.5 items 1-2.
2. **Planing-table stability (B4).** "Statically unstable from Fn ~0.6" (11.4, 11.5 item 6, Chinese summary) is
   withdrawn as stated: wrong criterion for a non-symmetric stiffness, C_s missing, grid dependence ignored, the
   non-passivity of B(omega) at every node not discussed. Re-evaluated in 12.5 with the corrected criterion, C_s and a
   grid series.
3. **C0B (B3, B8).** The B6 row compared C0 alone with the steady slope and normalised by the hydrostatic stiffness,
   which hid a factor 2 in C0_33 (-0.0426 vs -0.0203); 11.2 item 9 named C_s, the code never computed it. Re-registered
   (12.1).
4. **Zero-speed spectrum without a beach (B7).** The quick tier ran this row on SMALL M 16 with symmetric extents and
   it FAILED (1.5e-4 sqrt(g/L), 668 eigenvalues with Re > 0); 11.4 and 11.5 item 10 reported only the full tier's PASS
   and explained B5's failure by the stretched domain. The explanation is wrong: at U = 0 the system is zeta' = -R_s^-1
   phi_F, phi_F' = -g zeta, so lambda^2 = g eig(R_s^-1) and every complex eigenvalue pair of the discrete (collocation,
   non-symmetric) Neumann-to-Dirichlet map R_s gives a growing mode. The M 16 grid has one such pair, the M 20 grid none
   (exploration B6R-E6); whether a zero-speed grid without a beach is neutral is a property of the particular panel
   grid, as B5 had said ("the discrete operator is not exactly symmetric"). The registered "expected FAIL" was right in
   substance; the quick tier confirms it. Row SPr reports the count.
5. **Minor (B8).** "WEdb reproduces Mei et al." is overstated (agreement on A13 near the critical frequency only; B11
   10 %, A31 6 %); the WE pass at 24 x 12 is pre-asymptotic (the 48 x 24 member fails); field_velocity's fixed step was
   a latent hazard near the wall (fixed, 12.2 item 8); the docstrings of doublebody.py (K "a consistent conservative
   discretisation") and of the removed c0b_check (which promised a C_s output it never computed) are corrected.
6. **Free-surface operator (B2).** 11.5 item 9 attributed the DXD failure to the representation's base-flow error
   alone; K is inconsistent at every grid non-smoothness line independent of the base flow (rows DXa, DXu).
7. **Steady domain (B6).** 11.4 and 11.5 item 4 compared the steady Wigley with experiments on a domain whose upstream
   beach is shorter than one wavelength above Fn 0.28 and whose Fn 0.408 cut lay in the downstream beach; re-run on the
   scaled domain (12.5).

### 12.6 Findings after the review (replace the conflicting statements of 11.4-11.6, marked in place)

1. **What B6 verified and what it did not.** The Green-identity rows show that the solver, the force evaluation and the
   adjoint pairing are coded consistently (implementation, 8/9 pass); they say nothing about the free-surface
   operators. The B6 tiers without them: 111/139 (full), 74/92 (quick).
2. **Free-surface operators (B2 confirmed with an exact double-body flow).** The advective operator A is consistent
   everywhere (orders 2.0-2.8 with the exact spheroid flow and the exact uniform stream, at the waterline kinks and the
   stretching transitions included). The conservative kinematic operator K is consistent near the hull wall (with the
   exact flow: orders 2.3 and 1.3 for K f and K 1 on lines 0-2) but NOT at grid non-smoothness lines: K 1 grows like 1/h
   there (DXu) and its error does not fall in the band and lattice (DXa); this is the discrete metric identity failing
   (12.2 item 3), independent of the base flow. The B6 near-wall DXD failure was mostly the representation's base flow
   (DXb: 3 % of U on line 0 with hull 40 x 8, falling with hull refinement). Effect on results: negligible on the
   wall-sided Wigley I (KCN 0.06 %), up to 34 % (median 10 %) on the flared prismatic body -- **the flared-body
   coefficients, the GSp convergence and the planing table are not verified until K is replaced** (step 2, 12.7).
3. **Static stiffness.** The double-body plant law needs the frozen base-pressure stiffness C_s; it is now derived,
   computed from surface quantities, verified two ways (CS: 0.9 % on the wall-sided and on the flared Wigley) and part
   of the plant law and the tables. Its size: 3 % of C_hs on the Wigley I at Fn 0.3 (half of C0's heave entry, opposite
   sign), and on the planing body comparable with C0 (C_s heave 0.29-0.96, pitch 0.07-0.23 at Fn 0.6-1.0). C0 + C_s
   still differs from the steady-force slope by 8.8 % of the dynamic stiffness in heave (0.26 % of C_hs): the steady-wave
   interactions the radiation linearisation neglects; C0B fails as expected and the plant uses C0 + C_s.
4. **Planing-table stability (B4) re-evaluated.** With the correct criterion and C_s, every node of the planing table
   (Fn 0.4-1.0) is statically stable on all three grids (CDs) and every free decay of the plant law decays (CD, also the
   Wigley table). The B6 statement "statically unstable from Fn ~0.6" is withdrawn. What remains open: (a) the
   hydrodynamic stiffness C0 + C_s is not grid-converged at Fn 0.8-1.0 (at Fn 1.0 its heave entry goes from -0.31 to
   +0.20 between M 30 and M 60), so only the total static verdict is robust (C_hs dominates); (b) at Fn 0.6-1.0 the
   non-symmetric coupling makes the undamped heave-pitch pair circulatory on the coarser grids (complex pairs, shrinking
   with refinement); (c) **B(omega) is not passive at low frequency from Fn 0.8 (M 30; from Fn 0.6 on M 20)**, i.e. the
   linear model lets the stream feed energy into heave-pitch motions near their natural frequencies; the free decays
   still decay, and with K inconsistent on this body (item 2) the cause cannot be attributed yet. Plant use: these
   tables are not recommended (item 2).
5. **Pointed ends at an attitude (B5).** The wet-range mesh removes the review's pathology (no dry or inverted panels;
   forces continuous), and the Wigley I is smooth in sink and trim (3 % from the linear prediction over +-2e-3). The
   prismatic body is not: at any nonzero attitude its chine is submerged near the deeper tip (its height above the
   waterline goes to zero at the tips) and the stem meets the keel in a 3-D corner; the base flow is singular there and
   the force changes deviate from linear by 25 % to 8 x (AT5 FAIL). Consequences: finite-difference attitude derivatives
   on this hull are unreliable at steps below ~2e-3 (C_s therefore uses the frozen-field formula), and the planing
   hulls need a geometric treatment of the chine tip (or a separation condition) before free-attitude steady solutions.
6. **Steady problem (B6).** The double-body steady pipeline agrees with an exact linear solution within 1.0 % on the
   wavelength-scaled domain (SK; the B6 domain +13 %). For the Wigley rows the scaled domain changes nothing below Fn
   0.32 and 1-1.5 % at Fn 0.408; the domain x 1.5 changes the Fn 0.289 cut by 0.2 %. **The +42-45 % wave-cut excess at
   Fn 0.25-0.289 is therefore not a domain effect**; K's inconsistency is negligible on this hull (KCN), so the
   remaining candidates are the double-body steady model itself at low Fn and the representation's near-wall base flow
   (the review's hull-mesh probe), to be separated in the next steady step. A caveat: where the true wave resistance is
   exponentially small (deep body, Fn 0.35) the near-field force has a domain-dependent floor of order 1e-6 in C_W.
7. **Zero speed without a beach (B7).** Neutral or slowly growing depending on the grid (complex eigenvalue pairs of
   the discrete Neumann-to-Dirichlet map); B5's statement stands, B6's explanation is withdrawn.
8. **Experiment 3'.** The pressure-release image does not flip the sign of the base-flow stiffness; it reduces its
   destabilising size to 0.22 of the rigid lid's. Without waves, the rigid lid alone would make the prism divergent in
   heave from about Fn 0.8; the pressure-release limit and the free-surface model with C_s are stable to Fn 1.0. The
   planing-speed base flow (steady free surface, 11.6 item 2) remains necessary for the coefficients, not for static
   stability.
9. **Plant recommendation (replaces 11.6's last lines).** Double-body tables with the plant law F = ... - (C0 + C_s)(q -
   qbar) for wall-sided hulls without submerged chines up to Fn ~0.4-0.5 (Wigley I: C_s verified, operators consistent
   on that hull to 0.06 %); flared and planing hulls: not recommended until step 2 (12.7) and the chine-tip treatment.

### 12.7 Plan for the review's step 2: an edge-flux (finite-volume) A/K pair that is exactly adjoint AND consistent

Requirement: (i) exact discrete reciprocity Wa K_U = A_{-U}^T Wa (keeps the Timman-Newman / Haskind / pseudo-energy
structure of 11.2 items 4, 5, 8); (ii) both operators consistent on the real grids, including the band kinks at pointed
waterline ends, the band/lattice junction and the stretching transitions (rows DXa, DXu with K 1 = div_h W); (iii)
upwind dissipation of the right sign (every mode decays, SP); (iv) the hull wall a zero-flux characteristic boundary.
The present pair satisfies (i), (iii), (iv) but not (ii) for K, because the panel area is not the Jacobian of the
six-point metric stencils and the discrete metric identity fails at those lines (12.2 item 3).

Construction (no new physics; the finite-volume idea already used on the hull, `wgrad_operator_fv`):
1. **Geometric fluxes.** For every edge e of the free-surface grid, F_e = int_e W.nu dl by two-point Gauss quadrature
   along the edge (base flow at edge points: `field_velocity`, or the closed form in the DXa test); the hull-wall edges
   get F = 0 (characteristic boundary, which also removes the representation's spurious wall-normal velocity from the
   operators), the centre-plane edges F = 0 by symmetry. Cell divergence (div W)_c = (1/a_c) sum_e F_e: Gauss's
   theorem on each polygon, so K 1 = div W holds by construction on ANY grid (discrete GCL).
2. **Conservative central operator.** K_c f = (1/a_c) sum_e F_e f_e with a symmetric face reconstruction along the grid
   line through the face (four points, index weights (-1, 7, 7, -1)/12; mirror values at the wall and the centre plane,
   inflow values zero, one-sided at the outflow end).
3. **Skew/symmetric split (exact adjointness for any K_c).** S_U = (Wa K_c - (Wa K_c)^T)/2 (skew), D_U = diag(Wa div W),
   and an edge-based dissipation V with Wa V symmetric positive semi-definite and independent of the sign of U:
       A_U = Wa^-1 (S_U - D_U/2) - V,     K_U = Wa^-1 (S_U + D_U/2) - V.
   Since S_{-U} = -S_U and D_{-U} = -D_U, (Wa A_{-U})^T = S_U + D_U/2 - Wa V = Wa K_U: reciprocity exact (FSR stays an
   implementation row). Consistency: K_U -> div(W f) because K_c is consistent and S/Wa = (K_c - Wa^-1 K_c^T Wa)/2 with
   the transpose of a flux-form divergence a consistent -W.grad (in the finite-volume sense: at the irregular lines a
   local truncation error O(1) of flux form, which converges globally -- supraconvergence -- unlike the present O(1/h)
   that does not; DXa/DXu measure exactly this).
4. **Dissipation.** Wa V = sum_d D3_d^T diag(c |F_e|) D3_d, D3_d the third difference across the edges of grid-line
   direction d (even reflection at the wall and the centre plane, truncated to lower order at the outer edges), c chosen
   so that the uniform-grid symbol matches the present (1/60) |u| delta6 (fifth-order upwind-biased scheme of B5, kept
   because third order damped the steady wake too much, B5-E7). Wa V symmetric PSD gives the pseudo-energy residual
   -2 rho g zeta.V zeta <= 0 of 11.2 item 8.
5. **Verification (registered before running, as always).** DXa and DXu (A f, K f, K 1 in all regions, thresholds of
   12.1), SP (every mode decays), FSR / TN (implementation rows), GS / GSp / DOM, KCN (cons vs nat: must fall to the
   consistency error), WE, Vd, SF; and the B6 rows that the inconsistency touched (TNp, the flared-body coefficients).
Estimate: 1-1.5 days (operator assembly 0.5 day; boundary closures and the dissipation symbol match 0.5 day; tiers and
review fixes 0.5 day). Bottleneck: the boundary closures (wall, outflow) keeping Wa V symmetric and the reconstruction
consistent; the solver cost is unchanged (same sparsity order, one LU per frequency).

### 12.8 Next step and estimate (W4; replaces 11.6 where they differ)

1. **Step 2: the edge-flux A/K pair (12.7)**, then re-run DXa, DXu, SP, TN/TNp, GS, GSp, KCN, CD/CDs, PV and the B6 rows
   on flared bodies. 1-1.5 days; bottleneck: boundary closures that keep Wa V symmetric and the reconstruction
   consistent.
2. **Low-frequency non-passivity at Fn >= 0.8 on the planing body**: re-examine after step 2 (if it persists, analyse the
   energy flux of the pseudo-energy balance per frequency). 0.5 day.
3. **Chine tips of planing hulls at an attitude** (submerged chine near the tips, stem-keel corner): a geometric or
   separation treatment before any free-attitude steady solution on these hulls. 1 day, outcome uncertain (research item
   of design S4 for the separation part).
4. **Steady low-Fn excess on the Wigley** (not a domain effect): hull-mesh and near-wall base-flow series at Fn 0.289,
   then the wall-tangent base flow on the first lines (11.6 item 1). 0.5-1 day.
5. **Steady free-surface base flow for planing speeds** (11.6 item 2), after items 1-3. 1.5-2 days.
6. **Low speed and table density** (11.6 item 5), unchanged. 1 day.

## 13. Stage B7 (2026-10-07): the planing feasibility gate -- steady planing of prismatic hard-chine surfaces with ONE set of equations (linear potential flow, gravity in the free-surface condition), wetted area from the solution, Kutta condition at the transom

Question of the gate (orchestrator, for the user): on a simple prismatic planing hull, compute the steady planing
solution with this set of equations plus flow-separation conditions at the chines and the transom, compare lift, trim
(and wetted length, centre of pressure) with Savitsky's data; GO = continue this route, NO-GO = report why, so that the
user can choose a fallback. Time box about one day of agent wall-clock (start 2026-10-07 14:20 UTC).
Code: `hydro/rankine/planing_kernel.py` (linear free-surface response to a pressure distribution, tabulated),
`hydro/rankine/planing_steady.py` (solver; production class `PlaningSprayRootFast`, lateral scheme `hat0`),
`hydro/rankine/vlm_ref.py` (independent thin-wing vortex lattice for the high-speed limit), gate script
`studies/rankine_planing_gate.py` (tiers quick / full, `--register`). Outputs `studies/_cache/rankine/planing_gate_
{quick,full}.json/.log`, registration log `studies/_cache/rankine/planing_registration_log.json`. No WaveBEM code was
read for this stage (WaveBEM has no planing capability); nothing installed; no git commit.

**GO/NO-GO rule (binding).** Numerics adequate for a verdict = PK1-PK4, PL1, PL2 pass and U_num <= 5 % (PC1u).
- **GO** if all verification rows (PK*, PC1, PC1cp, PC1u, PL*) pass AND VL.all, VL.beta20, VCP and VEQ pass.
- **NO-GO** if the numerics are adequate AND a robust validation fraction fails (VLR.all or VLR.beta20 < 80 %): the
  model misses the data by more than the tolerance plus the numerical uncertainty.
- **INCONCLUSIVE** otherwise (numerics inadequate, or the misses lie within the numerical uncertainty).
No raw towing data are used: the NACA scans (Chambliss & Boyd 1953, TN 2876, tables I-II) are available only as
images with an OCR layer too corrupted to transcribe without page images (checked 2026-10-07); Savitsky's equations are
the registered reference, as the gate question asks. This is a limitation, recorded in 13.6.

### 13.1b Amendment 1 (2026-10-07, after an aborted quick tier, before any completed tier; no threshold, row or job changed)

The first quick tier (started 17:19:09 UTC on the registered code) was stopped after 12 min inside row PK2: its REFERENCE quadrature (64 Gauss points per k interval, a 400-point Gauss rule per linear piece evaluated in a Python loop over about 2000 intervals) alone would have exceeded the quick-tier budget. The reference was rewritten as the closed-form integral of the piecewise-linear lateral element against cos(k (y_t - y)) (exact; agrees with a 2e6-point trapezoidal integration to 1e-10) and vectorised over the intervals -- the same quantity, computed faster. Tested once after the change: PK2 = 7.0e-8 (tolerance 1e-6). No production code changed. Log of the aborted run kept as `planing_gate_quick_aborted1.log` (header only, no row completed). ### 13.1c Amendment 2 (2026-10-07, after the first complete quick tier `planing_gate_quick_r1.*`, before the full tier; no threshold, no binding row, no condition changed)

**PK1 reference resolution.** The first quick tier (17:32:28 UTC, registered code) failed PK1 (9.4 %). Diagnosis (scratch `t_pk1*.py`): the differences sit only in the near-field entries of the target lines on and next to the element, with the lateral pattern of the element's second derivative (-2 : 1) -- the signature of truncated lateral image sums in the brute-force REFERENCE (the hydrostatic part does not decay in k_y, so the folded lateral sum converges slowly). With more lateral images the reference converges to the tables: max difference 12 % (8 images), 7.2 % (16), 5.7 % (32), 2.3 % (64), 0.80 % (128) for the slope spec at the keel element; the tables did not change with the k_y range (k_max 2e5 ... 2e8) or the number of x folds. PK1 now uses 128 lateral images and a 40 b lateral box in both tiers (quick: the slope spec at the keel element only, for time). The check, the tolerance (5e-3) and the binding status are unchanged. A trial change of the kernel (subtracting the image-truncated hydrostatic limit) made the tables depend strongly on the number of x folds and was reverted; `planing_kernel.py` is functionally the registered file but its bytes differ after the revert (no copy of the registered bytes was kept), so it is re-registered; the re-run quick tier must reproduce the first quick tier in every other row (checked in 13.4).

**PL1 (information added here, the row itself unchanged).** The first quick tier failed PL1 (5.8 %). Exploration after that run: with the damping of PL1 (l_mu = 50 b) the planform lift is 0.058604 (C_V 10), 0.053650 (20), 0.049849 (40), i.e. it keeps falling below the vortex-lattice limit (0.05630, 0.05520, 0.05483, 0.05464 at 12/30, 24/60, 36/80, 48/100 lateral/chordwise panels; about 0.0541 extrapolated in 1/n). At C_V 20 and 40 the damping eps = 1/50 is much larger than k0 = 1/C_V^2 (eps/k0 = 8 and 32), outside the regime eps << k0 in which the first-order Richardson extrapolation is valid (the production rule l_mu = 0.5 lambda_0 gives eps/k0 = 0.32); with the production rule the C_V 10 value is 0.058577 (unchanged). PL1 as registered therefore tests the solver outside the damping regime it uses, and its 1/C_V^2 extrapolation is not supported by the C_V 10-40 values; it is kept unchanged (binding) and is expected to fail; the planing-regime damping is verified by PK4 (Richardson pair). This finding was made after the quick tier and is reported, not used to change the row.

### 13.2 Formulation and derivation (M1; written before the tiers ran)

**Survey of established steady planing potential-flow methods (what was taken, what was not).**
- Wagner (1932), Tulin (1957) "slender surfaces planing at high speeds": the planing bottom as a thin surface with the
  boundary conditions transferred to z = 0, gravity neglected (phi = 0 on the free surface); in each cross-plane the
  section enters the water at V = U tau, the wetted half-breadth follows from the condition that the free surface rises
  to meet the bottom (Wagner's wetting condition), which gives the factor pi/2 and the keel-chine wetted-length
  difference b tan(beta)/(pi tan(tau)) used by Savitsky (1964). Taken: the linearised wetting (spray-root) condition and
  the slender limit as a check (PL2).
- Wang & Rispin (1971), three-dimensional planing at high Froude number: the planing surface at Fn -> inf is the lower
  half of a thin lifting surface (antisymmetric extension; lift = half the wing lift of the same planform). Taken: the
  high-speed limit as an independent check (PL1, against a vortex lattice of the wing).
- Doctors (1974), planing surfaces as distributions of finite pressure elements with the linear Kelvin (gravity)
  free-surface response; Cheng & Wellicome (1989), transversely variable pressure strips. Taken: the pressure-patch
  formulation with the exact linear gravity response -- this is the only family that keeps gravity IN the free-surface
  condition, as the user's equations require.
- Lai & Troesch (1995, 1996), vortex-lattice method at high speed (phi = 0 free surface, gravity only as added
  hydrostatics), spray root from slender-body (Wagner) theory, modelling issues of the chine flow. Savander, Scorpio &
  Taylor (2002), vortex lattice in the Wagner class with the spray-root line found iteratively and ADDITIONAL vortex
  patches for the forward spray and for the side jets off the wetted chines (patch width from the flow speed at the
  chine, iterative). Taken: the vortex-lattice-type 1/4-3/4 placement idea (the Kutta condition), the iterative
  spray-root line. Not taken: the side-jet patches (an extra, partly heuristic model of chine separation; see item C).
- Sun & Faltinsen (2007, read for this gate), nonlinear 2D+t with gravity and flow separation from the chine: vertical
  force over-predicted (relative to Troesch's tests and Savitsky) unless a transom "suction" correction (L_K reduced by
  0.5 b, an empirical device) is applied; they attribute the difference to the three-dimensional pressure drop near the
  transom, which needs gravity. In the present 3-D linear theory that drop is part of the solution (Kutta condition with
  gravity), not a correction.

**Equations.** Hull-fixed frame, stream U in +x, z up, fluid z < 0; nondimensional with the beam b, U and rho
(pressure / (rho U^2)); one parameter C_V = U / sqrt(g b), k0 = 1/C_V^2. Perturbation potential phi, free-surface
elevation zeta, applied pressure p on z = 0 (p = 0 on the free surface F, unknown on the wetted planform W). Linear
conditions on z = 0 (Kelvin):
    kinematic  zeta_x = phi_z,        dynamic  phi_x + k0 zeta + p = 0,        Laplace in z < 0, decaying.
With f(x, y) = (1/4 pi^2) int fhat e^{i k.x} and phihat_z = |k| phihat:
    zetahat = M(k) phat,    M(k) = -|k| / (k0 |k| - (k_x - i eps)^2),                                       (13.1)
eps -> 0+ (Rayleigh): the poles of the k_x integral lie in the upper half plane, so free waves appear only downstream
(radiation condition). This is the whole linear free-surface problem: zeta = G[p] with G the operator of symbol (13.1);
it is the steady Neumann-Kelvin problem of the earlier stages with the hull replaced by a pressure distribution on z = 0.
The bottom of a prismatic hull with trim tau, deadrise beta, keel depth d at the transom x = 0:
    h(x, y) = -d + (-x) tan(tau) + |y| tan(beta),   x <= 0, |y| <= 1/2.
Conditions:
- **(B) body.** zeta = h on W. Equivalent split used numerically: the slope form zeta_x = h_x = -tan(tau) (the linearised
  kinematic body condition phi_z = U h_x) on W, plus the level zeta = h at one point of W per lateral line.
- **(S) spray root.** The upstream boundary of W, x_LE(y), is unknown. Because zeta = G[p] is continuous (the pressure
  has an integrable (x - x_LE)^-1/2 singularity there; G smooths by one order in x), (B) up to the boundary means the free
  surface ahead of W rises to meet the bottom exactly at x_LE: this is Wagner's wetting condition in linear form; it
  produces the pile-up (wave rise) and, in the slender limit, the pi/2 factor (checked in PL2).
- **(K) transom.** Smooth detachment: the free surface leaves the transom edge at the edge height with the bottom slope
  and the pressure tends to zero there (bounded, no singularity). Counting (2-D argument, Symm/airfoil-type equation of
  the first kind): for a given W the solution of (B) has the inverse-square-root singularity at both ends; requiring it
  to be absent at the transom is one condition per lateral line, balanced by the unknown spray-root position of that
  line. The 2-D model problem (exploration B7-E1: high-speed plate, hats, Kutta row) recovers C_L = pi tau with error
  0.13 % at 64 cells, first order, and the cosine mesh second order.
- **(C) chines.** W is bounded by |y| = 1/2 aft of the chine-wetting point; forward of it the chines are dry because
  each lateral line starts at its own spray root. Smooth detachment at a wetted chine cannot be imposed in this linear
  theory with g > 0. Local analysis of (13.1) near an edge: across the transom (edge normal along x, k_x large) M ~
  |k|/k_x^2 -- smoothing of order one, the Kutta-type singular solutions exist and the condition (K) can be imposed;
  across a chine (edge normal along y, k_y large at bounded k_x) M -> -C_V^2, i.e. zeta = -p/(rho g) locally: the
  operator is of order zero there, it has no singular solutions to adjust, the solution has a bounded pressure JUMP at
  the chine and the free surface outboard stands p/(rho g) above (or below) the chine. In the gravity-free limit the
  chine behaves like a wing tip (pressure -> 0 like a square root, flow around the edge). Real chines shed the flow as
  a separated sheet (spray); modelling it needs an extra, nonlinear degree of freedom per station (Savander et al.'s
  side-jet patches, Sun & Faltinsen's separated free surface in 2D+t). Hence: no extra chine condition. The production
  discretisation (13.3, scheme H-Y0) puts the pressure to zero at the chine edge, i.e. the pressure part of the
  orchestrator's "p = 0 at the wetted chines" holds at the edge, but the free surface outboard does NOT leave the chine
  at the chine height; in the continuous limit the drop to zero becomes the jump of the linear theory (edge layer of
  width dy). The size of the jump is monitored (row VCH, `chine_ratio`: line-mean pressure extrapolated to the chine
  from the two outermost lines / mean pressure; 0 would mean smooth detachment).

**Choice of the linearisation and its error.** Both the free-surface condition and the body condition are linearised
about z = 0. Neglected: (i) terms of relative order tan(tau) (0.03-0.11 at 2-6 deg); (ii) the flat-plate replacement of
the V-section in the body condition, relative order tan(beta) (0.18, 0.36, 0.58 at 10/20/30 deg) -- this is Wagner's
original approximation, known to over-predict the 2-D wedge-entry force increasingly with deadrise; (iii) the spray jet
and the nonlinear chine separation (the cross-flow drag of Shuford's semi-empirical planing lift is of this kind);
(iv) the free surface between the static waterline and the spray root is not above the hull in this model (all on
z = 0). A body-exact or fully nonlinear steady planing solver (unknown wetted surface, jet truncation, separated chine
sheets) is a research project beyond the time box; the linear theory is the established, complete member of the family
that keeps gravity in the free-surface condition, so it is the right first test of "one set of equations". Its expected
error is therefore NOT small at 20-30 deg deadrise; the gate measures it.

**Forces.** Lift (vertical, linear): L = int_W p dA; moment about the transom M_T = int_W p (-x) dA; centre of pressure
l_p = M_T / L; C_L = L / (1/2 rho U^2 b^2) as Savitsky's C_L,beta. Pressure drag D_p = L tan(tau) (no leading-edge
suction on a planing surface: the spray carries it) -- not an independent check of the model, reported only. Wetted
lengths: L_K, L_C = spray-root positions at the keel and at the chine (linear extrapolation of the two innermost /
outermost lateral lines), lam = (L_K + L_C) / (2 b) as Savitsky.

**Limits used as checks.** C_V -> inf: lower half of the thin wing (PL1). Slender (C_V large, lam large): keel-chine
difference tan(beta)/(pi tan(tau)) (PL2). C_V -> 0: M -> -C_V^2, zeta = -p/(rho g) (PK3). Symmetry about the keel
plane: exact by construction (mirror basis), not a check.

### 13.3 Numerical method (M2) and the exploration runs that chose it (scratch scripts `scratchpad/plan/`; not verification rows)

**Kernel (`hydro/rankine/planing_kernel.py`).** The response of one basis element is needed only at lattice offsets
inside the wetted region, so it is tabulated once per (C_V, h, lateral nodes):
- x: a periodic box of length 8 l_mu with N = 8 l_mu / h points; the lattice samples of the inverse transform are exact
  for the periodic sum when the spectrum is folded over the images k_x + 2 pi l / h (|l| <= 8; the hat transform decays
  like l^-2); the box images are e^-8 smaller because of the Rayleigh decay e^{-x/l_mu}; l_mu = clip(0.5 lambda_0, 20 b,
  400 b), lambda_0 = 2 pi C_V^2 b. The eps -> 0 limit by first-order Richardson extrapolation, K = 2 K(2 l_mu) - K(l_mu)
  (the kernel is analytic in eps).
- y: exact continuous transform. For a lateral element g(y) (piecewise linear, mirror-symmetric) and a target y_t the
  weight W(k_y) = (1/pi) int g(y') cos(k_y (y_t - y')) dy' = (1/pi)[sum_c J_c sin(k_y d_c)/k_y - sum_c S_c cos(k_y d_c)/
  k_y^2] (corners y_c, value jumps J_c, slope jumps S_c, d_c = y_t - y_c); the x-transform is computed at k_y nodes
  (uniform step min(0.02, k0/16) to 3/b, then geometric ratio 1.005 to k_max = max(2e6, 20 C_V^2 (pi/h)^2) / b), the
  product integrated with Filon weights (R piecewise linear in k_y, the oscillating factor exact through Si and Ci; a
  6-point Gauss rule where k_y max|d_c| < 2).
- The local (hydrostatic) limit of the response, -C_V^2 times the element value (elevation rows) or its x-derivative
  (slope rows), is subtracted before the k_y integration and added back exactly.
Checks: PK1 (brute-force 2-D FFT), PK2 (Filon), PK3 (hydrostatic limit), PK4 (quadrature/box/folding/damping).

**Basis and rows (`hydro/rankine/planing_steady.py`, class `PlaningSprayRootFast`, lateral scheme `hat0` = H-Y0).**
Lateral nodes y_0 = 0 (keel) ... y_n = 1/2 (chine), uniform; lateral hats (the keel hat is its own mirror); NO element at
the chine: the pressure goes linearly to zero over the last lateral interval (pressure zero at the chine edge), the
body condition is collocated on the lines y_0 ... y_{n-1}. In the continuous limit this converges to the jump solution
of item C (the drop to zero happens in an edge layer of width dy); it is a discretisation of the same linear problem,
chosen because the element with the jump made the transom-chine corner ill-conditioned (B7-E10). Along each lateral
line, nodes x_i = -i h from the transom (i = 0, p = 0: the Kutta condition) to the spray-root node N (half-hat: the
pressure jumps there); x hats in between. Rows: the slope condition zeta_x = -tan(tau) at the midpoints of cells
0..N-1 of every line. For a given planform (the set {N}) this square system is the lifting-surface problem with
gravity; its pressure is proportional to tan(tau) and independent of d and beta. The level (spray-root) residual of
each line, r_t = zeta(-N_t h) - h(-N_t h), is linear in (d, tan tau, tan beta). Continuous spray-root positions
N_t + f_t: the linearisation r(N + f) = r(N) + J f, J_ts = r_t(N + e_s) - r_t(N), with all outputs interpolated in the
same way (first order in h); N is moved (at most 2 cells per iteration) until every f_t is in [0, 1), or, on returning
to a node set, within a slack of 0.15 cell. The neighbour planforms N + e_s come from the base factorisation (rank-one
change of the spray-root column plus a border; Sherman-Morrison and Schur complement; identical to fresh solves to
1e-14, 17 x faster). Modes: `solve(tau, d)`, `solve_lambda(tau, lam)` (d from the mean wetted length; used for the
Savitsky comparison at given tau and lam), `equilibrium(lift, l_cg)` (trim and lam from the lift and the moment of
the pressure force about the centre of gravity; scipy fsolve on (tau, lam)).

**Exploration runs (in order; each changed the method, none is a verification row).**
- B7-E1 (2-D model, log kernel): hats + half-hat at the spray root + p = 0 and collocation at the transom recover the
  exact high-speed flat-plate lift pi tau: 1.2 % (8 cells), 0.13 % (64), first order; cosine mesh second order.
- B7-E2 (kernel): x-FFT + k_y quadrature vs a brute-force 2-D FFT: 0.3-0.8 % with the k_y ratio 1.02, 0.1 % with 1.005
  (now the default). Hydrostatic limit approached as C_V^2/h -> 0 (only meaningful when the lattice resolves lambda_0).
- B7-E3 (elevation collocation at nodes, box strips): saw-tooth along x. Cause: in the gravity/slender regime the
  operator is a causal double integration along x (downstream "hollow" growing linearly), and nodal collocation is
  only marginally stable for it. Abandoned.
- B7-E4 (vortex-lattice loads at 1/4, slope at 3/4): oscillation near the transom on narrow strips, removed by a larger
  k_y range; k_max is now tied to the hydrostatic transition of the shortest lattice mode, C_V^2 (pi/h)^2.
- B7-E5 (hats in x, slope at cell midpoints, BOX strips in y): smooth along x, but the lateral load distribution is
  wavy and does not converge with the number of strips at C_V = 4 (C_L 0.105, 0.091, 0.106 for 8, 16, 24 strips at a
  fixed planform), robust to quadrature and damping, smooth at C_V = 12: each strip edge is a spurious pressure jump
  that radiates divergent waves. Hence lateral HATS (only the chine keeps its physical jump): fixed-planform C_L 0.0968,
  0.0968, 0.0963 for 16, 24, 32 intervals.
- B7-E6 (chine collocation exactly on the jump): the chine line's level residual responds with the wrong sign (local
  jump average dominates), Newton diverges; moving that collocation line inboard by a quarter interval fixes it.
- B7-E7 (spray-root iteration): start from the slender-body (Wagner) line, steps limited; per-line exact level
  conditions converge in 2-4 node sets at deadrise 10-30 deg. For the FLAT plate (beta = 0) the spray-root Jacobian has
  eigenvalues crossing zero (-8e-5, +8e-4, +2e-3 at C_V 4, lam 2): the discrete spray-root problem is singular there and
  does not converge (a smooth least-squares spray-root line, `PlaningSmoothLE`, does not cure it). Flat plates are
  therefore reported as information only; the registered validation uses deadrise 10-30 deg.
- B7-E8 (fast neighbour planforms): rank-one + border update equals fresh solves to 1.1e-14.
- B7-E10 (lateral convergence with the chine element): at REF the grid series (1/16, 8), (1/32, 16), (1/32, 24),
  (1/64, 32) gave C_L 0.0928, 0.1056, 0.1119, 0.0985 (non-monotone; the chine-line pressure near the transom
  oscillated, at C_V 2 up to +-117 at the finest grid: the corner of the transom Kutta condition and the chine jump is
  ill-conditioned in that basis). Tying the chine line's spray root to its neighbours fixed L_K - L_C and d (1.60-1.61,
  0.2626) but not the lift. Hence scheme H-Y0 (no chine element).
- B7-E11 (scheme H-Y0): REF series (1/16, 8), (1/32, 16), (1/64, 32): C_L 0.09145, 0.09682, 0.09870 (monotone,
  observed order about 1.5), l_p/(lam b) 0.6615, 0.6495, 0.6507, L_K - L_C 1.616, 1.614, 1.614; at C_V 2 (beta 20, tau 4,
  lam 3): 0.1849, 0.1886, 0.1905. Convergence of the spray-root iteration in 2-5 node sets at deadrise 10-30 deg; flat
  plates still singular.
- B7-E9 (values seen before registration, for honesty): at C_V 4 the ratio C_L / C_L,Savitsky was about 1.0-1.06 at
  beta 10 (1.37 at tau 2, lam 3.5), 1.12-1.35 at beta 20, 1.37-1.76 at beta 30; l_p/(lam b) within about 0.01-0.09;
  L_K - L_C within about 5 % of the Wagner value; with the earlier chine element one C_V 2 condition gave a negative
  lift (B7-E10); with H-Y0 (B7-E11 sweep): beta 10 at C_V 4: 0.95-1.10; beta 30 at C_V 4: 1.32-1.71; beta 20 at C_V 6:
  1.01-1.16; beta 30 at C_V 2: 1.45-2.29. These values were
  known when the tolerances below were written; the tolerances were NOT chosen from them (they follow the data scatter
  of Savitsky's equations and the acceptance file's V1 row).
