**Lift ratio C_L / C_L,Savitsky (69 conditions).** Median [min, max] per deadrise: 10 deg 1.05 [0.81, 1.27]; 20 deg
1.23 [0.92, 1.61]; 30 deg 1.40 [1.07, 2.29]. Per speed (all deadrise): C_V 2 median 1.27, C_V 4 1.16, C_V 6 1.06. By
deadrise and speed (median): 10 deg 1.19 / 1.02 / 0.94; 20 deg 1.30 / 1.17 / 1.10; 30 deg 1.44 / 1.38 / 1.28 (C_V 2 /
4 / 6). Three of 69 solves did not converge (beta 30, tau 2, lam 3.5: the longest keel-chine difference); they count as
failures.

**Deadrise diagnostic (post-processing of the same runs).** The linear lift divided by Savitsky's FLAT-PLATE lift C_L0
at the same (tau, lam, C_V) is nearly independent of deadrise: median 1.03 / 0.97 / 0.91 at C_V 2, 0.85 / 0.80 / 0.76
at C_V 4, 0.74 / 0.70 / 0.67 at C_V 6 (10 / 20 / 30 deg); Savitsky's deadrise term lowers C_L by about 15 % at 10 deg
and about 45 % at 30 deg (medians of C_L0/C_L,beta over the same conditions). Two separate deficits are visible: (i) the deadrise reduction of lift is almost absent (about 10 % from 10 to 30
deg in the linear theory); (ii) the speed dependence is too strong -- relative to the flat-plate data the linear lift is
about right at C_V 2 and 25-30 % low at C_V 6, i.e. too much gravity (buoyancy-like) lift at low speed and too little
dynamic lift at high speed (the nonlinear cross-flow lift of low-aspect-ratio planing surfaces, Shuford's cross-flow
term, is absent). At 10-20 deg and C_V 4-6 the two partly cancel, which is where the passing conditions lie.

**Equilibrium (beta 20, pressure moment only).** The linear theory trims lower than Savitsky by 0.3-1.1 deg (C_V 3-6) and
1.2-1.7 deg (C_V 2): tau 3.59 vs 4.79, 6.76 vs 8.43 (C_V 2); 3.13 vs 3.81, 1.88 vs 2.62, 5.64 vs 6.55, 3.40 vs 4.49
(C_V 3); 2.32 vs 2.82, 1.75 vs 2.18, 4.36 vs 4.72, 3.07 vs 3.66 (C_V 4); 2.33 vs 2.81, 2.05 vs 2.34 (C_V 6); the mean
wetted lengths agree within 10 %. The excess lift (item above) is absorbed by a lower trim.

**Chine.** The line-mean pressure extrapolated to the chine is 0.61 of the mean pressure (median; -0.19 ... 0.90): the
linear solution wants a large pressure at the wetted chines; the production basis forces it to zero over the last
lateral interval (item C).

### 13.5 Verdict

**Registered verdict: INCONCLUSIVE** (rule of 13.1): the numerics are not "adequate" in the registered sense because PK1
(0.62 % vs 0.5 %) and PL1 (5.2 % vs 3 %) failed, although the validation fails robustly (VLR.all 27.5 %, VLR.beta20 25 %
against 80 %, with the numerical uncertainty U_num = 1.95 % added to the tolerance).

**Assessment (not the registered verdict): NO-GO for the linear route, high confidence.** The two failed verification
rows cannot explain the validation result: PK1's 0.62 % is the residual of an under-resolved reference that converges
toward the tables, and the converged kernel error enters the lift at well below the 2 % grid uncertainty (PK4, PC1);
PL1 concerns the gravity-free limit (C_V >= 20), outside the validated speed range; its registered form used a damping
outside the method's valid regime (amendment 2), but the post-tier exploration below shows that the properly damped
lift also keeps falling with C_V and the gravity-free limit of the solver is NOT verified (item 3 of 13.6). Against these, the lift misses Savitsky by a median +23 % at the user's 20 deg
deadrise (+30 % at C_V 2, +17 % at C_V 4), +40 % at 30 deg, with a systematic deadrise trend that the theory cannot
produce (its lift is close to Savitsky's flat-plate lift at every deadrise). **Post-tier robustness analysis** (not a
registered row): even if the linear lift is rescaled per speed so that the 10 deg conditions match Savitsky exactly
(median factor 0.84 / 0.98 / 1.07 at C_V 2 / 4 / 6 -- i.e. granting any error of the gravity part or of the dynamic
part that is common to all deadrise angles), only 9 of 24 conditions at 20 deg (rescaled median ratio 1.10 / 1.14 /
1.18) and 1 of 18 at 30 deg (1.22 / 1.35 / 1.37) fall within 10 %. The cause is in the equations' linearisation
(flat-plate body condition, no chine separation), not in the numerics; refining the grid moves the lift by 2 %. Wetted
lengths, the Wagner keel-chine relation and the centre of pressure are reproduced -- the free-surface physics of the
linear theory is right, the near-hull pressure of a V-bottom is not.

### 13.6 Findings, limitations and honest caveats

1. **What the linear theory with gravity gets right.** With the wetted area from the solution, the keel-chine
   wetted-length difference follows Savitsky's (Wagner-type) relation (VW), the slender limit is reproduced (PL2), the
   centre of pressure follows Savitsky's equation (VCP), and at 10 deg deadrise the lift is within the data scatter.
   The pressure drop toward the transom that 2D+t needs an empirical correction for (Sun & Faltinsen's 0.5 b) is part of
   the solution here (Kutta condition with gravity).
2. **What it gets wrong, and why (fundamental, not numerical).** The lift does not fall with deadrise as the data do:
   the ratio C_L/C_L,Savitsky grows from about 1 at 10 deg to well above the tolerance at 20 and 30 deg, and the linear
   lift is close to Savitsky's FLAT-PLATE lift C_L0 at the same wetted length (13.4 deadrise diagnostic). In this theory
   deadrise enters only through the wetted planform (spray-root line) and the hydrostatic level; the body condition is
   that of a flat plate (Wagner's approximation), so the V-section's lower pressure is absent. This is the known
   weakness of Wagner's flat-plate approximation for wedge entry, which over-predicts the force increasingly with
   deadrise (Zhao & Faltinsen 1993; Faltinsen 2005, ch. 8); Savitsky's -0.0065 beta C_L0^0.6 term is the empirical
   form of the same physics. The second missing piece is chine-flow separation (item C): with g > 0 the linear theory
   cannot impose smooth detachment at a chine; the production basis puts the pressure to zero at the chine, the free
   surface outboard does not leave at the chine height. Neither defect is removable by refinement: the numerical
   uncertainty of the lift (PC1, PK4) is about 2 %, the misses at 20-30 deg are tens of per cent. A second, smaller
   deficit is the speed dependence: relative to Savitsky's flat-plate lift the linear lift falls from about 1.0 at C_V 2
   to about 0.7 at C_V 6 at every deadrise -- too much gravity (buoyancy-like) lift at low speed and too little dynamic
   lift at high speed, where real low-aspect-ratio planing surfaces gain the nonlinear cross-flow lift (Shuford 1958)
   that a linear theory does not contain.
3. **Numerical status.** Kernel: brute-force agreement 0.62 % (PK1, binding 0.5 %: FAIL), at the size of the
   reference's own residual (it converges toward the tables with more lateral images, amendment 2); Filon weights
   7e-8; hydrostatic limit 0.3 %; quadrature/box/folding/damping sensitivity <= 0.06 %; grid series monotone with
   observed order 1.5, finest change 1.95 %.
   The high-speed check PL1 failed as registered (5.2 %): its damping (l_mu = 50 b) is outside the regime in which the
   damping extrapolation is valid at C_V >= 20 (amendment 2). **Correction after the full tier (post-tier exploration,
   scratch `t_pl1d.py`, coarse grid h 1/16, 8 lateral intervals, damping by the production rule):** the planform lift
   (lam 2, tau 4 deg) is 0.0724, 0.0609, 0.0561, 0.0524 at C_V 4, 7, 10, 14 -- still falling at C_V 14 and already
   below the thin-wing limit 0.0541 (vortex lattice extrapolated in resolution), not of the form a + b/C_V^2. So the
   approach to the gravity-free limit is slow and its value is not established; whether the solver's gravity-free
   limit equals the half-wing lift (as it must) is OPEN (candidates: a slowly decaying gravity tail of the wake, the
   k_y resolution at very small k0 -- the coarse run used k0/4 steps --, or a defect in the dynamic part). The
   statement of amendment 2 that PL1's failure is explained by the check's damping is therefore withdrawn as
   incomplete. Consequence for the verdict: a common error of this kind changes the lift at all deadrise angles alike;
   the robustness analysis of 13.5 shows the deadrise trend fails even if any such common factor is removed.
4. **Flat plates (beta = 0)** do not converge in this discretisation (the discrete spray-root Jacobian is singular,
   B7-E7); not part of the registered validation (deadrise 10-30 deg), reported as a limitation.
5. **Raw data.** No raw towing-tank data were used: the NACA tables (Chambliss & Boyd 1953; Shuford 1958) are
   available as scans whose OCR layer is too corrupted to transcribe reliably without page images. The comparison is
   with Savitsky's equations only, which the gate question names; Savitsky's lift equation itself fits the NACA data to
   a few per cent, so a 25-50 % miss is not a data-scatter question.
6. **Equilibrium trim** is less sensitive than lift: a lift excess of 15-30 % lowers the equilibrium trim by roughly
   10-30 % of the trim -- 0.3-1.1 deg at C_V 3-6 and 1.2-1.7 deg at C_V 2 for the registered cases (VEQ 9 of 12 within
   1 deg, FAIL against 80 %); the wetted lengths agree within 10 %. This is why the registered rule requires the lift
   rows as well: at the small trims of a lightly loaded fast hull a wrong lift can hide inside a 1 deg trim tolerance.
7. **Code hygiene.** `planing_steady.py` keeps the exploration classes of 13.3 (`PrismaticPlaning` vortex-lattice type,
   `PrismaticPlaningHat` with box strips, `PlaningSprayRoot`, `PlaningSmoothLE`) and its module docstring still
   describes the vortex-lattice variant; the production path is `PlaningSprayRootFast(..., lateral="hat0")` as
   documented in 13.3. Left unchanged after the full tier so that the file stays the registered one.

### 13.7 What it would take, and the fallback routes

- **Within "one set of equations" (potential flow with gravity):** the deadrise physics needs the body condition on
  the actual V-bottom, not on z = 0, and a model of the chine-separated sheet. Two derivable (non-empirical) routes:
  (a) a generalised-Wagner / modified-Logvinovich correction of the body condition on each cross-section, coupled to the
  3-D linear free-surface response used here (keeps gravity and 3-D transom effects; the wedge part is analytic; 1.5-3
  days with verification against the Zhao-Faltinsen similarity solutions; outcome uncertain at the chine-wetted part);
  (b) a body-exact steady potential solver with an unknown wetted surface, jet truncation and separated chine sheets
  (the class of Savander et al. 2002 with gravity; research level, 1-2 weeks, high risk).
- **Fallback (b) of the user's options:** keep the present high-speed plant (2D+t strip theory with its registered,
  speed-indexed buoyancy factor) for the planing range, with this gate's result as the documented reason; or a
  nonlinear 2D+t with gravity and chine separation (Sun & Faltinsen 2007), which needs the empirical transom correction
  that the linear 3-D theory makes unnecessary.
- **Confidence.** High that the linear theory with flat-plate body conditions cannot meet a +-10 % lift tolerance at
  20-30 deg deadrise (consistent across C_V 2-6, trims 2-6 deg, wetted lengths 1.5-3.5, and with the established
  wedge-entry results); moderate on the exact size of the miss at C_V 2 (largest gravity effects, coarsest relative
  resolution of the waves).

## 14. Stage B8 (2026-10-07/08): route 1 of the planing gate -- 3-D linear free surface with gravity, nonlinear wedge-entry cross-sections (modified Logvinovich model), flow separation at wetted chines

Question (orchestrator, for the user's decision "route 1"): keep the 3-D linear free surface with gravity of B7, replace
the flat-plate (Wagner) cross-section by a nonlinear wedge-entry solution, treat chine-wetted sections with flow
separation at the chine; GATE at deadrise 20 deg: lift within +-10 % of Savitsky on a registered fraction of conditions
over C_V 2-6 (10 and 30 deg reported), equilibrium trim within +-1 deg. Time box 1.5-3 days of agent wall clock, earlier
if decisive. Start 2026-10-07 20:27 UTC. Code: `hydro/rankine/planing_wedge2d.py` (2-D wedge entry: MLM, the rejected
zero-pressure separation model, Wagner reference), `hydro/rankine/planing_nl.py` (composite with the B7 solver, the
chine-wetted section force, equilibrium), gate script `studies/rankine_planing_b8.py` (tiers quick / full,
`--register`); the B7 files are unchanged. Outputs `studies/_cache/rankine/planing_b8_{quick,full}.json/.log`;
registration entries `b8:*` in `planing_registration_log.json`. No WaveBEM code read; nothing installed; no git commit.
Sources read for this stage (WebFetch; nothing saved in the repository): Iafrati & Battistin, arXiv 1212.6699 (table 1:
the Zhao & Faltinsen 1993 similarity values); "On wedge-slamming pressures" (arXiv 2011.10378v3, table 1: OLM, MLM, GWM,
exact-integral and Zhao-Faltinsen peak pressures, the MLM pressure formula); Wen, Liu, Del Buono, Qu & Iafrati 2022
(arXiv 2201.00959, J. Fluids Struct.: CFD/BEM of the transition (chine-wetted) stage, eqs 16-24, appendix 7.1);
Zhao, Faltinsen & Aarsnes 1996 (21st Symp. Naval Hydrodyn., separation by a Kutta condition at the knuckle, results
only as figures). Transcribed numbers: `data/external/wedge_entry/` (SOURCES.md entry).

### 14.1 The PL1 failure of B7 (investigated first; exploration runs B8-E1, B8-E2, scratch `scratchpad/b8/`)

**Cause found: collocation on the kinks of the lateral hat basis.** The production lateral scheme of B7 (hat0) represents
the pressure by piecewise-linear hats in y and collocates the body condition on the lateral NODES, i.e. exactly on the
slope discontinuities (kinks) of the basis. The vertical velocity of a pressure field with a kink in y is
log-singular at the kink in the gravity-free limit (the Dirichlet-to-Neumann symbol |k| applied to a function whose
second derivative has a delta: phi_z ~ log abs(y - y_j)). With gravity the kernel turns hydrostatic (M -> -C_V^2) only
beyond k_y ~ C_V^2 k_x^2, so the log is cut off at a C_V-dependent scale: the discrete self-influence grows like
ln C_V and the DISCRETE problem has no gravity-free limit.
- B8-E1 (slope response of one interior hat at its own node vs at points off the node; h = 1/16, 8 intervals): on the
  node -3.109, -3.876, -4.650, -5.422 at C_V 2, 4, 8, 16 (a constant -0.77 per doubling of C_V = 1.11 ln C_V); a
  quarter interval off the node -0.979, -0.973, -0.971, -0.971 (converged).
- B8-E2 (rectangular flat planform lam 2, tau 4 deg, planform mode; node collocation = production vs collocation at the
  interval midpoints, off the kinks -- a square system for a rectangular planform): see the table in 14.4 (row PL1M,
  PL1B). At C_V = inf (gravity-free kernel, no hydrostatic split): node collocation 0.0260 (h 1/16, 8) and 0.0353
  (1/32, 16) -- meaningless, the self term diverges with k_max; midpoint collocation 0.06097 and 0.05788, approaching the
  half-wing vortex lattice (0.05483 at 36 x 80, about 0.0541 extrapolated). At C_V 12 (h 1/16, 8): node 0.05404, midpoint
  0.06087 (h 1/32, 16: 0.05710, 0.05811) (= the C_V = inf midpoint value at that grid within 0.2 %: smooth approach to the gravity-free limit).
- B8-E5 (same rectangular planform, C_V 2, 4, 6, lateral refinement at h = 1/32; node / midpoint collocation):
  C_V 4: 16 intervals 0.07461 / 0.08128, 24: 0.07655 / 0.07920, 32: 0.07784 / 0.07780, 48: 0.07820 / 0.07988, 64:
  0.07807 / 0.07913; C_V 2: 16: 0.12081 / 0.12504, 32: 0.12357 / 0.12530, 48: 0.12471 / 0.12489, 64: 0.12491 / 0.12615;
  C_V 6: 16: 0.06440 / 0.06476, 32: 0.06492 / -, 48: 0.06576 / 0.06777, 64: 0.06646 / 0.06654 (h = 1/64 changes the
  32-interval values by 0.1-0.2 %: the lateral resolution dominates). Lateral convergence is erratic (the pressure jump
  of the linear theory at a wetted chine, B7 item C, is forced to zero over the last interval, and the kink term
  above), but both collocations close in on each other: B7's production (16 intervals, node) is LOW by about 3 %, 6 %,
  3.5 % at C_V 2, 4, 6 against the mean of the two 64-interval values; 32 intervals by 1.6 %, 1.0 %, 2.4 %.
**Consequences.** (i) PL1's failure in B7 was a discretisation defect of the lateral collocation, not of the kernel, the
x discretisation or the Kutta condition: with off-kink collocation the same operator approaches the thin-wing limit
(binding check PL1M in 14.3). (ii) At the planing speeds C_V 2-6 the same defect makes B7's production lift a few per
cent LOW (B7's 1.95 % grid change and order 1.5 were this effect); it does not touch B7's conclusion (its lift was too
HIGH by 10-60 %). (iii) For B8 the lateral resolution of the 3-D linear part is doubled (production h = 1/32, 32
intervals; registered before the tiers), and the remaining bias on the rectangular planform (PL1B) is part of the
numerical uncertainty U_num. (iv) Fix for later stages: off-kink collocation also for planforms with a spray-root line
(ragged-boundary bookkeeping), or a C1 lateral basis (quadratic B-splines), plus an explicit treatment of the chine jump.

### 14.2 Formulation (M1; written before the tiers)

**What is replaced, and the structure.** For a slender planing surface (beam b small against the wetted length and
against the gravity wavelength lambda_0 = 2 pi C_V^2 b) the flow has two regions. OUTER: the 3-D field on the length
scales of the hull and of the gravity waves, linear in the free-surface condition (Kelvin, radiation condition) -- B7's
free-surface operator. INNER: each cross-plane near the hull, scale b, "time" t = (x + L_K)/U; the section enters the
water like a 2-D wedge with velocity V = dh/dt. B7's 3-D linear theory contains the LINEAR inner problem: its body
condition on z = 0 is, section by section, Wagner's flat plate (half-width c(x) from the spray-root line, pressure
-rho U phi_x, flow around the chine once the chine is wetted). Route 1 replaces that linear inner problem by a
NONLINEAR one. The consistent combination of an outer and an inner solution is the composite expansion
outer + inner - (common part), the common part being the inner limit of the outer solution = the linear inner problem
driven by the SAME kinematics:
    L = L_3D,lin + int_{-L_K}^{0} [F_NL(x) - F_W(x)] dx,   M_T = M_3D,lin + int (-x) [F_NL(x) - F_W(x)] dx       (14.1)
(L lift, M_T moment about the transom, F per unit length). Nothing is counted twice: F_W is exactly the part of the
linear solution that the nonlinear section re-computes (slender limit of B7's own sections), everything else in
L_3D,lin -- gravity (wave and hydrostatic pressure), the 3-D pressure relief at the transom (Kutta), the 3-D parts of
the spray-root line -- stays as computed by B7, and the nonlinear section adds only what differs from its own linear
version. In the gravity-free slender limit L_3D,lin -> int F_W dx and (14.1) becomes the pure nonlinear 2D+t lift; for
beta -> 0 in the chine-dry stage F_NL -> F_W (MLM -> Wagner) and (14.1) -> B7.

**Matching (how the 3-D gravity wave field drives the sections).** The inner problem sees the outer flow as an effective
entry: penetration h(x) of the keel below the LOCAL outer free-surface level and V = U dh/dx. The 3-D linear solution
gives them through its own wetted planform: Wagner's condition for a wedge (any velocity history) is c = pi h/(2 tan
beta), so the inner keel-to-chine-wetting distance L_K - L_C fixes the mean entry velocity
    V/U = (2/pi) l tan(beta) / (L_K - L_C),    h(x) = (V/U)(x + L_K),   l = b/2,                               (14.2)
with L_K, L_C the keel and chine spray-root positions of the B7 solution (they contain the gravity wave rise or
hollow and the 3-D effects of the outer field). For the slender Wagner planform (L_K - L_C = b tan beta/(pi tan tau))
(14.2) gives V = U tan(tau) and h = keel depth below z = 0. Approximation (registered sensitivity row "v_slender"):
V is constant along the hull, i.e. the outer slope dh_o/dx is taken equal to its mean over the chine-dry part also in
the chine-wetted part (the outer field varies on lambda_0 >= 25 b, the hull is 1.5-4.5 b long; at C_V 2 the phase
change over the hull is up to about 1 rad, so this is the weakest link at C_V 2). Gravity is not in the 2-D sections:
the inner problem is the gravity-free nonlinear wedge entry; the hydrostatic and wave pressures are in L_3D,lin.

**Chine-dry sections: modified Logvinovich model (MLM; Korobkin 2004), derived.** Wagner's potential on the flat
projection, phi = -V sqrt(c^2 - y^2) on abs(y) < c, transferred to the body z = f(y) - h(t), f = abs(y) tan(beta), by a
first-order Taylor shift: phi_b = phi + (f - h) phi_z = -V [R + f - h], R = sqrt(c^2 - y^2). On the body the
tangential velocity is d phi_b/ds, the normal velocity is the exact body normal velocity V/sqrt(1 + f'^2); with the
fixed-frame time derivative phi_t = d phi_b/dt + V phi_z, Bernoulli p = -rho (phi_t + |grad phi|^2/2) gives, term by
term (D = d phi_b/dy = V (Y - T), Y = y/R, T = tan beta):
    p/rho = Vdot (R + f - h) + V c cdot/R - V^2 - V (D T - V)/(1 + T^2) - (D^2 + V^2)/(2 (1 + T^2))
          = Vdot (R + f - h) + V c cdot/R - (V^2/2) [sin^2(beta) + cos^2(beta) c^2/R^2].                        (14.3)
This is Korobkin's MLM pressure (the published form, arXiv 2011.10378 eq. for constant V, is identical; code check
W2A reproduces the published MLM peak pressures). c from Wagner's condition c = pi h/(2 T). Force per unit length:
F = 2 int_0^a p dy, a = first zero of p (the jet root; MLM pressure is negative between a and c); closed form with
y = c sin(theta): 2 [V c cdot th - (V^2/2) sin^2 b c sin th - (V^2/2) cos^2 b c ln(sec th + tan th) + Vdot (c^2 (th/2 +
sin 2th/4) + c^2 T sin^2 th/2 - h c sin th)] (check W2B). For constant V the force is self-similar,
F = rho V^2 h kappa_MLM(beta); kappa_MLM/kappa_Wagner = 0.839, 0.733, 0.649 at 10, 20, 30 deg (kappa_Wagner =
pi^3/(4 tan^2 beta)); against the similarity solution of the fully nonlinear problem (Dobrovol'skaya; Zhao & Faltinsen
1993) via Wen et al.'s fit of its force (k2 cot b, fit error <= 1.9 %): -1.7 %, +2.1 %, +9.1 % (rows W2C). The MLM PEAK
pressure is too high (+5 %, +18 %, +39 %, rows W2P; a known property: the jet-root peak is narrow, the force is not
affected in proportion). In (14.1), with constant V: F_NL - F_W = rho V^2 h (kappa_MLM - kappa_W) in the chine-dry part.

**Chine-wetted sections: flow separation at the chine.**
- *Physical derivation tried first and rejected (exploration B8-E4, rows W2R).* Logvinovich's zero-pressure condition in
  MLM form (Tassin, Korobkin & Cooker 2014): after chine wetting the potential keeps Wagner's form with a VIRTUAL
  half-width c > l fixed by p(l) = 0 in (14.3), q = c^2 - l^2: dq/dt = V [sin^2 b sqrt(q) + cos^2 b (q + l^2)/sqrt(q)] -
  2 (Vdot/V) sqrt(q)(sqrt(q) + l T - h); force = (14.3) integrated over the real bottom. Derived and implemented
  (`planing_wedge2d.wedge_entry`). Against the CFD/BEM of the same nonlinear problem (Wen et al. 2022, constant V) its
  transition-stage force collapses within about 0.1 h0 after chine wetting and keeps falling BELOW the steady
  supercavitating-flow force (Cs 0.19 at 20 deg, h/h0 = 2.5, vs the exact steady value 0.80): its impulse over h/h0 in
  [2/pi, 2] is 0.30, 0.25, 0.21 of the CFD-based value at 10, 20, 30 deg. Reason: the Wagner-type potential has no
  mechanism for the steady separated (cavity) flow, whose pressure is a stagnation pressure of order rho V^2/2; the
  model's force -> 0 as cdot -> 0. Rejected as physically wrong for this stage (it would lower the lift by an error).
- *Model used (registered empirical element E-B8-1).* The similitude of Wen et al. (2022): the chine-wetted force of a
  wedge entering at constant speed decays from its value at chine wetting to the force of the steady supercavitating
  (free-streamline) flow past the wedge, Cs_inf (Gurevich; exact, appendix 7.1 of the paper, computed here by
  quadrature; beta = 0 gives Kirchhoff's 2 pi/(pi + 4), check W2S), with one universal decay function of
    h* = ((h - h_cw)/h_cw) cot(beta)^1.3075,   C* = (1.539 h* + 2.618)/(h*^2 + 8.081 h* + 2.169) (C* = 1 for h* <= 0.067),
    F_NL = rho V^2 l [Cs_inf + C*(h*) (Cs_cw - Cs_inf)],   h_cw = (2/pi) l tan(beta).                            (14.4)
  It is a fit to numerical solutions (FVM-VOF, checked against the BEM-FEM of Iafrati & Battistin) of the gravity-free
  nonlinear problem, deadrise 5-70 deg (constants from 15-45 deg), not to towing-tank data and not to Savitsky; nothing
  is tuned here. Anchor: Cs_cw = the MLM force at chine wetting (continuity of the section force; Wen et al. use the
  similarity value k1 k2, 2 % different at 20 deg, 9 % at 30 deg -- sensitivity row "anchor_wen"). The acceleration
  term of Wen et al. (added-mass coefficient in the transition stage) is not used: V is constant in (14.2).
- *Common part.* The linear section after chine wetting is Wagner's plate of half-width l with flow around the chine:
  F_W = d/dt (rho pi l^2 V/2) = 0 at constant V. So in the chine-wetted part (14.1) ADDS the separated-flow force; B7's
  3-D linear solution keeps its own chine-wetted lift (3-D and gravity effects) unchanged.

**Wetted length, spray root, chine wetting.** Unchanged from B7: the spray-root line from the linearised Wagner
condition with the 3-D gravity response (MLM uses Wagner's c by construction, so this is consistent); the chine-wetting
station is where the spray-root line reaches the chine (L_C); the inner chine-wetting station of (14.2) is the same
point by construction. lam = (L_K + L_C)/2 as in B7 and Savitsky.

**Forces and outputs.** C_L = 2 L/(rho U^2 b^2) as Savitsky's C_L,beta; centre of pressure l_p = M_T/L; equilibrium
(pressure force only, as B7): (tau, lam) from L = Delta and l_p = l_cg with the composite forces (fsolve on top of
B7's `solve_lambda`). Drag is not part of this gate.

**Errors of the formulation (stated before the runs).** (i) V constant along the hull (14.2); (ii) the nonlinear
increment is not relieved by the 3-D transom (Kutta) effect -- in (14.1) only the linear part feels the transom; a
transom relief of the nonlinear section force would need the nonlinear 3-D problem (or an empirical transom correction,
e.g. Sun & Faltinsen's 0.5 b, not used); (iii) MLM's force error +9 % at 30 deg; (iv) E-B8-1 is a fit (its own scatter
against its CFD is a few per cent in the figures of the paper); (v) gravity is absent from the 2-D sections (cross-flow
Froude number U tan(tau)/sqrt(g b/2) = 0.2-0.7 at the registered conditions: gravity is not negligible in the inner
problem -- the jets and the separated free surface would rise less -- only its linear part is in L_3D,lin).

**What was known before the registration (honesty, V1).** Development smoke run with the B8 production grid (REF and
C_V 4, beta 20, tau 4, lam 2.5): composite C_L 0.1222 and 0.1007 (B7 linear part 0.0985, 0.0829; Savitsky 0.0837,
0.0696: ratios 1.46, 1.45; V/U = 1.02 tan(tau)). B8-E3 (scratch `composite_est.py`; values seen before 14.3
was written): (14.1) evaluated with B7's full-tier lifts and SLENDER kinematics (V = U tan tau, L_K = lam + dKC/2):
the nonlinear 2-D section lift exceeds the Wagner section lift for every wetted-chine condition (2-D NL / Wagner 1.0-1.9;
the MLM reduction of the chine-dry part is outweighed by the separated-flow force), so the composite lift is ABOVE B7's:
median C_L/C_L,Savitsky at 20 deg 1.40 (B7 1.23), 0 of 24 conditions within 10 %; 10 deg 1.33, 30 deg 1.46. A variant
without the separated force (MLM only) would lower B7's lift by about 0.27 C_L,Wagner at 20 deg. These numbers were known
when 14.3 was written; the formulation above was fixed on the grounds given (matched-asymptotic composite, MLM verified
in 2-D, separated force from the nonlinear-flow CFD because the derivable zero-pressure model fails its 2-D check), not
chosen to move the result; the variants are reported as information rows only.

### 14.3 Registration of the checks and of the GO/NO-GO rule (written before the tiers; V1)

Production: B7's scheme for the 3-D linear part (h = b/32, hat0, Richardson in the damping) with 32 lateral intervals
(doubled after B8-E5, 14.1), (14.1)-(14.4) for the sections (4001 quadrature nodes per stage, the chine-wetting station duplicated
so that the jump of F_W is integrated exactly). REF = C_V 4, beta 20, tau 4 deg, lam 3 (as B7). Tolerances are the
acceptance file's (V rows: Savitsky lift +-10 %, trim +-1 deg; Zhao & Faltinsen pressure and force +-10 %) or B7's;
none was chosen from a value seen. Conditions: B7's 69 (beta 10/20/30 x C_V 2/4/6 x tau 2/4/6 x lam 1.5/2.5/3.5, chines
wetted per Savitsky) and B7's 16 equilibrium cases (beta 20; used where Savitsky's solution is inside tau 2-15 deg,
lam <= 4).

**GO/NO-GO rule (binding).** Numerics adequate = W2A, W2B, W2S, C1, PL1M, PC1, PC1u (<= 5 %) and PL2 pass (the kernel
rows PK1-PK4 of B7 apply unchanged to the unchanged 3-D code; PK1's 0.62 % vs 0.5 % is the residual of B7's reference,
13.1c). W2C/W2P are model-accuracy rows of the 2-D section, reported, not part of "adequate".
- **GO** if adequate AND VL.beta20 >= 80 % AND VEQ >= 80 %.
- **NO-GO** if adequate AND (VLR.beta20 < 80 % OR VEQ < 80 %).
- **INCONCLUSIVE** otherwise.
Tier jobs: quick W2 (all), PL1M (two levels; PL1B at C_V 4), PC1 (two levels), PL2 (lam 3), V (5 conditions), VEQ (1 case);
full W2, PL1M (three levels; PL1B at C_V 2, 4, 6), PC1 (three levels), PL2 (lam 3, 4), V (69), VEQ (16).

### 14.3a Record of the runs (no amendment to 14.2/14.3)

Registration entry **2026-10-07T21:15:10+00:00** (`b8:### 14.2` 752ae52dc526, `b8:### 14.3` e7f495915b7c,
`b8:constants` f38729f7f146; code `planing_kernel.py` 8b4479acbd72 and `planing_steady.py` f5b5415f36e1 = the B7
registered files, `vlm_ref.py` 4f5496d289d6, `planing_wedge2d.py` 21f4b8bd8e0a, `planing_nl.py` d8f6afa060b1,
`rankine_planing_b8.py` 1050de672b96). Quick tier 21:15:20 (528 s, `planing_b8_quick.*`). Full tier: a first start at
21:24:50 from a Bash background job was stopped at 22:00 (after the verification rows and 8 validation conditions,
log kept as `planing_b8_full_aborted1.log`) because the slow non-converging beta-10 conditions would have run past
the 2-hour limit of such jobs; it was restarted unchanged as a detached process at 22:00:59 (`planing_b8_full.*`).
The rows of the first start are identical in every digit to the second. Nothing was changed between the registration
and the runs (the log header records the registered code and sections).
**The second run was stopped at 00:24 UTC (2026-10-08) inside the last job (VEQ),** before it wrote its JSON: the
equilibrium cases took 31 s, 2511 s (a case outside Savitsky's range, not used) and > 40 min for the third, because
fsolve visits (tau, lam) where B7's spray-root iteration does not converge and then runs 200 node sets per call; the 16
cases would have needed many hours. All rows before VEQ are complete in `planing_b8_full.log` (wall time to the end of
V: 3582 s; with VEQ running the 1-h limit was exceeded: **T FAIL**). By the rule of 14.3 the verdict is determined without VEQ
(numerics adequate and VLR.beta20 failed => NO-GO whatever VEQ gives). VEQ was then evaluated **post-tier**
(`scratchpad/b8/posttier.py`, outputs copied to `studies/_cache/rankine/planing_b8_posttier.json/.log`; NOT a
registered run) with the same composite, cases and tolerance, the spray-root iteration capped at 12 node sets and fsolve
at 30 evaluations; the same script recomputed the beta-20 conditions to give the variant fractions (identical C_L to
the full-tier log).

section lift = median 1.26, 1.27, 1.24 at 10, 20, 30 deg; range 0.88-1.82): the MLM reduction of the chine-dry force
(factor 0.84 / 0.73 / 0.65) is outweighed in every condition with a chine-wetted length of more than about 0.5 b by the
separated-flow force of the chine-wetted sections. Variants (information, median at 20 deg): separated force removed
(mlm_only) 0.98 [0.77, 1.47]; chine-dry correction removed (sep_only) 1.65; anchor at the similarity force 1.43;
slender inner velocity 1.43. Fractions within 10 % at 20 deg (post-tier): composite 0 of 24, B7 linear part 2,
mlm_only 9, sep_only 0, anchor_wen 0, v_slender 0. The doubled lateral resolution raised B7's linear lift by about 2-3 % (B7 production at
REF 0.0968, here 0.0985), as PL1B predicted. Convergence: at 32 lateral intervals the spray-root iteration of B7 no
longer converges at 10 deg deadrise for tau >= 4 deg (18 of 27 conditions; at 16 intervals all converged in B7) and at
the three longest-keel-chine conditions at 20 and 30 deg -- the small-deadrise singularity of B7-E7 (flat plate) moves
to larger deadrise as the lateral spacing shrinks; non-converged conditions count as failures (only the beta-20 ones are
binding: 3 of 24).

1.35 vs 2.62 / 4.11 vs 3.34 (not converged); 3.88 vs 6.55 / 2.85 vs 2.26; 2.58 vs 4.49 / 4.24 vs 3.34; C_V 4: 2.00 vs
2.82 / 2.64 vs 2.14; 1.42 vs 2.18 / 3.67 vs 3.01 (not converged); 3.09 vs 4.72 / 2.70 vs 2.14; 2.23 vs 3.66 / 3.95 vs
3.01; C_V 6: 2.13 vs 2.81 / 2.51 vs 2.06; 1.70 vs 2.34 / 3.55 vs 2.81. The composite trims LOWER than Savitsky in every
case (more lift per degree) and needs a LONGER wetted length (its centre of pressure lies forward). Against B7 (trim
0.3-1.7 deg low, lam within 10 %) both errors grew.

### 14.5 Verdict

**Registered verdict: NO-GO** (rule of 14.3). The numerics are adequate (W2A, W2B, W2S, C1, PL1M, PC1, PC1u, PL2 all
pass; U_num = 2.4 %), and the robust lift fraction at 20 deg is 0 of 24 against 80 %: every converged beta-20 condition
is 25-55 % above Savitsky, the closest being 1.25 against a tolerance of 1.124. The miss is 10-20 times the numerical
uncertainty and has one sign. VEQ, evaluated after the stopped run, also fails (3 of 12 within 1 deg; trims 0.6-3.4 deg low).

### 14.6 Findings, limitations, honest caveats

1. **The route-1 premise is contradicted by its own physics.** B7's diagnosis (lift too high because the flat-plate
   section lacks the deadrise reduction and the chine separation) assumed both corrections lower the lift. Done
   consistently, they do not: the nonlinear wedge entry (MLM, within 2 % of the similarity-solution force at 20 deg)
   lowers the chine-dry section force by 27 %, but separated flow at a wetted chine -- as computed by the nonlinear 2-D
   theory (CFD/BEM of Wen et al., decaying slowly towards the steady free-streamline force) -- carries a large force
   that the linear section (flow around the chine, constant added mass) does not have. For Savitsky's conditions
   (chine-wetted lengths 0.25-3.4 b) the second effect is larger: the nonlinear 2-D section lift is 1.24-1.27 times the
   Wagner section lift (median), so the composite moves AWAY from the data at every deadrise. The only variant near
   Savitsky (mlm_only, median 0.98 at 20 deg, range 0.77-1.47) drops the separated force entirely, which is physically
   wrong (it is needed for the flat plate: Savitsky's lam^0.5 growth of the dynamic lift at beta = 0) and still misses
   the tolerance in 15 of 24 conditions (9 of 24 within 10 %); it is not a candidate.
2. **What the data imply.** At 20 deg the 2-D nonlinear section lift alone (gravity-free, no 3-D relief) is already
   close to or above Savitsky's TOTAL lift at C_V 4-6 for tau >= 4 deg (e.g. 0.074 vs 0.070 and 0.063 at tau 4, lam 2.5). Real planing surfaces therefore lose much of the 2-D separated-flow force
   near the transom (the 3-D relief Sun & Faltinsen 2007 needed an empirical 0.5 b transom correction for) and/or in
   gravity (cross-flow Froude numbers 0.2-0.7). In (14.1) neither is present for the nonlinear part: the transom Kutta
   condition acts only on the linear part. Both are 3-D or gravity-nonlinear effects of the separated flow -- exactly
   what a composite of a linear 3-D field and gravity-free 2-D sections cannot contain. Adding them back would need
   either an empirical transom-relief function (Garme, Sun & Faltinsen: fitted constants, against the user's G rules)
   or a nonlinear 3-D (or 2D+t with gravity plus a derived 3-D transom model) treatment of the separated flow.
3. **Centre of pressure** moves forward by 0.07-0.14 lam b (the nonlinear forces sit near chine wetting): the trim
   balance cannot hide the lift excess either: trims 0.6-3.4 deg low, wetted lengths 15-31 % long (14.4).
4. **PL1 closed.** B7's gravity-free-limit failure was a lateral collocation defect (collocation on the hat kinks;
   self-influence ~ 1.11 ln C_V; no discrete gravity-free limit). With off-kink collocation the operator meets the
   thin-wing limit within 0.2 %. At C_V 2-6 the defect made B7's lift 3-6 % LOW (16 intervals), 1-2.4 % at the 32
   intervals used here: it cannot have caused B7's or B8's over-prediction.
5. **Limitations.** Savitsky's equations as reference (no raw tank data, as in B7); E-B8-1 is a fit to CFD of the
   gravity-free problem (registered); V constant along the hull (variant v_slender changes the median by 1 %); no
   acceleration term in the chine-wetted section; MLM's force is 9 % high at 30 deg (W2C) -- that would only lower the
   30 deg composite by a few per cent; the spray-root iteration does not converge for small deadrise at the finer
   lateral grid (18 of 27 beta-10 conditions), not binding here, but a defect to fix (smooth spray-root
   parametrisation, B7-E7) before any later use of the B7 solver at small deadrise.

### 14.7 Next step (for the user's decision)

Route 1 as specified (linear 3-D gravity field + nonlinear 2-D sections, composite) is NO-GO: the consistent
nonlinear section raises the lift. What remains:
- (a) **Route 2 (fully nonlinear steady planing, 3-D)** with separated chine sheets and the transom: the only
  potential-flow route that contains the 3-D relief of the separated force; research-level, 1-2 weeks, high risk.
- (b) **2D+t nonlinear with gravity in the section** (Sun & Faltinsen class) plus a transom relief derived from the
  3-D linear theory of B7 (the ratio of B7's 3-D sectional load to its slender limit, applied to the whole section
  force -- a multiplicative coupling that is not an asymptotic result and would need its own validation); 2-4 days;
  outcome uncertain; gravity in the section addresses the cross-flow Froude number issue.
- (c) **Fallback**: keep the present high-speed plant (2D+t strip theory with its registered speed-indexed buoyancy
  factor) for the planing range, with B7 and B8 as the documented reasons that a single linear-gravity formulation with
  nonlinear sections does not meet the +-10 % lift tolerance.
My recommendation: (c) now for the product, with (b) only if the user wants one more bounded attempt; (a) is not
justified by the evidence so far.
